# TextMouth code audit and polish TODO

Audit date: September 4, 2026. Local branch: `audit/2026-09-04-polish`.
Starting commit: `1e71d75`.

September 8 follow-up: T11's missing EPUB headings and a library teardown crash
are repaired on `fix/epub-missing-headings`. See the
[investigation and validation record](EPUB-HEADINGS-FIX-2026-09-08.md).

September 28 follow-up: user-reported audio speed, EPUB page-list heading, and
heading-vocabulary fixes plus the first Q07 repair are merged into `main`; see
[September 28 follow-up fixes](#september-28-follow-up-fixes).

## Scope and evidence

The audit covered the application, models, parsers, services, view models, SwiftUI/AppKit views, persistence, migrations, build configuration, and production test coverage: **94 production Swift files, about 30,500 lines**, plus the hosted tests and their 18 legacy scenario groups. Review emphasized data preservation, reading-content fidelity, playback state, multiple windows, and accessibility.

The existing July audit and feature notes were checked against current code. A previous “complete” checkbox was treated as historical context, not proof that the current workflow is correct. New fixes use production code and disposable fixtures. Dangerous deletion regressions exercise validation or isolated test storage.

The pre-existing edits in `DAISYParser.swift`, `AudioZIPClassificationTests.swift`, and `LegacyScenarioHarness.swift` were preserved and included in verification. They remain uncommitted and separate from this audit's commits.

Evidence labels below distinguish:

- **Verified:** exercised by a regression test or build experiment in this audit.
- **Confirmed:** a concrete defect follows from the current implementation; its stated trigger still needs a dedicated regression test before repair.
- **Risk:** a credible failure path requiring controlled reproduction, such as concurrent writes or system callback ordering.
- **Design:** the intended behavior needs to be settled before changing it.
- **Manual:** requires running the interface, assistive technology, or audio hardware.

## Ranking

Every item has a priority, impact, and difficulty. Difficulty includes regression coverage and migration consequences, rather than just the size of the edit.

| Rank | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| P0 | Address immediately: a direct destructive-path hazard. The P0 findings discovered here are fixed. |
| P1 | Next release blocker or major reliability work. |
| P2 | Important correctness, usability, or performance improvement. |
| P3 | Consistency, maintenance, or documentation improvement. |
| Impact 5 | Potential loss of user data or an application crash. |
| Impact 4 | Broken playback, missing content, or a core inaccessible workflow. |
| Impact 3 | Incorrect results or a substantially disrupted workflow. |
| Impact 2 | Localized inconsistency or avoidable friction. |
| Impact 1 | Small maintenance or presentation issue. |
| Difficulty 1 | Small local change with straightforward verification. |
| Difficulty 2 | Bounded change plus focused regression coverage. |
| Difficulty 3 | Several collaborating components or a nontrivial fixture. |
| Difficulty 4 | Persistence, lifecycle, or platform behavior spanning multiple components. |
| Difficulty 5 | Architectural coordination, migration, or extensive hardware/performance validation. |

Recommended next sequence: **S01 → S02 → S03 → S04/S05/S06 → Q01 → P01/P02 → T01 → I01**. Storage coordination is a dependency for several other repairs. Use priority and impact to order the work; difficulty describes effort, not severity. Stable item IDs identify findings rather than an execution sequence.

## Completed fixes

Checked items are implemented locally. Test names identify production regression coverage; interface-only changes additionally require the manual checks below.

| ID | Priority | Impact | Difficulty | Fix and evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F01 | P0 | 5 | 2 | Reject empty, traversing, mismatched, and symlinked book-package deletion targets. A legacy empty `.tmb` name previously resolved to the containing library folder. `DeletionSafetyTests`. |
| F02 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Reject reserved/path-like folder names and invalid parent paths consistently in the model, filesystem helper, and sheets. Preserve established slash-to-hyphen normalization. `FolderSafetyTests`. |
| F03 | P2 | 3 | 2 | Publish a new folder only after directory creation succeeds. `FolderSafetyTests.testFailedDirectoryCreationDoesNotPublishFolder`. |
| F04 | P2 | 3 | 3 | Create empty descendant directories during folder moves/renames. `FolderSafetyTests` and `LibraryMoveTransactionTests`. |
| F05 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Stop per-book runtime speech speed/pitch from overwriting global preferences. Global persistence stays in `SettingsViewModel`. `PlaybackSettingsAuditTests`. |
| F06 | P1 | 3 | 2 | Use quarter-step audio multipliers for keyboard/buttons and audio-specific settings; prevent saving multipliers as global WPM; reset the live player consistently. `PlaybackSettingsAuditTests`. |
| F07 | P1 | 4 | 3 | Compose substitution offset maps in rule order instead of combining coordinates from different intermediate texts. `SubstitutionCompositionTests`, Unicode and legacy substitution coverage. |
| F08 | P1 | 5 | 2 | Retain original ZIPs after partial, empty, or unaccepted batch results. Successful batches still honor the deletion preference. `ZIPImportTests`. |
| F09 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Route single audio/DAISY ZIPs and pasted text through duplicate resolution. Original-file handling waits for acceptance. `ZIPImportTests`. |
| F10 | P2 | 3 | 1 | Organize the actual imported book IDs into a ZIP folder, preserving separate Keep Both copies with the same hash. |
| F11 | P1 | 4 | 3 | Discover nested audiobook tracks consistently during import and reading; exclude Finder metadata, hidden files, and symlinks. Real WAV and ZIP fixtures in `AudioDirectoryImportTests`. |
| F12 | P1 | 4 | 3 | Use relative manifest filenames, deduplicate repeated order entries, reject missing/outside segment references and invalid clip ranges, and reject unreadable ordinary track durations. `AudioDirectoryImportTests`. Remaining persisted-index/media validation is T01/I04. |
| F13 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Preserve inline and block code in Markdown reading text. `ParserContentFidelityTests`. |
| F14 | P1 | 4 | 3 | Capture Markdown heading positions during extraction so earlier matching body text cannot steal the heading location. Includes Unicode coverage in `ParserContentFidelityTests`. |
| F15 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Emit DOCX hyperlinks once and keep run breaks/tabs in their source order. `ParserContentFidelityTests`. |
| F16 | P1 | 5 | 2 | Bound DOCX list levels, reject negative array indices, and handle outline level 9 as body text without overflowing on large values. `ParserContentFidelityTests`. |
| F17 | P1 | 4 | 3 | Scope reader statistics finalization and sleep-fade cleanup to the owning book. Closing/pausing an inactive reader no longer cancels another book's session/fade. `ReaderLifecycleAuditTests`. |
| F18 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Loop audiobooks through their audio backend, restart listening statistics, and clear the play/pause UI state on completion. `AudioDirectoryImportTests` and `ReaderLifecycleAuditTests`. |
| F19 | P1 | 5 | 2 | Clamp negative restored positions, ignore content-load callbacks after close, guard playback while loading/closed, and protect backend chapter access. `ReaderLifecycleAuditTests`. |
| F20 | P1 | 5 | 3 | Reject data migrations through symlinks into the source, including nonexistent child destinations. The regression exposed Foundation's unresolved-ancestor case and verifies the corrected guard. `DataDirectoryMigrationTests`. |
| F21 | P1 | 4 | 3 | Preserve parents, nesting levels, and source order in DAISY NCX navigation. `PackageNavigationAuditTests`. |
| F22 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Resolve EPUB sibling chapter paths, percent encoding, and literal dots within the ZIP namespace in both direct and cached parsing. `PackageNavigationAuditTests`. |
| F23 | P1 | 4 | 2 | Propagate build failure before packaging, select the exact Release/Debug product, retain the full build log, and share one build recipe. A disposable mock toolchain verified failures, both configurations, and paths with spaces. |
| F24 | P2 | 3 | 2 | Make search results native buttons with stable result/rotor identity and an input-independent activation hint. Compiled; VoiceOver/braille verification remains A01. |
| F25 | P1 | 5 | 2 | Prevent integer overflow in jump-time input, format invalid durations safely, and use the same restored audio-speed clamp for estimates and playback. `PlaybackPolicyProductionTests`. |
| F26 | P2 | 3 | 1 | Use UTF-16 chapter lengths for heading-panel timing/percentage offsets, matching stored heading coordinates after emoji and combining text. |
| F27 | P0 | 5 | 2 | Reject unsafe legacy folder paths and stale folder references at the recursive-deletion boundary, before deleting any contents. `FolderSafetyTests`. |

- [x] Implement F01–F27 in focused local commits.
- [x] Increment text-cache version to `3.0.3` for corrected extraction. Existing caches are rebuilt through the normal versioned workflow.
- [x] Run the complete hosted production suite with complete strict-concurrency checking.
- [x] Build and package Release through the corrected Makefile.

**Cache compatibility:** corrected extraction can change text length, heading identifiers, and chapter boundaries. Numeric positions are retained/clamped by the existing workflow; semantic bookmark relocation across re-extraction is still T06. This audit does not promise that an old bookmark will identify the same sentence after the extracted text changes.

### September 8 follow-up fixes

- [x] Complete T11 and reproduce the Helliconia mismatch against a copied source/cache. Recover **127 headings across 122 chapters**, preserving that book's reading text and all existing heading IDs. Fifteen EPUB/HTML regressions cover the shared parsing, cache migration, and reader workflow.
- [x] Fix the reported `LibraryViewModel.deinit` crash. Background statistics work can release the final reference on a utility queue; timer cancellation now returns to the main thread without retaining the dying model. Three lifecycle regressions verify worker release, immediate main-thread release, and timer replacement. This specific crash was not separately listed in the September 4 TODO; broader concurrency work remains Q02.
- [x] Complete the hosted production suite with strict-concurrency checking: **836 tests, 1 optional profiling test skipped, 0 failures**. The copied-book regression ran and passed. Test result: `Test-TextMouth-2026.09.08_23-56-04--0400.xcresult`.
- [x] Build and package Release with `make build` (completed September 9): `build/TextMouth.app`, build `20260909035900`, code commit `534e8f7`.

### September 9 cache-update performance

September 9 cache-update follow-up: implemented shared extraction/index results,
two bounded EPUB workers with large-file barriers, per-book progress checkpoints,
interrupted-run recovery, and duplicate-batch guards. **848 tests passed, 1 optional
profiling test skipped, 0 failures**. The copied four-book benchmark decreased
from 7.37 s serial to 3.89 s with two workers. Cache/index versions are unchanged;
completed caches do not need another rebuild. See the
[performance and validation record](CACHE-UPDATES-2026-09-09.md).

Release packaging passed: `build/TextMouth-CacheUpdate.app`, build
`20260909043607`, code commit `9283914`. The existing `build/TextMouth.app`
was left in place for the running app.

### September 28 follow-up fixes

Orchestrated session: each fix was implemented on its own branch in an isolated
worktree, reviewed, re-tested on `main` after a non-squash merge, and recorded
here. Agent commits carry their own model attribution; merge commits record the
integration.

- [x] **Audio speed granularity (user report).** Audio books stepped only by 0.25x and
  the value was once rendered with one decimal ("1.2" for 1.25). One `AudioSpeed`
  helper now owns the 0.25–4.0 range, a 0.1x step for the playback bar, menu items,
  and shortcuts, a 0.05x step for the per-book sheet (stepper plus slider, both
  with explicit VoiceOver labels and values), snapping to the 0.05 grid, and one
  formatter ("1.25x" visible, "1.25 times speed" spoken). Saved audio multipliers
  no longer reach the speech engine's WPM path (reader open and global settings
  changes), which had slowed "Where Am I?" to 50 WPM in audio books. The per-book
  sheet never shows voice, WPM, or pitch controls for audio books.
  `PlaybackSettingsAuditTests`, `PlaybackPolicyProductionTests`.
- [x] **EPUB page lists and hidden navigation as headings (user report, "Echo of Worlds").**
  A guide `toc` reference without a fragment harvested every link in the navigation
  document, including the hidden page list (508 page links) and landmarks. Only
  table-of-contents semantics count now (EPUB 3 `toc` nav or `doc-toc`, NCX
  `navMap`, guide TOC); page lists, landmarks, and `hidden` subtrees contribute no
  headings, speech, or annotations, in direct and cached reading. Annotations for
  anchors inside a heading are hoisted before the heading instead of corrupting
  its label. Also found and fixed: a Markdown line beginning with an annotation
  opened a CommonMark HTML block that swallowed the rest of the line, silently
  dropping paragraphs that start with an anchor (86 of 623 cached books in the
  live library were affected; Echo of Worlds lost 145 paragraphs); empty named
  anchors produced literal "[]"; a line break inside a heading split it.
  Echo of Worlds: 86 chapters, 438 → 161 headings, 0 page-number headings, 0
  marker leaks. Text cache **3.0.5** rebuilds affected caches through the normal
  versioned workflow (`BookContentIndex.currentVersion` unchanged; the index is
  regenerated with the cache). `EPUBHeadingRecoveryTests` (16 new, including an
  end-to-end fixture of the reported shape), `LegacyMarkdownCacheTests`.
- [x] **Headings vocabulary (user request).** User-facing text no longer says "chapter":
  the reader header shows the current heading and "Heading N of M" counted over the
  same list the headings panel shows; Where Am I, the copied position, bookmark
  rows and rotor entries, search result locations, and sentence/paragraph moves
  across content units all use one `HeadingPosition` rule; library rows show
  percent complete instead of a content-unit number. Generated fallback titles
  ("Chapter N" for untitled spine documents, "Chapter" for untitled m4b markers)
  are content and stay unchanged so heading IDs remain stable.
  `HeadingVocabularyTests` and updated Where Am I, bookmark, and library tests.
- [x] **Q07 (partial): log writes cannot crash the app.** `TextMouthPaths.appendLog`
  used legacy FileHandle calls that raised uncatchable exceptions on a full disk
  (observed during this session on a volume at 99%). Writes now use the throwing
  APIs and drop the line on failure; logs rotate at 2 MB into one `.previous`
  generation; VoiceOver focus diagnostics share the same serial writer.
  `LogWriteSafetyTests`. Remaining Q07 work: decide which book text and path
  details the logs need.

- [x] **S07 and S08 — Folder-name equivalence and subtree validation.** One
  `FolderPathEquivalence` rule (Unicode NFC, plus case folding when the library
  volume ignores case; detection cached per directory, unknown treated as
  case-insensitive) backs every collision check in create, rename, move, and
  remove. Removing a folder validates its whole subtree first (destination
  exists and is outside the folder, no equivalent child names, depth limit) and
  refuses with a `FolderOperationConflict` message that names the folders
  involved; nothing touches disk on refusal. Case-only and normalization-only
  renames rename the directory in place (two-step fallback). Folder rows that
  older versions let share one directory are merged once at startup, logged as
  `FOLDER_REPAIR`, deleting nothing. `FolderPathEquivalenceTests`,
  `FolderDuplicateRepairTests`, `FolderSafetyTests`, `LibraryMoveTransactionTests`.
  Not verified on a real case-sensitive volume or HFS+.
- [x] **A02 — Cancellable, responsive in-book search.** Searches run through a
  `@MainActor` `BookSearchModel`: 250 ms debounce, work on a detached task,
  cancellation between chapters and inside regex enumeration, a 2,000-result cap
  with a status line, generation-tagged completions so stale results never
  replace newer ones, and a VoiceOver announcement of each completed search.
  Results (chapter, UTF-16 offset, snippet, heading location, row identity) are
  identical to the previous synchronous search, verified against the shared
  matcher on Unicode text. Command-F is ignored for audio books.
  `BookSearchModelTests`.

- [x] **I07 and T04 (DAISY) — Inline fragment fidelity and malformed-XML reporting.**
  September 29. DAISY 2.02 SMIL fragments over inline containers now yield every
  word once, in order, with a read ledger for overlapping references (a sentence
  then a word inside it). A shared `DAISYSAXParser` records the first parser
  error with its line and column: damaged OPF, NCX, and SMIL files throw
  `DAISYParserError.malformedXML` naming the file; DTBook content keeps the text
  read before the damage and reports the rest through the debug log
  (`DAISY_INCOMPLETE_CONTENT`); `ncc.html` parses as tolerant HTML with SwiftSoup.
  The bundled DAISY fixtures extract byte-identically. `DAISYMalformedXMLTests`,
  `DAISYInlineFragmentTests` (40 new). The DOCX half of T04 remains open; the
  cache bump that lets older DAISY 2.02 caches regain truncated text ships with
  the T02 work (3.0.6).

- [x] **C02 — Validate externally editable settings at one boundary.** September 29.
  One `SettingsPolicy` table (numeric ranges with defaults and the 0 "not chosen"
  sentinel for speed and pitch; enum-backed choices) is applied when decoding
  `config.json` field by field (`LenientFields`, one `STORED_VALUE_REPAIRED` log
  line per repair), when applying a config snapshot, on the `@AppStorage` read
  path, and where services read the preference store directly. Out-of-range
  numbers clamp, non-finite values take the default, unknown choices keep the
  current setting, an unreadable `dataDirectoryPath` keeps the directory in use,
  and a bad field never resets the other fields. Saved book state repairs
  negative or oversized indices and offsets, invalid speeds (audio multipliers
  through `AudioSpeed`), pitches, and counts instead of failing the library.
  Restored sleep-timer minutes are clamped. `SettingsPolicyTests` (41 new), plus
  updated config, book-state, playback, and sleep-timer tests. Behavior change:
  a saved audio speed of 0 plays at 1.0x (was 0.25x).
- [x] **S01 — Recover unreadable library indexes without replacing the evidence.**
  September 29. `LibraryPersistenceService.load()` returns loaded, missing, or
  unreadable; an unreadable `library.json` is first copied verbatim to
  `library.json.unreadable-<UTC timestamp>` (a hard link when the bytes cannot be
  read), never overwritten or deleted. Index writes are suspended on the
  persistence queue (save, flush, legacy migration, data-directory migration)
  while a provisional library is rebuilt from packages and folders on disk so
  reading can continue; startup repairs return early. An accessible sheet and a
  library banner offer Try Again (keeps newer reading positions from the
  session), Rebuild Library Index (saves the provisional library after a copy
  exists), Continue Reading, Quit, and Show Saved Copy in Finder; Storage
  settings show "Library index unreadable" and disable location changes. A
  readable legacy index is now deleted only after the new index is written.
  `LibraryIndexQuarantineTests`, `LibraryIndexPersistenceTests`,
  `LibraryIndexRecoveryTests`, `LibraryFolderAdoptionTests` (41 new). Not run
  live with VoiceOver (A01).

- [x] **T02 — Preserve literal plain text through the cache pipeline.** September 29.
  `PlainTextParser.blocks(in:)` is the one line analysis (unchanged heading
  rules: ATX `#`, setext underlines, short all-caps lines) behind both the direct
  parser and the cache; the cache writes body lines as literal Markdown
  (`MarkdownLiteral.escaped` backslash-escapes CommonMark's ASCII punctuation
  per scalar, writes U+FEFF as a character reference, maps U+0000 to U+FFFD), so
  `*stars*`, `_underscores_`, backticks, `<tags>`, brackets, list markers,
  quotes, entities, and `a < b` are read as written. The direct path now applies
  the same normalization (trimmed lines, paragraph lines joined by spaces, blocks
  separated by one blank line), and a seeded 1,000-source test plus fixtures
  assert `direct.text == cached.text` and identical headings. Cache **3.0.6**
  rebuilds every text cache, which also restores DAISY 2.02 fragment text.
  `ParserContentFidelityTests`, `LegacyMarkdownCacheTests` (15 new). Saved
  positions in `.txt` books shift after the rebuild (T06); CRLF handling is
  unchanged (T08).

- [x] **P06 — Advance safely when filtering removes all speakable text.** September 29.
  A chapter is speakable only when its fully prepared text (skipped sections
  removed, punctuation pauses, substitutions) contains a non-whitespace
  character (`hasSpeakableText`); `nextSpeakableChapterIndex` searches forward
  once, never wraps. Play, chapter ends, navigation and resume, and the
  look-ahead pre-render pass over silent chapters; when nothing remains the
  book ends through its configured behavior with no Playing state, no play
  sound, and no listening session, and Loop stops after one silent pass.
  Whitespace-only text receives no pause marks; a pre-rendered chapter is kept
  only while it is still the chosen next chapter. `SpeakableChapterAdvanceTests`
  (37 new). Not listened to live.

- [x] **A03 — Keyboard- and VoiceOver-operable substitution editing and reordering.**
  September 30. Each rule row is an accessibility container labelled with the
  rule's summary (name, pattern, replacement, matching options; no enabled
  state) holding an "Enable <name or pattern>" checkbox and Edit, Move Up, Move
  Down, and Delete buttons (dimmed at the ends); the row's VoiceOver actions
  offer only the commands that can act. Pointer behavior is kept (click to edit,
  drag to reorder, context menu with Move Up/Down added). Moves, including drags,
  announce "<name> moved to position N of M"; deletes announce "<name> deleted";
  the footer explains both ways to reorder. One shared `VoiceOverAnnouncer`
  (message, optional window, priority; injectable poster for tests) replaces the
  three copies in the reader, library, and sleep timer with unchanged behavior.
  `VoiceOverAnnouncerTests`, `SubstitutionKeyboardAccessTests` (22 new),
  `AccessibilityTestSupport` shared helper. Not run with live VoiceOver (A01).
- [x] **S03 — Verify legacy migration destinations before deleting source content.**
  September 30. `LegacyBookMigration` (new) replaces `migrateBookToTMB`: the
  original is fingerprinted (size and MD5), copied under a hidden temporary name,
  verified, and renamed into place without overwriting; a package already holding
  different content is never touched (the book moves into a new package from the
  same `tmbName` scheme and the original is kept, with a warning); an identical
  copy is reused only when the package is the book's own. `bookInfo.json` is
  written with the throwing API before anything is removed, and a failure discards
  only what the run created. The original is removed through the Trash
  preference (main actor; elsewhere it is kept and the reason reported), and the
  text cache is built last with failures reported as warnings. Interrupted runs
  are finished only on proof (package names the book and the copy matches the
  recorded hash); an original that is the copy itself, or that other records
  still read, is never removed. Failures are collected per book and shown once
  after startup repair, Rebuild Directory, and Reset to Default, with a
  `LEGACY_MIGRATION:` summary in the debug log. `LegacyBookMigrationTests`
  (28 new). Not verified against a real legacy library.

- [x] **C01 and C03 — Atomic configuration apply and self-write detection.**
  September 30. `ConfigService` reads `config.json` synchronously before any
  consumer sees its values; `hasExistingConfig` means a decodable file exists;
  launch applies the file's snapshot first, then voice discovery, with saving
  paused, and saves once only if the bytes would change (every save now skips
  identical bytes). The settings model receives each external edit once,
  synchronously. The watcher remembers the bytes last written or examined and
  compares by content on the main actor (own bytes ignored, other bytes applied
  once); it delivers on the main queue, coalesces bursts, tracks the file by
  device and inode, rearms immediately on rename/delete/revoke and after its own
  save (retrying a missing file five times), and checks content on every newly
  watched file. An edit landing between two saves is merged into the second
  (TextMouth's changed fields win); a repaired edit is written back once and
  recognized as its own; invalid JSON keeps current settings, stays on disk,
  and is logged with line and column. `ConfigWatcherTests` (23 new),
  `ConfigTestSupport`, rewritten watcher tests in `ConfigSyncTests`. Not tried
  with real editors.
- [x] **T03 — Preserve HTML block/table boundaries and literal text.** September 30.
  `HTMLReadingText` (new) holds one table of reading rules for both the direct
  reader and the Markdown converter: SwiftSoup block elements plus `br` are
  boundaries (cells, `br`, `ins`/`del`/`s` separate words; other blocks are
  paragraphs; a table row is one paragraph of space-joined cells), text is
  written through `MarkdownLiteral.escaped` (moved to its own file), headings
  are literal labels (a heading inside a heading, `pre`, or `code` is not a
  heading on either path), links keep angle-bracket destinations, emphasis is
  marked only where CommonMark is certain to match, `pre` keeps every line with a
  fence longer than any inner backtick run, inline code uses a longer backtick
  run, and adjacent code spans cannot merge. A test invariant asserts the
  direct and cached readings agree word for word under one normalization
  (Unicode whitespace runs to one space, trimmed) with equal heading labels and
  levels; a 100,000-source fuzz run found five bugs now pinned by tests
  (U+200B, combining marks, list numbers, merged fences, trimmed labels). Cache
  **3.0.7** rebuilds every text cache. Helliconia: 461 spoken asterisks removed,
  10,995 straight quotes restored, no heading IDs changed; `.html` books now
  strip duplicate navigation like EPUBs. `ParserContentFidelityTests` (25 new).
  About 8% slower conversion; heading IDs shift only where a label regains
  characters (T06).

- [x] **T04 (DOCX half) — Report malformed DOCX XML instead of returning partial documents.**
  September 30. `PackageSAXParser` (new, format-neutral) holds the SAX driver and
  damage-reason table formerly in `DAISYSAXParser`; `DAISYSAXParser` and the new
  `DOCXSAXParser` are thin subclasses, and `PackageDamage` shares the sentence and
  log line. Damaged `styles.xml`, `numbering.xml`, or relationships parts throw
  `DOCXParserError.malformedXML(file:reason:)`; `document.xml` text read before the
  damage is kept with open runs, links, paragraphs, and tables closed (whitespace
  at the cut dropped so emphasis stays valid) and a `DOCX_INCOMPLETE_CONTENT` log
  line; damage before any text throws; damaged `core.xml` falls back to the file
  name with a `DOCX_DAMAGED_METADATA` line. Parts are parsed from raw bytes, so
  invalid bytes are reported with line and column and UTF-16 parts now read.
  Fixtures pinned by byte count and SHA-256 convert identically. T04 is now
  complete. `DOCXMalformedXMLTests` (21 new), `DamagedFileTestSupport`,
  `ZIPTestSupport`.

- [x] **T01 — Validate persisted content-index structure and media paths.** September 30.
  `BookContentIndex.validationFailure(for:audioBaseURL:)` names the first problem
  by `bookInfo.json` field (chapter positions, cumulative offsets and totals,
  heading levels and containment, size caps of 100 million UTF-16 units, finite
  non-negative audio timing with a 1 ms total tolerance, relative in-folder audio
  paths that are regular files where they exist, text indexes carrying no audio);
  a missing audio file or folder does not reject the index, so the book keeps every
  chapter and playback reports the missing file with Retry (P04); `isValid`
  wraps it. `BookContentAnalysisService.load` and `completedTextCacheIndex` use
  it, log `CONTENT_INDEX_REJECTED: <title>: <reason>` once, and re-analyze; a text
  index whose chapter count or lengths disagree with `content.md` is also
  re-indexed. `resolvedAudioFilePaths` returns nil instead of a shortened list.
  The builder records audio paths by resolved location and keeps headings inside
  their chapter. All 12 TestData books keep the fast path.
  `ContentIndexValidationTests` (65 new), `AudioFixtureSupport`.

- [x] **P04 — Observe audio item failures and expose recoverable playback errors.**
  September 30. `AudioPlaybackService` checks that a file exists and is readable
  before building a player, observes `AVPlayerItem`/`AVPlayer` status and errors,
  failed-to-play-to-end notices, and stalls (progress checked 10 s after any
  play, resume, seek, or stall, after letting AVFoundation try to recover), and
  reports them through one typed failure (`onPlaybackFailed` and a published
  value) that clears the playing state and keeps the position. A late
  end-of-file notice for a replaced track is ignored. The reader stops like a
  user Stop (Now Playing, listening session closed, sleep-timer countdown paused,
  Where Am I resume flag cleared), sets a message naming the chapter and file
  with what to do, speaks it, and shows a "Playback Problem" alert whose default
  button is Retry (plays again from the saved position). Reader errors over a
  loaded book are now alerts instead of replacing the book with "Failed to load
  book". `AudioPlaybackFailureTests`, `ReaderAudioFailureTests`,
  `AudioTestFixtures` (32 new). Not verified in the running app.

- [x] **S06 — Keep Remove from Library Only removed across pending saves and restart.**
  September 30. `RemovedFromLibraryMarker` (new) writes `removedFromLibrary.json`
  (book ID, title, author, date) into the package before the row is taken; a
  failed marker write refuses the removal with an alert naming the book and
  package. On the metadata queue the marker write, cancelling the book's pending
  save, and deleting `bookInfo.json` are one step, and every later save for a
  retired package (debounced, at quit, from background extraction) is dropped;
  tracking uses book ID plus package location so a re-imported copy keeps
  saving. Orphan adoption skips marked packages at startup, index recovery, and
  rebuild, and a listed book whose package marker names it is taken out at
  startup (the marker wins over an out-of-date index). A failed `bookInfo.json`
  deletion is reported but the book stays out. Removal uses the book's current
  library entry rather than the dialog's snapshot. `LibraryOnlyRemovalTests`
  (26 new). Follow-up for the import path: `importTMBPackage` must remove the
  marker from a staged package, and re-import should reinstate a retained
  package by content hash.

- [x] **C05 — Checkpoint active listening time.** September 30. `ReadingStatsService`
  keeps an `ActiveSession` with a credited-through point that never moves back:
  every 60 seconds (the interval is injectable) the timer credits the slice since
  the last checkpoint to the grand, per-book, and daily totals and saves; ending
  a session is a final checkpoint plus close; `startSession` ending another
  session and `flushPendingSaves` credit the same way, so a crash loses at most
  one minute and no second is counted twice. Today and This Week add only the
  uncredited remainder. A session across midnight is now split at the first
  checkpoint after midnight (the exact split is C06). The timer keeps firing
  during menus and modal panels and no longer rewrites unchanged statistics
  while idle. `init(fileURL:checkpointInterval:)` is the test seam.
  `ReadingStatsServiceTests` (15 new).
- [x] **I06 — Report partial EPUB chapter extraction and keep originals.** September 30.
  `ExtractionReport` (new, format-neutral) lists chapters an extraction could not
  read (missing file, damaged file by checksum or decompression, unreadable
  markup, spine entry with no file, unsupported media type) with a count of blank
  chapters kept separately; EPUB member checksums are now verified and a damaged
  container or package document throws a named error; media types compare
  case-insensitively. Cache generation carries the report, logs one
  `EPUB_INCOMPLETE_CONTENT: <title>: <message>` line per generation, and a
  rebuild that finds nothing readable leaves the earlier `content.md` untouched.
  An EPUB import with omissions completes with one warning ("… The original
  file was kept.") and never deletes or offers to delete the original; an
  incomplete duplicate carries its warning inside the duplicate dialog; ZIP
  batches list incomplete books and keep the ZIP; an EPUB with nothing readable
  fails the import and removes the new package; the reader shows the reason
  instead of an empty book. Other formats remain S02. `ExtractionReportTests`,
  `EPUBPartialExtractionTests` (43 new). Alerts not checked with VoiceOver.

- [x] **T08 — Decode text imports by BOM or declared encoding and handle every line ending.**
  October 2. `TextFileDecoder` (new) decodes `.txt`, `.md`, and `.html` on both
  the direct and cached paths: BOM (UTF-8, UTF-16 LE/BE, UTF-32), unmarked
  UTF-16 when unambiguous, UTF-8, an HTML `<meta charset>`/`http-equiv`/XML
  declaration in the first 1024 bytes (iso-8859-1 and us-ascii read as
  Windows-1252 as browsers do), then a Windows-1252 table that never refuses a
  byte, with one `TEXT_ENCODING_FALLBACK` log line per read; an unknown label or
  bytes invalid in the declared encoding throw `TextEncodingError`; invalid bytes
  after a UTF-16/32 BOM are "damaged or incomplete". Leading U+FEFF is removed;
  CR LF and lone CR become LF in all three formats so setext underlines follow
  their heading and CR LF files no longer read one paragraph per line. No cache
  bump (caches rebuild at install anyway). `TextFileDecoderTests` (30),
  `TextEncodingFidelityTests` (8).

- [x] **P02 — Carry continue-next autoplay intent into the next reader.** October 2.
  `ReaderAutoplayRequests` (new) holds one consumable request per book (a newer
  request replaces it, claiming removes it, a request older than 30 s or across a
  clock jump is discarded). The finishing reader leaves the request inside its
  0.5 s delay and then posts the notification; a new reader claims it when loading
  finishes and starts through `play()` from the saved position without a second
  announcement (a failed load drops it); an already-open reader claims it on the
  notification (paused resumes, playing stays untouched, a speaking Where Am I is
  cut off first). The router picks exactly one opener: the registered Library
  window, or, only when none is registered, an AppDelegate fallback that opens
  through an `openWindow` action lent by reader windows. A sleep timer belonging
  to the finished book (running or just expired at end of document) stops the
  continuation with "Finished: <title>. Next book not started because of the
  sleep timer." `ReaderViewModel` takes its request store and notification
  center as init arguments. `ReaderAutoplayRequestsTests`,
  `ContinueToNextBookTests`, router tests (39 new). Not run live.

- [x] **S02 — Make every import commit conditional on readable content and durable metadata.**
  October 2. `ImportStaging` and `ImportFailure` (new): every import (file,
  audio ZIP, DAISY audio ZIP, ZIP batch, `.tmb` package, pasted text) builds its
  package as a hidden `.import-<UUID>.tmb` in the destination folder and
  publishes it with one rename that never replaces an existing package; a text
  cache failure in any format, audio without a playable chapter, or a
  `bookInfo.json` that cannot be written and read back (checked for book ID,
  checksum, format, file name, and index) fails the import, removes the staged
  package, and never touches the source. Every import returns `ImportedBook` or
  throws `ImportFailure` ("Could not import "<file>". <Step> failed: <cause>";
  one `IMPORT_FAILED` log line; plain-language audio and ZIP causes). Leftover
  staging packages are never adopted. Package imports clear a carried removal
  marker, and a retained package for the same book ID or checksum is validated
  in place and taken back through `reinstatePackage(of:)` (record rebuilt from
  the marker and content file when `bookInfo.json` is gone; position and
  bookmarks are lost unless a matching listed copy exists). `ImportStagingTests`,
  `StagedImportTests`, `RetainedPackageImportTests` (46 new).

- [x] **S05 — Merge completed analysis with the latest durable book state.** October 2.
  `saveContentAnalysis` reads the latest state on the metadata queue (the
  waiting save, else `bookInfo.json` on disk, else the book it was given),
  changes only the analysis-owned fields and the index, and returns the merged
  book, which the migration and reader call sites apply to the library row
  through one helper; a result whose source hash or format differs, or whose
  record belongs to another book, is dropped with one
  `CONTENT_ANALYSIS_DROPPED` line (retired packages still drop silently). Every
  record carries a rising `BookState.revision` (persisted; the queue never lets
  it fall); a save without an index keeps the stored index, counts, and cache
  version; a failed analysis write no longer loses a waiting user save. The
  queue's ordering guarantee is documented in one comment block.
  `ContentAnalysisMergeTests` (25 new).

- [x] **A07 and A08 — Real operation outcomes and removal wording that matches the mode.**
  October 2. `OperationOutcome` (new): folder create, rename, move, and remove,
  book moves, rebuild, data-directory move, and reset have `…ReturningOutcome`
  variants (the old names keep showing the library alert). The Storage settings
  status line shows "Done…" only on success, otherwise the failure sentence,
  announced once through `VoiceOverAnnouncer`, with buttons disabled while an
  operation runs or the index is unreadable and re-enabled for retry; legacy
  books that could not move are listed after "Done". The library's folder and
  move sheets stay open with their input on failure, show the reason in red
  ("Error: …" to VoiceOver, announced once, cleared when the input changes), and
  navigate only on success; a renamed or moved folder's window follows it.
  `RemovalWording` (new) is the one source for every removal label and message
  in three modes (Remove from Library Only, Move to Trash, Delete Permanently);
  the context menu and edit bar say exactly what they do when confirmation is
  off, Delete Everything says where files go, and the Duplicate Finder reads
  "Move N Selected to Trash" or "Delete N Selected Permanently", asks first when
  confirmation is on, deletes through the Trash-aware path, and keeps books it
  could not remove selected. `FolderOperationOutcomeTests`,
  `StorageOperationOutcomeTests`, `RemovalWordingTests`, `RemovalOutcomeTests`
  (45 new; one skips without VoiceOver running). The main Remove Book dialog in
  `ContentView` and the menu title are a follow-up.

- [x] **P03 — Invalidate pre-rendered speech when its inputs change.** October 2.
  Rendered speech carries a signature (hash and length of the exact spoken text
  plus the voice; speed and pitch are applied at play time, so they are excluded
  on purpose). A look-ahead render is kept only if the chapter prepared again
  from the current settings has the same signature; the chapter-end handoff uses
  pre-rendered audio only for that chapter, finished, with a matching signature,
  otherwise it logs once and starts from the current text (generation checks
  unchanged). `refreshLookAhead()` re-arms the look-ahead whenever the voice, a
  substitution rule, the punctuation level, or a skipped section changes, while
  playing or paused. Direct speech switches to rendered audio only when it
  matches the text being spoken and the current voice (seek, resume, sleep
  fade, watchdog); stopping abandons the look-ahead (closes the September 29
  follow-up). The reader takes an injectable render scheduler.
  `PreRenderedSpeechInvalidationTests` (41) and two engine tests (43 new). Not
  listened to live.
- [x] **A08 follow-up — Remove Book dialog and menu title.** October 2. The main
  Remove Book dialog's title, buttons, and message come from
  `RemovalChoiceWording` ("Move to Trash" or "Delete Permanently" beside "Remove
  from Library Only"), updating live through a shared `RemovalPreferencesModel`
  that signals only when one of the two removal preferences changes; the menu
  bar's Remove command reads "Remove from Library..." when confirmation is on
  and otherwise "Move to Trash" or "Delete Permanently" (a live check against
  the app's real Library menu confirms the title changes). Three new tests.

- [x] **Q01 — Isolate all hosted-test side effects, including legacy migration and preferences.**
  October 3. `TextMouthRuntime` (new) decides hosted-test isolation once from
  `XCTestConfigurationFilePath` and supplies the home directory and the
  preferences store (the real home and `.standard` in the app; a per-process
  `$TMPDIR/TextMouthTests-<pid>/` sandbox with its data root, legacy locations,
  and all preference stores under `SandboxedPreferences/` in tests). Every former
  `.standard` site (sound effects, the substitution switch, the data-directory
  key, window restoration) and the default stores of the settings model,
  keyboard shortcuts, and sleep timer use it, with keys unchanged. Sandbox and
  legacy Application Support migration work only inside the runtime's home. The
  announcer records instead of posting under tests; a bundle observer closes any
  reader window a test leaves open and fails that test. Sentinel tests plant
  uniquely named files at the real locations, fingerprint real preference keys
  read-only, run startup repair, both migrations, settings changes, and
  `setCustomRoot`, and prove nothing outside the sandbox changed, also with the
  home pointed at an empty folder. `HostedTestIsolationTests` and observers
  (26 new). Past runs left about 10,900 empty test plists in the real
  preferences folder; cleanup is a user decision.

- [x] **I04 — Validate actual media duration against segment ranges.** October 2.
  `AudioMediaProbe` (new) probes each referenced audio file once per parse (up to
  four at a time) with precise timing (`AVURLAssetPreferPreciseDurationAndTimingKey`,
  needed because VBR MP3s without a Xing header were estimated 20 to 40 percent
  wrong), requires a playable asset with a decodable audio track and a positive
  duration, and decodes the track's own first half second and last second when
  the file is readable (the only check that catches a truncated `.m4a`/`.m4b`).
  Every section's start must be before its file's end; an end may run one
  second plus one percent past it (DAISY Pipeline's tolerance, LAME and AAC
  priming) and is then moved back to the file's length with one
  `AUDIO_SEGMENT_CLAMPED` line; further is an error naming the heading, file,
  and real length in spoken words. Overlapping sections stay allowed (the DAISY
  converter produces them); sections within a file may not start before an
  earlier one. A damaged file reuses the `PackageDamage` sentence. Valid
  audiobooks parse exactly as before (pinned). `AudioMediaValidationTests` (37),
  `AudioMediaImportTests` (6); the two audio fixture helpers are unified in
  `AudioTestFixtures`. MP3 verified outside the suite only.

- [x] **S09 — Surface persistence and cleanup failures consistently.** October 3.
  `PersistenceFailureCenter` (new, reportable from any thread) keeps the latest
  failure per file (path, error, time, retry) for the library index (debounced,
  flush, legacy migration, rebuild), book records (reported from their own queue
  without blocking it; a write whose package is gone while the library folder
  remains is dropped with a `BOOK_METADATA_DROPPED` line), statistics, settings,
  substitution rules, the move rollback (both paths named), and the import's
  original-file deletion, which is now throwing and Trash-aware; a later
  successful write of the same file clears it, retries never write stale data,
  and `hasUnsavedChanges` is immediate. The Library window shows one banner row
  per subsystem with Retry and Dismiss (VoiceOver labels name the subsystem;
  failures that start together are announced in one message). Quitting flushes
  without ending the listening session and, with unsaved changes, cancels once
  with Retry / Quit Anyway / Cancel. The Storage status says "Changes not saved"
  with reasons and a Retry Saving button, and the Delete Original File dialog
  says Trash or permanent. Disk operations in the move transaction are
  injectable (`LibraryTransitionDisk`). 68 new tests (one skips without an
  assistive client). Not driven with real VoiceOver.

- [x] **I02 — Import loose DAISY packages with their dependent files.** October 3.
  `DAISYParser+PackageInventory` (new) lists the files a book needs from its OPF
  manifest or from the NCC and the SMIL, text, audio, CSS, and image files they
  reference (fragments and percent escapes resolved as the reader resolves them;
  a leading slash is the book's folder; `..` escapes, `file:` URLs, and symbolic
  links pointing outside are errors naming the reference; web addresses are
  ignored; a damaged manifest falls back to the whole folder). A loose OPF,
  `ncc.html`, or DAISY folder imports as one package with that inventory under
  its original layout (`ncc.html` wins over an OPF; several OPFs at the top are
  several books), with a checksum of the OPF or NCC content plus every file's
  path and size, so re-imports are duplicates; DAISY folder imports are never
  deleted or offered for deletion. ZIP batches import each packaged book once
  and skip hidden files and `__MACOSX`. Also fixed: DAISY folders behind a
  symbolic link listed files with a `private/` prefix and could not be read;
  opening a DAISY `.tmb` was refused on its hash; recovery adopted the first
  file of a multi-file package (now the recorded file name); package files
  named with `#`, `%`, or `:` were skipped. `DAISYPackageInventoryTests`,
  `DAISYPackageImportTests`, shared fixtures (44 new). The Open panel's folder
  handling is being moved to a dedicated command in a follow-up.
- [x] **I02 follow-up — Import DAISY Folder command.** October 3. The Open Book
  panel is file-only again; Library > Import DAISY Folder… (Command-Shift-O)
  shows a folder-only panel as a sheet on the key Library window, and the
  chosen folders import as DAISY packages into the folder being viewed (one book
  per folder, or one per top-level OPF; a folder with no book is refused with a
  reason). Finder "Open With" on an `ncc.html` or `.opf` still routes through
  the package import. Two tests read the real Library menu and the panel's
  configuration without showing anything.

- [x] **C06 — Attribute statistics across day boundaries and calendar changes.**
  October 3. `ListeningDays` (new): day keys are always Gregorian `yyyy-MM-dd`
  with the POSIX locale and ASCII digits in the current time zone; every
  credited slice is split at local midnight with `dateInterval(of: .day)` and
  published in one update (Today and This Week count only the open session's
  uncredited time within their days); weeks and streaks step back one calendar
  day at a time (a test covers the day Samoa skipped); a slice is attributed in
  the zone current when credited and credited time never moves.
  `ListeningDayKeyMigration` (new) rewrites keys saved under other calendars
  once (Gregorian first, then the user's calendar as the old formatter wrote
  it, then ASCII digits, then the other macOS calendars; every reading must
  round-trip; same-day keys merge), keeps unreadable keys as `legacy:<key>`
  entries counted in the all-time total only, logs once, and marks the file
  with `dayKeyVersion`. The unused `pruneOldEntries()` is removed. The service
  takes `userCalendar:` for tests. `ReadingStatsDayAttributionTests` (19),
  `ListeningDayKeyMigrationTests` (9).

- [x] **A04 — Bookmark rows and actions reachable by keyboard, focus restored after edits.**
  October 3. Every bookmark row is a native button (Go To on Return or Space)
  with its own Edit Note and Delete buttons named by bookmark number and
  VoiceOver actions on the row group and its main button; rows are cached and
  kept alive so note edits never move focus. One `focusedControl` binding and a
  pure decision choose the focus after opening (the last bookmark at or before
  the reading position, else the first after it), adding (the new bookmark; the
  Add button after a cancel), saving or cancelling an edit (that bookmark), and
  deleting (the next, else the previous, else Add), applied to keyboard focus
  and, through a per-request `InitialVoiceOverFocusModifier`, to the VoiceOver
  cursor; Up and Down Arrow move between rows and the focused row scrolls into
  view. Hosted test windows are titled so VoiceOver never brings them forward.
  `BookmarkKeyboardAccessTests` (35 new; the label check runs only with an
  assistive client). Not verified with live VoiceOver.

- [x] **A09 — Keep metadata writeback errors and destinations clear.** October 3.
  `EPUBMetadataSave` (new): the reader and the library share one save that works
  on the book as the library holds it, writes the library's copy of the EPUB
  first (the parser now hashes the prepared copy before replacing the file, so a
  failed write always leaves the file as it was), and only then updates the row,
  `bookInfo.json`, the hash, and the reader; a failure changes nothing anywhere,
  names the file ("Could not save metadata to the library's copy of "alice.epub":
  … Nothing was changed."), and keeps the editor open with the user's edits; a
  book no longer in the library is not saved; the library's dropped `try?` is
  gone. The toggle reads "Also write to the library's copy of the EPUB file"
  with a hint that the original is untouched, and Save's VoiceOver label says
  library only or library and copy. A failed edit no longer leaks into Now
  Playing, the window title, later saves, or finished-book statistics. The
  reader announces "Settings applied as global defaults"; a failed config save
  reaches the persistence banner. `EPUBMetadataWritebackTests` (11 new).

- [x] **A06 — Restore folder and category sorting consistently.** October 3.
  `LibrarySortState` (new): the window title, the list, and the Sort menu all
  derive from `Library.sortState(at:)` on every draw, so a window shows its
  saved sort from the first frame (and after index recovery's Try Again or a
  change in another window) with no stored copy, no timing guard, and no
  load/save `onChange` handlers; the only save is `userChangedSort(_:at:)`,
  under the view the change was made in. Each field's last direction is
  remembered per view (`rememberedDirections`, omitted when empty, tolerant of
  old files); manual order has no direction (the direction command is disabled
  there, and a saved manual-descending setting shows the arranged order). Sort
  fields are checkable menu items; changes announce "Sorted by Title,
  descending" and Apply Sort to All Subfolders announces too and copies field,
  direction, and remembered directions but never the book order. Reordering
  works only where one folder's manual order is shown (Folders and No Folder,
  not while searching) and keyboard moves write each moved book's record like
  drags. `LibrarySortStateTests`, `LibrarySortSavingTests`,
  `ManualOrderRearrangingTests` (27 new). Not checked live.

- [x] **C04 — Complete configuration mirroring and refresh open settings views.**
  October 3. `StoredPreference` (new) defines each preference once and a store
  observer lets the sound effects service, the substitution switch, and the
  shortcut manager follow the shared store, so an applied edit takes effect
  without relaunch and changes made in the Sounds, Text, and Shortcuts tabs save
  `config.json`. The file now carries every portable preference under one
  documented portability table: the playback-bar control preferences, the sound
  effect preferences (every event written, `None` for no sound, volume to the
  hundredth), the substitution switch, and the keyboard shortcuts as readable
  text such as `option+shift+command+b` (lenient on reading; a mistyped shortcut
  leaves that action unchanged; a shortcut given to one action is removed from
  others). A missing new field keeps the current value. The Storage pane's
  choices bind to shared state so outside edits show at once; unhandled edits
  merge entry by entry for shortcuts and sounds. `PreferenceStoreTests` and
  extended config tests (41 new).

- [x] **C07 — Refresh live statistics without requiring playback to stop.** October 3.
  The old 30-second timer bumped state the view never read, so the pane only
  advanced at checkpoints; now `ReadingStatsFigures` reads every figure at one
  instant on each tick (clock and tick source injectable; the timer kept in
  state so settings changes do not restart it), rows are keyed by
  `ReadingStatsTile` with fixed identities and accessibility identifiers, no
  `.id` is used, and nothing is announced. All Time includes the open session's
  unsaved time (`allTimeListeningTime(at:)`), finished books are counted at a
  given instant, and one wording function builds each tile's visible value and
  spoken label with correct plurals ("1 day", "3 books") and durations spoken
  in words. `ReadingStatsViewTests` (9) plus service tests (12 new). Not
  observed with live VoiceOver.

- [x] **I05 — Bound archive and recursive-import resource use.** October 3.
  `ArchiveBudget` and `ArchiveExtraction` (new) inspect an archive before
  extracting (declared total against 8 GB when it holds audio or other ZIPs and
  2 GB otherwise, 10,000 entries, one nesting level, 1 GB kept free on the
  library's disk) and extract entry by entry within the budget, holding each
  file to its recorded size and a running total; extraction happens in an
  "Archive Extraction" folder in the data directory, and leftover
  `extract-<pid>-<uuid>` folders are swept at startup. `ArchiveEntryReader`
  (new) gives the EPUB, DOCX, and DAISY readers one entry reader with
  per-entry limits (256 MB audio, 64 MB otherwise). ZIP batches report
  extraction progress, honor cancellation (`cancelImports()`, logged as
  `IMPORT_CANCELLED`), and list archives that are too deep, damaged, or lying
  about sizes as failures rather than skipping them. Path escapes are refused
  with a named error and ZIPFoundation 0.9.20's behavior is recorded in tests.
  80 new tests. The visible Cancel button landed in a follow-up the same night.

- [x] **A05 — Route app-wide actions when a reader is focused or no Library
  window exists.** October 3. `LibraryWindowRouter` listens for every app-wide
  action and decides once through
  `handler(for:keyWindow:registeredLibraryWindows:readerWindows:)`: a registered
  Library window (key, else first registered) performs it through the closure it
  gave on registration; with none, a reader window opens a book; otherwise the
  router opens a Library window through SwiftUI's own delegate path
  (`applicationOpenUntitledFile`, the only call that worked in probes) and
  replays the action one turn after it registers (actions wait up to 30 s; one
  window is requested at a time). Library windows register again when SwiftUI
  shows them again (Finder opens into a re-shown window were silently ignored
  before), closed windows no longer receive notifications, panels bring their
  window forward first, Finder opens go through the router with a 2 s fallback,
  Go to Library works (it never had), and the blank "New Book Reader Window"
  item is removed. `LibraryWindowRouterTests` grew from 16 to 36. Not verified
  live.

- [x] **T07 — Fingerprint cache sources and `content.md` so edits and replaced
  sources are detected.** October 3. `TextCacheFingerprint` (size, nanosecond
  modification time, full MD5 through the existing `md5Hash` function) is
  recorded for the source file and for the exact bytes written to `content.md`
  whenever a cache is written; the content index carries both and they are
  mirrored as analysis-owned fields on the library row and the package record
  (the record is the trusted copy because the row can lag). An unchanged book
  costs two `stat` calls; a file with its old size and a new date is hashed and
  kept when the bytes match; an edited `content.md` or a replaced source
  rebuilds the cache from the source; a missing or unreadable source never
  counts as a change (the cache is the only copy of the text). Pairing a saved
  index with its text now compares heading count, offset, label, and level,
  per-chapter word counts, and the totals, naming the first mismatch. Startup
  queues only books whose source or `content.md` size differs from the row;
  older caches gain fingerprints on their first verified load or in the
  background statistics pass, one book at a time at utility priority.
  `TextCacheFingerprintTests`, `TextCacheVerificationTests` (45 new). Not run
  against the real library (about 627 books get one hashing pass).
- [x] **Q03 — Add reproducible CI and deliberate dependency locking.** October 3.
  `project.yml` requires exact versions (ZIPFoundation 0.9.20, SwiftSoup
  2.13.9, swift-markdown 0.9.0, which brings swift-cmark 0.9.0) and
  `TextMouth.xcodeproj/project.xcworkspace/xcshareddata/swiftpm/Package.resolved`
  is committed instead of ignored; deleting it and regenerating reproduces it
  byte for byte. `make check` runs `Scripts/check.sh`: regenerate, resolve
  strictly from `Package.resolved` (`-onlyUsePackageVersionsFromResolvedFile`,
  after checking the file exists because that flag passes silently without
  it), verify every pin, build Release, run the hosted suite, and fail on any
  error, on zero executed tests, or on a pin that moved; logs land in
  `build/check/`. `make resolve-packages` and `make update-packages` are the
  deliberate upgrade path; `make check-selftest` (30 cases with dry runs and
  stubbed tools) and `make check CHECK_ARGS=--dry-run` test the gate in
  seconds. `.github/workflows/check.yml` runs the gate on `macos-26` with
  Xcode 26.0.1 (actions pinned by commit, XcodeGen by digest, SwiftPM cache,
  log upload on failure); it has never run on GitHub. The first gate run caught
  real drift: fresh checkouts resolved swift-markdown 0.9.0 while the
  long-lived checkout still built 0.7.3. No Swift files changed.
- [x] **I05 follow-up — Cancel button in the import overlay.** October 3.
  `ProgressOverlay` takes an optional `onCancel`; the import overlay built by
  `LibraryWindowView.importProgressOverlay(for:)` passes
  `libraryViewModel.cancelImports`, so the button (VoiceOver "Cancel Import",
  hint "Stops importing ZIP archives after the current book. Books already
  imported stay in the library.", Escape presses it) ends a ZIP batch after the
  book in progress. The overlay is an accessibility container so the button is
  reachable, and the spoken status includes the message when the bar has no
  total. Shared key-equivalent and labelled-element helpers moved from the
  bookmark tests into `AccessibilityTestSupport`. `ProgressOverlayTests`
  (9 new; two skip without an assistive client).
- [x] **Tooling — Agent message board for concurrent agents.** October 3.
  `.claude/skills/agent-bbs/` adds a Python CLI over SQLite (`bbs.py`) where
  the agents working in parallel worktrees register with a role, branch, and
  claimed files, see overlapping claims, post build, commit, finding, and
  question notices, read an inbox of what concerns them, reply in threads,
  and wait for new messages; `build-slot-with-bbs.sh` announces builds on the
  board while holding a build slot. The board database
  (`.claude/agent-bbs/board.sqlite`, shared by every worktree) stays out of
  git. 85 Python unit tests; no Swift or project file changed, so the Xcode
  suite was not re-run for this merge.

- [x] **Q07 — Define what the diagnostic logs may contain and keep them bounded
  (remaining half).** October 3. `DiagnosticLog` is the one policy behind every
  log call in the reader, speech, sleep-timer, Now Playing, keyboard, VoiceOver
  focus, storage, statistics, migration, and import code: `event` lines are
  always written exactly as given and echoed to the console; `trace` lines are
  written only when `TEXTMOUTH_TRACE_LOG` is set or the hidden
  `diagnosticTraceLogging` preference is on, carry a `TRACE <category>:` mark,
  are limited to 20 a second per category, and report dropped lines in one
  `TRACE_SUPPRESSED:` line at most once a minute. Reader playback chatter (30
  lines), ordinary speech transitions (26), `COUNTDOWN_PROGRESS`, focus lines,
  and `MEDIA_ROUTE` became trace; playback failures became events
  (`AUDIO_PLAYBACK_FAILED`, `PLAYBACK_RESUME_REJECTED`). Every line is one
  line, paths inside the data directory are written relative to it
  (`loggablePath`), DAISY lines name the book folder and file, and focus lines
  give a label's length and a window's number, never text or titles.
  Sleep-timer and speech files keep their own logs through the same writer
  (two generations each). `LogContentPolicyTests` scans the source for book
  text, rule contents, absolute paths, and titles in log calls and names the
  files still writing directly (`AppDelegate`, `ConfigService`,
  `BookContentAnalysisService`, `ArchiveExtraction`, `LibraryWindowRouter`).
  `DiagnosticLogTests`, `LogContentPolicyTests` (33 new). Not run with
  tracing on against real VoiceOver focus sequences.

- [x] **I01 — Queue import decisions as well as file operations.** October 3.

  batch's questions. ContentView's three alerts became one sheet.

  `ImportDecisionFlowTests` (30 new; one more skip needs an assistive
  client). Not verified with live VoiceOver or in a running multi-window app.

- [x] **P10 — Refresh open readers after shared book edits and moves.** October 3.
  The reproduction showed a reader going stale after a library edit, folder

  per-book revision driven by a `didSet` on the library, every change to the

  audio reloads when resumed; an edit during loading shows once loaded; a
  load that fails because the book moved retries from the new path. Fixed in

  reader too. `LibraryBookUpdatesTests`, `ReaderRefreshTests` (24 new). Not
  verified live; the Now Playing refresh is unobservable from tests; the full
  Change Location command is not driven.

- [x] **S10 — Harden migration publication against destination changes and
  aliases.** October 3. The old code deleted a file that arrived in the empty
  new location during the copy and still reported success; a data directory
  that was a link was "copied" as a link, so the real library's paths were
  rewritten to the new location with no copy made; a new location that was a
  link to an empty folder was replaced by a real folder. `DataDirectoryMigrator`
  (its own file now) checks the new location again right before publishing:
  the name must still lead to the same folder and it must still be empty or
  missing; only an empty folder is ever removed (`rmdir`, which the kernel
  refuses for a non-empty folder) and the final rename uses `moveItem`, which
  never replaces an existing item, so anything that arrives stops the move,
  the staged copy is deleted, the directory in use and its setting stay, and
  the message names what arrived and where (Storage settings status line, the
  library alert, a VoiceOver announcement). Links: a linked data directory is
  copied from the folder it leads to; a new location that is a link to an
  empty folder receives the data there and the link stays; the Done message
  names the real folder; a link that leads nowhere, a file at the new
  location, and overlapping real folders are refused by name (the F20 rules
  unchanged). Paths are stored as chosen. `DataDirectoryMigrationTests` (19
  new, interrupting at the exact moments before `rmdir` and the rename).
  Tested on APFS only; not run with real VoiceOver.

- [x] **P05 — Prove chapter completion is delivered once per playback
  generation.** October 3. A probe showed AVFoundation posting an earlier
  "played to end" notice as the next play's seek landed (a looping one-file
  book skipped its first chapter or stalled), an end arriving just after Pause
  advancing and playing the next chapter while paused, a paused seek past a
  clip's end doing the same, a seek back at the end losing the seek or leaving
  the player stopped until a false "stalled" error, the reader's speech check
  comparing a generation with itself, and speech `didFinish` matching a new
  utterance at a reused address. Now every load, play, seek, stop, and finish
  starts a new audio generation; the end notice records the generation and
  playhead at posting; `AudioEndNoticePolicy` ends the play, holds it while
  paused (resume finishes it), ignores it as stale with one trace line, or
  restarts that play once on a fresh player; position ticks are tied to their
  player; a clip finishes only while playing; each chapter play gets a
  `ChapterPlay` token carried in the callback it hands the speech or audio
  service, so the reader moves on from the chapter that played, once; a
  finished utterance stays alive until its finish is handled.
  `CompletionGenerationTests`, `CompletionGenerationReaderTests` with the
  shared `SegmentedAudioBookFixture` (32 new; real AVFoundation callbacks and
  a 20-round seeded stress test). Not verified with variable-bit-rate MP3s.

- [x] **T09 — Retain short embedded PDF text when OCR is unavailable or less
  accurate.** October 3. With Vision available, the old parser replaced the
  title "Ballast" with OCR's "Ballasi", turned Greek and Hebrew titles into
  garbage, failed a whole PDF whose only text was "iv", dropped a picture's
  words beside a caption or under a paragraph, and returned only the stamps
  of a stamped scan. Now embedded text is always kept; OCR reads only the
  page's images (found by reading its drawing instructions; images that
  embedded text already covers by a quarter or more, and tiny images, are
  skipped) or the whole page within its crop box when it has no text; an OCR
  line that matches an embedded line, repeats a long embedded phrase, or sits
  on top of embedded text is dropped, and the rest go between whole
  paragraphs where they appear on the page. `PDFTextParser.extract(from:)`
  returns an `ExtractionReport` with a `pdf` page report (embedded only, OCR
  only, both, none; why OCR could not run; where it found nothing) that
  reaches imports as a warning that keeps the original file, is logged once
  per cache generation as `PDF_INCOMPLETE_CONTENT`, and gets its own ZIP
  summary section ("1 book has pages that could not be read"). The two
  TestData PDFs and a 200-page text PDF produce byte-identical output;
  finding images adds about a tenth to extraction time. New
  `PDFTextRecognizer`, `PDFPageImageScanner`, `PDFRecognitionPlan`,
  `PDFPageTextMerger`, `PDFExtractionDiagnostics`; seven test files (73
  new). No cache-format bump: PDFs with very short pages, pictures holding
  words, or stamped scans benefit from Redo Text Extraction. Only synthetic
  PDFs were tested.

  with the legacy migration's remover written as a closure, the one expression
  the mode found ambiguous. Zero errors and zero warnings. An audit traced the
  1,179 runtime isolation checks the mode adds to the app to main-thread or
  synchronous code, except the voice warm-up's buffer callback, which relies on
  AVSpeechSynthesizer calling back on the main thread (it did, 38 of 38 times);
  building it in a `nonisolated` helper is a follow-up (board #732). No new tests.

- [x] **Performance baselines (Q05).** October 3. Eleven hosted tests measure a
  library and books generated to the size of the developer's (launch with 643
  books, the first File Size sort and one list derivation per sort, a live
  position update, a checkpoint's bytes, opening a 1 MB EPUB and a three-file
  audiobook, the speech of a million-character chapter with the 16 rules, the
  rendered speech of a 40,000-character chapter, extracting the EPUB, a cloud
  refresh, one reader publish to the Headings sheet) and compare each median of
  five runs with the committed `performance-baselines.json`; a number over twice
  its baseline is measured again and fails only if it stays over twice; the
  tests skip while the Mac is busy or on another Mac model, and `make check`
  makes them measure anyway; `make perf-baselines` records new baselines on a
  quiet Mac and changes nothing unless the whole run succeeds. 37 new tests,
  26 of them for the rules. `make check` itself was not run end to end on the
  branch (disk space); its self-test and a dry run cover the new step, so the
  next GitHub run is the real check.

- [x] **Fifth follow-up bundle.** October 3. An End of heading sleep timer stops
  where the heading that was current when it was set ends, reached by playback
  (checked in the reader's refresh, at most every 0.12 s), by a chapter end with
  the fade, or by a forward move, and at the end of the book; a backward move
  leaves it waiting (board #787). The voice warm-up's buffer callback is built
  in a helper that is safe on any thread (#732). The session lease does its disk
  work on its own queue off the main actor, so a Sessions folder that has not
  synced cannot stall the main thread. The standard substitution rules stay in
  memory until a rule changes, so a synced `substitutions.json` arriving later
  is adopted without a conflict copy. Use This Library takes the storage hold
  itself and is refused while any other operation holds it, naming the
  operation. Four test files share one helper for a test's own data directory.
  18 new tests.

- [x] **Reading-text cache and speech prepared at open (PERF22, second half).**
  October 3, at the user's request. An agent measured the open path on main
  (the Markdown parse is 92 to 97 percent of every load; swift-markdown's tree
  and walk are three quarters of it) and compared, with numbers, lazy
  per-chapter parsing (no help for one-chapter books; chapter text is read at
  about 70 places), a direct cmark walk (still three times slower than a cache
  on the 28 MB book, and heading IDs depend on swift-markdown's labels), a
  spoken-text cache (needless once the opened chapter's speech is prepared in
  the background), and a kept reading text. Chosen: `ReadingTextCache`, this
  Mac's copy of each chapter's title, text, and headings for books whose
  `content.md` is a megabyte or more (183 of the user's 625 text books), at
  `~/Library/Caches/com.textmouth.TextMouth/Reading Text/<book ID>.readingtext`,
  never in the package (a cloud library would sync it, a move would copy it,
  the File Size sort would count it); used only when the record's digest, the
  file's size, dates, and inode, the title, the index version, and the build
  match and the saved index validates; removed a day after the last open (swept
  at launch off the main thread), when the text is extracted again, or when
  the book is deleted; a damaged file is logged `READING_TEXT_CACHE_REJECTED`
  and removed. Reopens are about ten times faster with far lower peak memory;
  first opens are unchanged; the text was byte-identical on every measured
  book. A text book that opens without playing prepares the speech of the
  chapter Play will start in (after a cover, the first chapter with words) off
  the main thread, discarded if a rule or speech setting changes first. 38 new
  tests and an opt-in benchmark. Not verified: a real sync client that rewrites
  `content.md`'s extended attributes (one extra parse).

- [x] **Reading-text follow-ups.** October 3. The `open.textBook` baseline
  measures a first open again, forgetting the kept text before each run, beside
  a new `open.textBookCached` baseline (207.6 ms and 11.8 ms), and the baselines
  file was re-recorded with every other number within about one percent of the
  first recording. An EPUB's package metadata is no longer read when the book
  opens: it comes from the book's row when the row keeps every field, and is
  otherwise read off the main thread once the book shows (the 1,447-document
  anthology reopens in 65 ms instead of 112). Play on a chapter over 100,000
  characters whose speech is being prepared in the background waits for it,
  showing Playing, instead of preparing the chapter again on the main thread
  (a 20 MB one-chapter book: 4 s of blocked main thread to 0.1 ms); Play/Pause,
  Stop, the sleep timer's stop, and closing end the wait. 18 new tests.

- [x] **A smaller app (Q09).** October 3. The Release app fell from 32.7 MB to
  11.0 MB. Two thirds of the old size was waste: no scheme is committed, so Xcode's
  generated scheme compiled the app and its packages with code coverage
  instrumentation (13.5 MB and slower code), and the symbol tables (16.9 MB) were
  never stripped. `make build` now passes `CLANG_COVERAGE_MAPPING=NO`, the Release
  app's symbols are stripped into its dSYM, unreachable code is dead-stripped
  (57 KB; Swift's metadata keeps most code reachable), `-Osize` was measured and
  not applied (0.8 MB saved for 5 to 8 percent slower opens and launches, 41 to
  47 percent slower on two text loops), `make check` reports the app's size and
  fails above 50 MB (`APP_SIZE_LIMIT_MB`, one place), and `make install` keeps the
  installed build's dSYM so its crash reports can be read with `atos`. What
  remains: the app's own code is 6.3 MB, SwiftSoup 3.0 MB, swift-markdown with
  cmark 0.8 MB, ZIPFoundation 0.2 MB; Release builds are arm64 only (a universal
  app would be about 22 MB). Self-test cases cover the guard and the coverage
  setting.

- [x] **AirPods and media keys taken by the test suite.** October 4, reported by the
  user ("my AirPods aren't controlling playback of the book in my installed version").
  The installed app's command handling was intact; the hosted test suites that the
  agents run inside TextMouth.app share its bundle identifier and, since the October 2
  and 3 playback tests, reported silent test playback as playing, so macOS sent the
  AirPods presses to the test host while the user's paused copy heard nothing (the
  timing matches an agent's suite). The shared Now Playing service now joins the
  system's media session only outside a test host; media commands are accepted on
  whichever thread MediaPlayer calls from through one `nonisolated` handler, so a
  command can never trap in the Swift 6 mode; and only the reader whose item the system
  shows clears Now Playing, so closing a window that never played no longer tells
  macOS the app stopped. `MediaCommandPlaybackTests` drive real readers with the
  commands the user's AirPods send (play, pause, previous track, next track, with a
  sleep-timer fade, an End of heading timer, a chapter prepared in the background,
  Continue to Next Book, a closed second window, and the Bluetooth suppression
  window). 16 new tests. Not verified with AirPods in hand.

- [x] **Export for every book, and Edit Text in the user's own editor (FR05's
  export and editing answers).** October 4. File > Export… (and the context menus,
  and an Export button in the reader toolbar behind a Playback setting) lists what a

  or its tracks, numbered in play order and named for their chapters), Original file,
  Text as Markdown, Text as plain text, Original recording, Transcript; copies the
  chosen kinds to Documents or a chosen folder, never replacing a file, never
  downloading, with the data directory refused and one announcement at the end.
  Edit Text in Default Editor and Edit Text With… (a reader's new Book menu, a text
  book's context menu) open content.md in the user's editor after a one-time
  explanation; a saved edit is taken within a moment or on activation, re-indexed
  with new fingerprints at the current cache format, the kept reading text dropped,
  open readers shown the new text at the same words, with "Text updated" spoken. An
  edit TextMouth cannot take (not UTF-8, no chapter with text, the source changed
  too, a record that cannot take it) is kept beside content.md as
  `content.md.edited-<time>` and announced, the book held until a taken edit, and
  every rebuild path refuses to write over it. 48 new tests. Not verified with real
  editors. Open: edits saved while TextMouth is closed are replaced without a copy;
  an audiobook's transcript is not editable yet (the transcription UI task).

- [x] **Reader polish (October 4 evening).** `ReaderView`'s body is split into parts of
  its own (its type-check time 1,337 ms to 7 ms; board #829 closed); Where Am I on a

- [x] **Feed search and polish (October 4 evening).** Add Feed searches Apple's podcast

- [x] **Duplicate sheet controls (October 6).** At the user's request, the controls that
  sat twice before VoiceOver in the app's sheets are gone: the sleep timer sheet's End
  of current heading button, which ended the New Timer section right above Stop At's End

  does (and shared All's tag in a book with level-6 headings); and the Book Information
  sheet's header Close, which only dismissed, as Cancel does with Escape. Four read-only

  audio editor) found those two and otherwise deliberate pairings and bugs of other
  kinds, listed in the October 3 follow-ups paragraph. 4 new tests, one of them (the
  hosted Book Information sheet) skipped where SwiftUI exposes no buttons to
  accessibility, as the sleep timer sheet's rendered test is.
