- [x] **One place-based sleep timer: End of heading (October 6 evening).**
  `feature/sleep-timer-end-of-heading`, six commits, at the user's request ("I like the
  End of Heading wording better … I really want those two things to be under one concept
  called Headings to not just apply to normal books but to any document"): End of
  chapter folded into End of heading, and the sheet's Stop At offers End of heading and
  End of book. A heading is one concept for any document, where the next heading of any
  level begins (as Next Heading finds it) or the book ends: EPUB and recovered
  navigation labels, Markdown, HTML, plain text, DAISY, and an audiobook's chapters,
  tracks, and chapter markers. The two timers differed in three cases, now decided the
  heading's way: a chapter with a second heading stops at that heading, not the
  chapter's end; a next chapter that opens with its heading stops at the chapter's end
  with the audiobook fade, as before; a next chapter with text before its first heading,
  or none, plays on under the heading. A stop at the heading's end says "Sleep timer
  expired at the end of the heading. Playback paused." wherever it comes; every saved
  kind (endOfHeading, endOfChapter, endOfDocument) restores End of heading. New tests
  for a second heading in a chapter, a chapter entered under its heading, a book with no
  headings, a Markdown level-2 heading, the audiobook fade, and a real chaptered m4b
  stopping at its marker. Not verified: DAISY and segmented audiobooks have no timer
  test of their own; an m4b's marker inside one file stops the timer a moment past the
  marker and without the fade, since the file, not the chapter, reports its time left.

- [x] **The feed sheets' New Folder fields and the Return key (October 6 evening).**
  `fix/feed-sheets-new-folder-return`, three commits from the library agent's finding
  (#1205): Add Feed's Subscribe and Import OPML's Import stand down while the folder
  picker's New Folder fields are shown, following its `isMakingFolder` binding, so
  Return presses Create alone, and nothing until a name is typed (before, Return with
  the fields open and no name typed reached Subscribe or Import); Subscribe takes Return
  back when a page naming several feeds replaces the picker (the picker's disappearance
  resets the state); Done keeps Return once an import has ended; and Feed Info, which
  has no Return-key button, is pinned as leaving Return to its number fields, which
  enter the number typed. 5 hosted tests, synchronous, turning the run loop through a
  Task since an async test's run loop cannot run the main actor's work. Not verified:
  which button draws as the default in the live app, and live VoiceOver.

- [x] **Audio levels measured off the cooperative pool (October 6 evening).**
  `fix/audio-levels-off-the-pool`, two commits from the editor agent's finding (#1214):
  the audio editor's level measurement (`AudioEditorLevels.measure`) reads its
  composition on a thread of its own (`AudioEditorLevels.Reading`), bridged to its task
  by a cancellation handler that only raises a flag, since AVFoundation forbids
  cancelling a reader while it is read, so a cooperative-pool thread never waits in
  `copyNextSampleBuffer`; a cancelled measurement ends within a second with
  `CancellationError`; and a newer measurement waits for the older one to stop before it
  reads, one at a time per editor. A test reproduced the hang first against the old loop
  (eight pool threads blocked, the host killed), then the fix: more held measurements
  than cores leave the pool free, a cancellation during a twelve-hour read ends within a
  second, five started at once all end. 5 new tests; the unchecked-Sendable pin rose to
  26 for the reading object. Not verified: why CoreMedia never returns a blocked reader
  when the pool is starved; a reader that never returned would now cost one thread and
  fail its test rather than hang the host. Found, not fixed: `AudioEditRenderer.drain`
  has the same shape on the save and export path (board #1252).

- [x] **EPUB authors and a versioned metadata refresh** (`fix/epub-author-metadata`,
  merged 3d52c08b, October 7): 165 of the user's 729 EPUBs were listed under Unknown. A
  read-only pass over their package documents found 150 EPUB 3 packages naming the
  author only in a `<meta property="dcterms:creator">` element, which `parseOPF` and
  `readMetadata` never read (they took the first `dc:creator` or nothing); 7 rows that
  are not EPUBs at all (ZIPs of HTML with no container.xml, or whose container names a
  missing OPF); 4 with calibre's literal `Unknown` creator; and 4 naming no author
  anywhere. `EPUBParser.authorName(in:)` now reads the creator with the author role
  (EPUB 2 `opf:role`, EPUB 3 a refining meta), else the first named creator, else a
  dcterms:creator meta element, else an author-role contributor, trimming the name and
  reading a literal `Unknown` as no one; both entry points share it, and an edited
  author is written into that creator or a new one. The text cache carries no author, so
  a cache version bump would have reread every book's text and fixed no row:
  `ImportedFileMetadata.readerVersion` ("1.0.0") is now stamped on each imported row
  (`Book.metadataReaderVersion`, on the row and, with the book's next save, in its
  record), `SemanticVersion` holds the comparison the cache check already made, and
  `LibraryViewModel.refreshOutdatedMetadata` runs at launch before the text cache batch,
  reading every older row's file again off the main thread with the import's reader and
  replacing only a placeholder author with a name, so an author typed by hand stays; a
  book in the cloud is left unstamped and counted (`MaintenancePass.metadataRefresh`),
  and each read is `StorageWork.metadataRefresh`, which a data-directory move waits for.
  `Book.unknownAuthor` names the placeholder once. 24 new tests, and the every-field
  fixture of `ContentAnalysisMergeTests` carries the stamp, since a book rebuilt from
  its record must have every field. Not verified: the pass against the live library,
  which no test reads (the same read-only pass predicts 150 authors found); the DOCX,
  PDF, DAISY, and audio readers under the refresh. Found, not fixed: the 7 non-EPUB
  rows, the 4 literal-Unknown rows, and the 4 authorless books stay under Unknown, which
  is right for the last two. README and ARCHITECTURE were updated on the branch, and the
  agent notes record what the library held and what stays unverified.

- [x] **The Unopened category in place of No Folder** (`feature/unopened-category`,

- [x] **Speech guarded from the engine's markup parser, and recovery from a refused
  utterance** (`fix/speech-markup-guard`, merged aec5c91b, October 7, B01):
  `SpeechMarkupGuard.neutralized(_:)` turns the brackets of every tag-shaped run into
  U+2039 and U+203A, one UTF-16 unit for one, so guarded text keeps its length and every
  offset; every utterance made from book text (direct chunks and fade segments, the
  engine render, Where Am I) passes through it, and math and code with bare words stay
  as they are, since the engine speaks "x < y" but swallows text after `< y >`, `<5>`,
  and `x << 2 >> 1`, all probed offline. `SpeechService` answers an unexpected cancel of
  its live utterance by replacing `directSynth` and retrying once per offset
  (`DIRECT_SYNTH_REPLACED`, `DIRECT_RETRY`); a repeat ends speech stopped at the cursor
  with "Speech stopped: the Mac's speech engine refused this text."
  (`DIRECT_RETRY_FAILED`, `onSpeechRefused`). `AudioEngineService.render` gives its
  synthesizer a `RenderCancellationObserver` so a refusal wakes the render wait at once
  (`RENDER_REFUSED`) instead of after 120 seconds. The unchecked-Sendable pin rose to
  27. Not verified: the engine's behavior with premium or Siri voices (only Samantha
  compact was probed); Where Am I's guarded line has no test. Open: the reader does not
  yet take `onSpeechRefused`, so after a refusal Now Playing still shows the book
  playing and the listening session stays open, crediting listening time, until the
  next press (board #1308; the Play/Pause button itself follows the service); `WhereAmIDelegateHelper` hears only `didFinish` (#1296).

  fine words (`FineWords`, `BookWords`, `TextPlace`: one word rule for every later branch, each word keyed

  composed-character boundaries, with the place's share of the book counted as `Book.restsAtEnd` counts a
  place, the versions it was measured in, and a format version, every field read leniently;
  `stands(at:in:)` is the direct check); and `TextAnchorFinder` (pure, `Sendable`), returning where each
  place goes and how it was found (unchanged, found, or a best guess with its reason): the end-of-book
  rule, the direct check, candidates where four words after or before the place stand (2 per word after, 1
  per word before), ties and locality as silent best guesses, the tolerant second search, and the last
  guess; `findHeading` finds a skipped heading by level, label, and words, or returns nil so the skip
  waits. Then `ReadingPosition.anchor`, `Bookmark.anchor`, and `SkippedHeading` with `Book.skippedHeadings`
  and `BookState.skippedHeadings`, written only when set (a book with neither writes exactly what it wrote
  before, pinned), kept in step with `skippedHeadingIDs`, which the reader still saves and older builds
  read. Checked against the addendum's marker truth by compiling the app's own engine files in a
  standalone harness: of 57,472 real places, 18,113 passed the direct check, 39,265 were found by their

  and none a few words off; 732 of 735 skipped headings found, and 3 more by the guess; 22 rules broken
  one at a time were each caught by the tests. Nine rules differ from the reference, each for a reason in
  the agent's notes (a place inside a word or a composed character, ties broken the same way every run, a
  place with no words after it, the end of the book, a skipped heading's label, the last guess without old
  text). Timings in an optimized standalone build: pride.txt's words read in 6.1 ms and indexed in 2.7 ms,
  1.1 µs a direct check, 0.058 ms a search. Nothing captures or moves a place yet, so no behavior changes.
  Agent `anchorengine`; its harness is kept with the other T06 programs in
  `~/programming/ai/claude/investigate/text-anchors-2026-10-10/engine-harness/`.

  (`fix/where-am-i-and-package-moves`, merged b985d72a, October 10): REV13 app findings 1 and 2 and the

  without pausing what waited and calls `startPlayHeldByWhereAmI()`, so a held Play, a held chapter, and a
  play waiting for its chapter's speech all start (a chapter still downloading goes on waiting), while the

  survives the cancel. Audio set aside for an off-main package move counts as playing

  Where Am I? pressed mid-move holds it (`resumeAudioAfterPackageMove()` holds it as
  `isChapterHeldByWhereAmI`), and a pause, Play/Pause (which used to play from the moving files), the sleep
  timer's end, a recording or another book starting, the AirPods leaving, a stop, or Release Media Controls
  during the move takes it, so the move's end leaves the book as the listener left it. Play/Pause during
  Where Am I? pauses a play still waiting to start, silently. Where Am I?'s resume plays the book afresh
  when `restartPlaybackBackendOnNextResume` is set (AirPods connected, or a paused audiobook's files moved
  during it), through one shared `restartPlaybackAtCursor()`, a text book always in direct speech. A Play
  in the moment Where Am I?'s end sound plays with a chapter held changes nothing: the chapter starts once,
  with the end sound only. The full-summary and speech-preparation tests pass the end sound themselves.
  Three checks were already fixed on main (a chapter download during Where Am I? held, bb12644e; the pause
  sound only when something paused, 0d5e1249; Release Media Controls in a window holding nothing says so,
  d38c3ea8). Side effects: a Pause during Where Am I? over a waiting play now makes the pause sound, and
  Play/Pause in the moment after the announcement with a chapter held pauses silently. Agent
  `whereamimoves`. Not verified: real AirPods, VoiceOver, braille; a text book on rendered audio (test
  chapters are too long to render); engine cancels and off-main moves are stood in for in the tests.

- [x] **T06's branch C: the diff relocator** (`feature/place-diff`, merged 20bc9d76, October 10):
  `PatienceDiff` and `PlaceRelocator` (pure, `Sendable`) move a book's position, bookmarks, and skipped
  headings from its old text (the old content.md as this build reads it) to its new one: a place whose
  anchor still stands, or that marks the end, stays (`TextAnchorFinder.settled`); otherwise it is found in
  the old text at its numbers or by its words, carried by the diff's rules (solid matches, the words that
  follow it, like-for-like stretches, removals slid back, moved text followed, the end to the end), else
  given to the saved-words search, then a silent guess; a skipped heading lands on the heading of its level
  where its own text lands, else is found by level, label, and words, else waits, and after a removal only
  a heading with its label takes the skip. `relocate(_:)` returns the places to save (anchors captured
  anew), how each was found, and the counts for `PLACES_RELOCATED` (", waiting N" when a skip waits). Over
  the addendum's exact truth, compiled unchanged in a standalone harness, it matches the measured program
  in all 106 comparisons: 57,472 real places all on their own words; 161 of 222,037 synthetic places on
  wrong words, 142 of them in moved paragraphs (the addendum's 146 was for the first note's words; its
  figure for fine words is 161); skipped headings 733 found and 2 guessed of 735 real, all 3,513 synthetic
  right; 40 rules broken one at a time were each caught by the tests. About 20 ms for a 750,000-unit novel
  and 100 to 130 ms for 3.8 million units, then about 2 µs a place, holding 24 to 126 MB. Nothing calls it
  yet (branch D wires it into `writeCache`, branch E into Edit Text). Agent `placediff`; its harness and the
  readings it needs are kept in `~/programming/ai/claude/investigate/text-anchors-2026-10-10/placediff-harness/`.

  but V16 and V23 (below) are fixed, each pinned by a test. A skipped heading is now found among the old

  `afconvert`, Sonnet at effort high, reviewed by the orchestrator): at the user's first launch of B06 (20:17) all four
  conversions froze about 30 s in, Apple's Vorbis decoder spinning inside AVAssetReader in TextMouth's own process
  (`copyNextSampleBuffer` → `ACSpanningInputBuffer::checkRelocateClientPacketDescriptions`, the afternoon's bug);
  the user force-quit. The conversion now runs `/usr/bin/afconvert` in a child process under `WatchedProcess`, killed
  at 60 s plus a tenth of the track, 1 GB resident, or 20 s without output growth (`OGG_CONVERSION_KILLED`);
  TextMouth reads only Ogg headers and refuses to decode Ogg elsewhere (the probe, the audio editor, transcription); a
  failed book waits for the next launch. A synthetic 20-minute track converts in 5.75 s at a 14 MB peak. The merged
  tree is the tested branch's tree.

  cancel race, `WatchedProcess.terminateAll()` runs at quit and before the guard's exit, and `OrphanedConversionSweep`
  kills at launch only an orphaned `/usr/bin/afconvert` of the current user writing a hidden `.ogg-conversion.m4a`;
  **(a5)** the watchdog times by system uptime; **(a6)** a folder book's index is checked before any track converts,
  and a failed commit discards the conversions; **(a7)** the temporary name is the UUID alone. Two memory-guard tests

- [x] **Export with conversion: Convert to HTML, RTF, and Word 97-2003** (`team/export`,
  merged 0db62a38, October 19; Opus lead `export-lead` with seven Sonnet workers and a review round, charter
  `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-19/team-export.md`).
  - **FMT18, the picker.** The Export dialog gains a Convert to picker beside "Text as Markdown", offered only while
    that box is checked. Its choices are Markdown (the default, the same file as before), HTML, RTF, and
    "Word 97-2003 (.doc)". A job runner (`BookExportConversion`) converts each book off the main thread under the
    dialog's progress and Stop. A book that fails is named, and the others still export.
  - **Markdown stays the base.** `ExportDocumentWalker` walks each book's content.md once with swift-markdown into
    `ExportDocument`, the model every writer reads. It holds headings with unique ASCII ids, paragraphs as runs, lists
    up to 8 levels deep, quotes, code, tables, and images as their alternative text. It leaves out content.md's
    chapter markers, its navigation annotations, and raw HTML.
  - **The writer interface.** `TextExportWriter` is the writer protocol, registered in `TextExportWriters.all`,
    which team braille's BRF and PEF writers join (`Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-19/export/interface-for-braille.md`).
  - **HTML.** `HTMLExportWriter` writes escaped XHTML through `XMLEscaping`, which also leaves out the characters
    XML 1.0 forbids. The page has a contents nav and `lang`, and links only http, https, and mailto addresses.
  - **FMT19, RTF and Word.** `RTFExportWriter` writes a style sheet whose headings carry `\outlinelevel`, and they
    come back at the same levels through `TextutilDocumentReader`. `WordDocExportWriter` makes .doc from that RTF with
    Apple's writer; its headings are plain text in Word, as its hint says.
  - **File safety.** Every exported file, whether copied, plain text, or converted, is now made in a scratch folder
    on the target's volume and moved in with `renamex_np` and `RENAME_EXCL`, or with an exclusive copy across volumes.
    An export therefore never replaces or removes a file in the chosen folder.
  - **The review round.** Two Opus reviewers' 12 findings were dealt with before the merge, 11 of them fixed:
    - a list item's text came out of reading order;
    - tests would have turned red as soon as braille's writers joined the registry;
    - a failed write could delete a file another export had just written (true on main before);
    - a converted checkbox gave a size 2 to 3 times too small (it now gives no size);
    - a title starting with dots could make a hidden file name.

  export merge on the user's, architecture, and developer pages, checked against the code. The Exporting books page gains
  the Convert to menu, the converted checkbox's name, and what HTML, RTF, and Word 97-2003 keep and lose. It says every
  exported file is made in a scratch folder and moved in whole, never over a file already there, and that Stop keeps no
  half-made file. The sentences the merge made false are corrected: README said Export only copies, and Stop was said
  to end an export before its next file.

- [x] **Braille: liblouis with four English tables, reading BRF, BRL, and PEF, and writing BRF and PEF** (`team/braille`,
  merged 3996434d, October 20; Opus lead `braille-lead` with fourteen Sonnet workers, an Opus finisher after a session
  restart, and two review rounds by three Opus reviewers; charter `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-19/team-braille.md`).
  - **FMT21, liblouis.** liblouis 3.39.0 is vendored unchanged in `Vendor/liblouis`, with hand-written `config.h` and
    `include/liblouis.h` for UCS-4. It runs only in `TextMouthBraille`, a C helper embedded in the app with three modes
    (check, forward, and back), under `WatchedProcess` limits, through `BrailleTranslator`, its only caller. Braille
    travels as Unicode cells. The helper loads a table by its bare name from inside the bundle's tables folder, and
    only the tables the catalog offers.
  - **Tables and the setting.** Four English tables are bundled: UEB and EBAE, each uncontracted and contracted, offered
    from their own metadata (`BrailleTableCatalog`). Settings gains a Braille tab with "Braille code" and "Contraction"
    (default UEB contracted), mirrored in config.json as `brailleTable`. The user keeps English as the default
    (question 1 of `humanQuestions/2026-10-19.md`).
  - **FMT20, reading.** `BrailleFileReader` reads BRF and BRL (braille ASCII in either case) and six-dot PEF into pages
    of cells kept in one buffer. It refuses files over 32 MB, 100,000 pages, or 4,000,000 lines. `BraillePageAnalyzer`
    finds:
    - centered headings, at level 1, read whole across lines;
    - cell-5 and cell-7 headings, at levels 2 and 3;
    - paragraphs, and list items with their runovers;
    - page numbers, which it leaves out of the text.

    `BrailleBookReader` translates the text to print into the Markdown cache. A braille book is `BookFormat.braille`, and
    keeps its import table in `Book.brailleTable`, through Remove from Library Only too. Book Information and Speak Info
    name its code.
  - **FMT22, writing.** `BrailleLayout` lays print out in BANA pages, 40 cells by 25 lines by default:
    - headings centered, or at cells 5 and 7;
    - paragraphs in cells 3 and 1;
    - lists in cells 1 and 3, with a blank line before and after;
    - the braille page number at the right of the last line.

    `BrailleFileWriter` writes BRF (with CR LF or LF, and a form feed between pages) and PEF, both in six-dot cells only.
    Export's Convert to offers BRF and PEF. Their options start from the Braille setting: the code, the contraction,
    cells per line (18 to 80), lines per page (4 to 50), page numbers, and, for BRF, line ends.
  - **Size.** The app grows by about 0.6 MB, to about 19.75 MB as `make build` makes it, export merge included: the
    helper is 152 KB, the tables 286 KB, and the licences 68 KB. Every literary table would make it about 30.5 MB, and
    all 481 tables about 33.2 MB.
  - **Licences.** liblouis's and LAME's licence files now ship in `Contents/Resources/Licenses`.
  - **Older builds.** A TextMouth built before this merge cannot open a library that holds a braille book. It keeps the
    library's file and writes nothing.
  - **The review rounds.** Two Opus reviewers made 18 findings and a third made 8, and all were fixed. Among them:
    - forward braille silently cut short at an escape;
    - long cell-5 and cell-7 headings, then multi-line centered titles, misread;
    - a book's code lost after Remove from Library Only;
    - the reader's memory at about twenty times a file's size;
    - a PEF holding an eight-dot cell that TextMouth itself would refuse;
    - a comma in the app's path breaking every table;
    - tests reading the user's real Braille setting;
    - a hosted test that would have failed on GitHub Actions;
    - lists read back as broken paragraphs, with invented headings.

- [x] **Braille tables from this Mac** (`team/mactables`, merged b5fffa42, October 20; Opus agent `mactables` with an Opus

    braille options (`BrailleTablePickers`). English stays built in and the default.
  - **The Mac's tables.** Every other language offers the literary tables VoiceOver carries on this Mac (liblouis 3.34.0,
    `/System/Library/ScreenReader/BrailleTables/LiblouisBrailleTranslator.brailletable`). They are read in place, with
    their headers loaded once at launch off the main thread. About 107 tables in 76 languages are usable on macOS 26.3.
  - **The check.** Each Mac table is checked before its first use (`MacBrailleTableCheck`): the helper loads it,

    eight-dot braille, or needs more is refused in a sentence. Refused tables include Greek, Portuguese contracted,
    Icelandic, Serbian, Hungarian, and the Hong Kong and Taiwanese Chinese tables.

    and the removal marker. A book records the Mac's liblouis version, so a table a macOS update changes is refused by
    name and the book keeps its text.
  - **Writers.** BRF and PEF exports now fail by name on an eight-dot cell, instead of dropping dots 7 and 8.
  - **The review round.** An Opus reviewer's eight findings were fixed, among them:
    - Hungarian tables that passed the check but exceeded the helper's limits on real books;
    - no record of the Mac's table version;
    - 485 headers read on the main thread;
    - Export showing the wrong code for an unavailable table, or using the old table during a check.

- [x] **A book moved while it opens loads from where it is now** (`fix/reader-load-race`, merged 825a3f23, October 21; Opus
  agent `racefix` with an Opus review round; brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-21/racefix.md`). The user asked:
  "Please investigate the failing tests / send a team to see if its a real problem and fix it."
  - **The race was real.** A reader reads its book on a background queue, while a library move (Move to Folder…, Rename
    Folder…, Move Folder…, Move to Library…) moves the package off the main thread and only then hands readers the new
    path. A read in between found nothing and called the book damaged ("The file “Tracks” in this audiobook is damaged
    or incomplete…"), as `ReaderRefreshTests.testABookMovedWhileItLoadsOpensFromWhereItIsNow` caught once on October 20.
  - **The fix.**
    - `LibraryBookUpdates.isPackageMoving` notes each moving package, and `beginReadingForLoad()` reads nothing of it
      until the move is over.
    - A failure that met a move loads the book again (`READER_LOAD_AFTER_MOVE`), even when the move is put back.
    - A cloud library's state check goes through the same move check.
    - A case-only folder rename tells readers only once it holds the storage.
    - A book that really is damaged still says so, in the same words.
  - **Tests.** A test seam holds the load at the racing moment, with no sleeps. Each new test fails without its fix: 4
    of 34 first, then 3 and 2 of 77 in the review round.

  `humanQuestions/2026-10-19.md`, answered: "For the italix yes it should try keeping all text formatting extractable
  from the braille converted to the intermediate format."
  - **Protocol 2.** The TextMouthBraille helper speaks protocol 2, defined once in
    `TextMouthBraille/include/TextMouthBrailleProtocol.h`. Each paragraph travels with a line of emphasis codes, and a

  - **Reading.** Reading braille keeps the emphasis liblouis reads back exactly, as `*…*`, `**…**`, or `***…***` in

    as a double quote), which is pinned by name. EBAE's emphasis is not read back.
  - **The review round.** An Opus review's three findings were fixed:

    - EBAE and Mac tables writing emphasis that changed words on reading back;

- [x] **Pages across formats, as a navigation unit** (`team/pages`, merged 77e798a0, October 21; Opus lead `pages-lead` with
  eight Sonnet workers, an Opus review, and an Opus fix agent; charter `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-21/team-pages.md`,
  design `PAGES-RESEARCH-2026-10-21.md`).
  - **PG01, the marker.** `<!-- page: LABEL -->` in content.md (`MarkdownPageMarker`, placed only through
    `MarkdownMarkerWriter`, so it never begins a line). It adds no reading text, so no saved place moves. Pages are
    recorded at each page's first whole word (`BookPages`, `PagePosition`), and `ReadingTextCache` layout 2 keeps them.
  - **PG02 to PG04, navigation.**
    - Page is a navigation unit, with Next Page (Option-Command-Right Arrow), Previous Page (Option-Command-Left Arrow),
      and Go to Page… (Option-Command-G).
    - The reader screen's "Page 12 of 300" button opens the page field.
    - Where Am I? says the page after the heading number.
    - Two Playback settings, both on: "Show the page on the reader screen" and "Say the page in Where Am I?".
  - **PG05 to PG08 and PG11, the formats.** EPUB page lists and page-break elements, PDF pages with their own labels,
    DOCX page breaks with section numbering, and DAISY page numbers (no longer read aloud as "[Page N]"). DOC, RTF,
    ODT, and WordML get pages through a second textutil run aligned with its plain text, which also mends the words
    textutil ran together at a break (B13).
  - **B12.** A finished Edit Text change is recorded (`Book.editedText`), so an automatic re-read keeps it and logs
    `TEXT_EDIT_KEPT`.
  - **PG09 is built but switched off** (`PageReaderVersions.launchRereadIsOn = false`). The Opus review found its launch
    re-read of existing books unsafe for the live library (see PG16). Until then only imports and Redo Text Extraction
    give pages.
  - **The fix agent** fixed the review's findings that touched new imports:
    - a marker beginning a line, which lost words in DAISY list items and DOCX;
    - a page at a chapter's end looping Next Page;
    - a double space after an empty EPUB page anchor.

- [x] **The October 21 documentation pass** (`docs/oct21`, merged 5c8a2a07, October 21; agent `docs21`, Sonnet): pages, braille
  emphasis, B12, and the reader-load race on the user's, architecture, and developer pages, checked against the code.
  The Page unit, Next Page, Previous Page, Go to Page…, the status row, Where Am I?, the Pages settings, page markers in
  Edit Text, which formats have pages, existing books gaining pages only through Redo Text Extraction for now, kept

- [x] **Existing books gain their pages at the next launch, safely** (`pages/pg16`, merged 5f38016b, October 21; Opus agent
  `pg16` with two Opus reviews; brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-21/pg16.md`). The user: "Go ahead an make it safe,
  I don't think I've made any edits and no one else has used the app so I think its safe".
  - **It is on.** PG09's launch re-read is switched on (`PageReaderVersions.launchRereadIsOn = true`).
  - **How each book is decided.** A book's content.md is compared byte for byte with its file read again without pages:
    - if equal, it is read again with pages, its places relocated;
    - if equal to the read with pages, it is TextMouth's own, from a stopped batch, and is only stamped;
    - if it differs and no edit is on record, it is first copied beside itself as `content.md.edited-<time>`, logged
      as `PAGES_REPLACED_TEXT`, and then read again with pages;
    - if its Edit Text change is recorded (B12), it keeps its text, and the newer of the row's and the record's edit
      record wins.
  - **Failures.** A failed read or page run is tried at one more launch (`+failed-once`, then `+failed`), a missing file
    is noted once, and `PAGES_UPGRADE_SUMMARY` sums up the batch.
  - **Pins.** Reading without pages reproduces the pre-page golden digests for navigation.epub, alice.docx,
    test_book.docx, lathe_of_heaven, and wipo.
  - **The suite.** The merge's own run had one load-sensitive failure, `BookSearchModelTests.testCancelledSearchPublishesNothing`.
    The version merge's run, which includes this merge, was green.

  there should probabbly be a procedure for that in the beta versions anyway".

  - **The script.** `Scripts/set-version.sh` (and `make set-version VERSION=x.y.z`) sets `MARKETING_VERSION` and
    regenerates the project, refusing a version that is not later. Its selftest is in `make check-selftest`.
  - **The procedure.** It is in `docs/developer/building.md` ("Versions") and in `AGENTS.md`:
    - the minor number goes up for a feature set, and the patch number for a fix-only install;
    - the build number stays the UTC time stamp;
    - the orchestrator bumps the version before the user's next install, and adds a section to the user's new
      `docs/user/whats-new.md`.

The feed's rules wait while an episode's text is open in an editor, and a re-download keeps a save no session took as an `.edited-` copy.
  - **Search.** Search in Book (Command-F) searches a transcribed audiobook's transcript, each result giving the time it is heard. Choosing one moves the audio there, and Go Back (Command-[) returns.
  - **Escaping.** Export writes Text as Markdown and a transcript's Markdown afresh (`MarkdownExportWriter`), escaping only what Markdown needs. A transcript's content.md escapes only what the reader needs (transcript layer 1.1.0, written again once per book as it opens).
  - **Retranscribe Audio…** names the command once a book has a transcript.
  - **Bugs fixed:**
    - edit checks racing a cleanup, a deletion, or a new transcription;
    - edits taken outside the transcript's sentences;
    - sessions left ended after a failed change;
    - overflow on damaged files;
    - cloud reads and uncopied rewrites;
    - a cleanup that empties a sentence;
    - a retranscription of a book only in iCloud thrown away when it finished;
    - the 1.1.0 rewrite taken for the user's edit;
    - email autolinks and a `!` before a link in exported Markdown.

During the beta the version is 0.MINOR.PATCH: PATCH goes up by one for each day of fixes or small backward-compatible features, and MINOR, with PATCH back to 0, for anything an older TextMouth cannot use safely; each bump adds a dated changelog section (`docs/developer/building.md`, "Versions"; AGENTS.md). `make bump-patch` and `make bump-minor` (set-version.sh `--patch`, `--minor`, 87 selftest cases).

- [x] **Abandoned audio reads, and transcript writes that fail midway** (`fix/polish020b`, merged b2e44b3c, October 23; Opus agent `polish020b`; brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-23/polish020b.md`), with the policy pin's fix (`fix/unchecked-pin`, merged 9ab48e49).
  - **Abandoned reads.** A file whose audio read passed its time limit is remembered until TextMouth quits, by its path, size, and date (`AsyncBridge.AbandonedReads`), and every later read of it is refused at once without starting. A file that hangs Apple's decoders therefore cannot pile up stuck reads, the October 14 failure.
  - **Failed installs.** A transcript install that fails after writing transcript.json puts back the transcript and its text as they were, leaving alone a file the user saved meanwhile.
  - **The suites.** The merge's suite failed one test, `UncheckedConcurrencyPolicyTests`' pin, since `AbandonedReads` is a new lock-confined `@unchecked Sendable`. The pin went from 27 to 28 in `fix/unchecked-pin`, whose suite was green.

  - **Reports:** in `build/orchestrator/`: `review-*`, `verify-*`, `*-fix-report.md`, `*-fix2-report.md`.
  - **A rule broken:** the pages reviewer's prompt lacked the live-library rule. It read library.json and copied two plain text books to /tmp; the copies are deleted, and every prompt now carries the rule.

- [x] **Text files in other encodings (FMT16)** (`formats/encodings`, merged 1cf19935 on `integrate/fmt16`, then fast-forwarded to main, October 24; Sonnet agent `fmt16`, task E of `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-24/tasks.md`; docs `docs/fmt16`).
  - **The guess.** At the import of a user's file, TextMouth guesses the encoding of a plain text, Markdown, or HTML file that is neither UTF-8 nor UTF-16 and names none, choosing among the encodings `TextEncodingCatalog` names.
    - It guesses only when `TextPlausibility` proves the Windows-1252 reading wrong (`isGarbled`), and the whole file reads back byte for byte.
    - Ties are broken the same way on every launch, and ISO-2022-JP is found by its structure.
  - **The record.** `Book.textEncoding` is kept in the row, bookInfo.json, and the removal marker.
    - Every later reading uses it (`recordedTextEncoding()`, `MarkdownCacheService.encodingPolicy(of:)`).
    - A damaged value reads as none.
    - A recovered book takes it only from its own record (2f84257a, the final review's one-line check).
  - **The wording.** Book Information shows "Text Encoding, Windows-1251 (Cyrillic), guessed", and Speak Info says "Text encoding: Windows-1251 (Cyrillic), guessed."
  - **Three review rounds:**
    - The first review found Western text guessed away (452 of 5,000 fuzz texts) and ties broken by the hash seed.
    - Round one fixed both, but German camelCase capital umlauts then went to Mac Roman.
    - Round two fixed that (213 to 2 of a broad set of 14,230 Western texts), kept the restored encoding on the row, and stopped wrong guesses for short Hebrew.
    - The user asked for the final check that day ("Just do the check now"). It found the branch ready to merge after the book-ID check.
  - **What it costs.** These are left garbled, not made wrong:
    - files of identifiers with accented capitals;
    - one-line Cyrillic notes without ы, ь, or я;
    - short texts in Hungarian, Croatian, Slovenian, and Latvian.

    An older TextMouth drops the recorded encoding.

- [x] **Choose a book's text encoding in Book Information (FMT16's Reopen with Encoding)** (`formats/encoding-choice`, merged 7b5375b9 and 0b1bf8dd on `integrate/encoding-choice`, then fast-forwarded to main, October 25; version 0.2.3; Opus agent `encodingchoice`, brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-24/encodingchoice.md`; docs `docs/encoding-choice`, Sonnet `docs25`).
  - **The request.** The user, October 24: "Go ahead and do the reopening with a different encoding … can it be set in the info screen for a book with the current one seleted in the popup?" It is one control, with no menu command, by the duplicate-controls rule.
  - **The pop-up.** Book Information's Text Encoding row is a pop-up for every plain text, Markdown, or HTML book added from a file.
    - Its options are "Automatic", "UTF-8", then the catalog.
    - The selected item names how its encoding was decided: guessed, chosen, from its byte order mark, declared in the file, or the default.
  - **Choosing an encoding.** A confirmation comes first. Then the file is read again in that encoding, strictly, and a file not valid in it is refused with nothing changed (`LibraryViewModel.readTextAgain`).
    - Places and bookmarks move with their words.
    - An edit is kept as a copy, and Edit Text stops.
    - Open readers, and readers opened meanwhile, get the new text.
    - The book stays busy for the whole re-read. A second re-read, Redo Text Extraction, Edit Text, Replace Existing, and the launch batch all wait.
    - A quit waits up to 10 seconds through the quit hold that feed downloads use, now shared.
    - If anything fails, content.md and contentIndex.json go back exactly.
  - **The record.** `textEncodingSource` ("chosen" or "automatic") is kept in the row, bookInfo.json, and the removal marker. One rule (`TextEncodingRecord.standing`) decides the encoding for saves, recovery, and rebuilds: the record's choice wins.
  - **Byte grammars.** Each multi-byte encoding has one, since Foundation accepts malformed bytes. EUC-JP's JIS X 0212 characters are refused, since Foundation misreads them.
  - **Reviews.** One Opus review found a wrong-text bug (EUC-JP 0x8F), a false "left the book as it was", and three risks. Its verification after the first fix round found a quit window and three writers that missed the busy list. Both rounds were fixed.

- [x] **The October 30 night: customizable Library views, braille file characters, a bug hunt, and a more trustworthy suite** (`team/views` 71f22891, `team/braillecodes` 0b523829, `team/bughunt` d1a1a602, `team/tests` 090bce28, the removal-marker pin 239b0dc8, merged on `integrate/oct30`, then fast-forwarded to main at 3769130e, November 1; version 0.2.4 (6116fbb5); Opus leads `views-lead`, `braillecodes-lead`, `bughunt-lead`, `tests-lead`, mostly Opus workers; Opus reviews, Sonnet verifiers; docs `docs/oct31` (merge 6fabf93f), Sonnet `docs31`; briefs `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-30/`).

  - **Library views** (`team/views`; design `LIBRARY-VIEWS-RESEARCH-2026-10-30.md`; questions in `humanQuestions/2026-10-30.md`, built with their recommended answers).

An Opus review found 9 problems; all fixed and verified. The changelog cautions against opening a library with Eurobraille or Unicode books in an older TextMouth.
  - **The bug hunt** (`team/bughunt`): five Opus reviews (playback, feeds, library, traps, import) and the triage's items; about 40 bugs fixed, each with a test. Its outside reviews found seven more data problems (fixed, `bughunt/datafix`) and that the new HTML depth guard changed the words of deeply nested books, so it was taken off again (`bughunt/revert-htmldepth`): EPUB and HTML read as on main.
  - **The suite** (`team/tests`): eleven load-sensitive tests made deterministic, vacuous tests made to fail, new tests for risky code, and four app bugs those tests found, fixed (an equalizer filter's NaN, MP3 chapter titles cut at a Windows line break, two escapers, a missing data directory's raw error).
  - **How it ran.** All 76 agents of the night ran on Opus, which spent the week fast and hit the 5-hour limit twice; the user then asked for Sonnet. The disk fell to 1 GB several times and the Mac froze under the build load at 12:41 on October 31 and was force-restarted. Builds then ran through the build train (`Scripts/agents/build-train.py`): one scheduled build every 30 minutes pooling every agent's branch.

- [x] **The next book in this window, Next and Previous in Folder, and the sleep timer going on with listening** (`feature/nextbook-window`, merged as aad1fab9, November 1; version 0.2.5 (4d72a43c); Sonnet agent `nextbook`; three Opus reviews and three Sonnet verifications, reports in `build/orchestrator/nextbook/`; docs `docs/nextbook` (merge ff328b9a), Sonnet `docs-nextbook`, with three wording fixes by the orchestrator; design `NEXTBOOK-WINDOW-RESEARCH-2026-11-01.md`; the user's questions 1 to 18 in `humanQuestions/2026-11-01.md`; briefs `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-11-01/`).
  - **The request.** The user, November 1: "I would want it to iether close the old window once the new one was moved to, or even better "rebuild" the original window to be have the new book as the active item viewed ... the second rebuild I think wwould also allow for example go to next/previus episodes in feeds or normal folders ... I would want a global setting for the behavior of moving from book to book for windows and a local one for feeds and folders if the behavior is set to move to another book." About 09:50: "the sleeping timer in particular I would like to move still running to the new book without a new window opening."
  - **The window.** When listening goes on to another book, the reader window shows that book in place: the scene's `$bookID` changes, and `.id(bookID)` gives the same window a new reader. This covers a book's end going on, Next and Previous in Reading List, and the new folder commands.
    - The Playback tab's "Open the next book in" (This Window, the default, or A New Window), `nextBookWindow` in config.json.
    - A feed's own "Open the next episode in" (Feed Info; "article" or "item" for such a feed) and a folder's "Open the next book in" (Folder Settings), each dimmed while that feed or folder stops at an item's end.
    - A book open in another window comes forward, and this window closes. A busy window falls back to a new one (`NextBookWindowRule`).
  - **Next in Folder and Previous in Folder** (Navigate menu, no keys by default). Next goes the way the end of the book would go on (question 9), Previous the other way. An episode not downloaded yet is downloaded first.
  - **The sleep timer goes on with listening.** The shared service holds it for the next book until that book's reader takes it, before anything plays.
    - A book that cannot open cancels it: "Sleep timer cancelled, because the next book could not be opened."
    - A timer that runs out or begins to fade before the take ends listening.
    - Next or Previous during a fade ends the fade and the timer: "Sleep timer cancelled, because you moved to another book while it faded out." (question 13).
    - The last move wins until its book has started (question 17).
  - **How it ran.** One Sonnet agent through six fix rounds: after three Opus reviews (R, N, and V findings), three Sonnet verifications (W, X, Y), and the orchestrator's own reading of the last round. Trains 1101-1530 and 1101-1600 failed only on a test fixture: a 57 ms test's settings save outlived its sandbox, because a move holds its reader for half a second, and the failure sentence landed in the next test. The fixture now writes the save before the sandbox goes (2f3674d2).

- [x] **iOS phase 0: the iOS app target compiles and links, and the Mac app compiles what it did** (`integrate/ios-phase0`, main fast-forwarded to it, November 4; five Sonnet teams, shell, a11y, engine, media, and xml, each a Sonnet lead with Sonnet workers; a Sonnet outside review of each team branch and an Opus review of every Mac change against main; reports in `build/orchestrator/ios0/`; research `IOS-PORT-RESEARCH-2026-11-03.md`; the user's questions in `humanQuestions/2026-11-03.md`; briefs `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-11-03/`).
  - **The request.** The user, the evening of November 3: "please try spending the night with well reviewed sonnet agent teams and the pooled contribution scheduled builds to try conducting the first phase and fixing all IOS errors without changing MacOS behavior if possible."
  - **The iOS target.** `TextMouthiOS` in project.yml: the same sources as the Mac app, less `TextMouth/Platform/macOS/` and the Mac's resources, with module name `TextMouth`, for iOS 26, with iOS builds of LAME and the exception catcher, and one placeholder screen. Xcode builds it once its iOS platform is downloaded. Until then `make typecheck-ios` compiles and links it with SwiftPM and the iOS SDK alone: BUILD SUCCEEDED, 0 errors, 0 warnings, where the census of the evening found 817 errors in 99 files.
  - **The Mac unchanged.** Every change to what the Mac compiles is one of these:
    - a move, unchanged, into `Platform/macOS`;
    - a `#if os(macOS)` wrapper around the old text;
    - an alias that is exactly the old type or expression on the Mac (`PlatformWindow`, `PlatformFont`, `PlatformView`, `PlatformApplication`, `checkboxToggleStyle()`, `radioGroupPickerStyle()`);
    - a declaration split out unchanged.

    `make mac-projection BASE=main`: 105 Swift files changed, 92 unchanged for the Mac. The other 13 are:
    - new shared code that only tests and aliases use;
    - the imports and extension lines of three files split out unchanged (FrontWindowKind, WindowAccessor, and AudioEditorLauncher+Describe);
    - four closures reflowed around a `#if`.

    The final review confirmed each. The version line became a setting group and the two C libraries became templates; every Mac target's settings are as before.
  - **The tools.**
    - `make typecheck-ios` (`CENSUS=1` counts every AppKit use left), `make typecheck-mac` (the Mac compiled and linked in about 96 seconds, without xcodebuild), and `make mac-projection`.
    - `Scripts/agents/heavy_lock.py`, one heavy job at a time, which the build train takes too.
    - Each train result now counts the iOS errors in the agent's own files.
    - `Scripts/agents/wait-for-result.py`, so an agent waits for its train result in the background.
  - **The teams.**
    - **xml:** a portable XML tree, named XMLDocument on iOS, so the EPUB, feed, and OPML parsers compile there unchanged. `PortableXMLDifferentialTests` compares it with Foundation's on 104 documents, with 14 listed differences.
    - **shell:** windows, the app delegate, menus, keys, and the shortcut model, whose stored form is untouched.
    - **a11y:** VoiceOver focus, the announcer, the text areas, and the sheets.
    - **engine:** helper programs, the editor round trip, Now Playing's device watching, and the view models.
    - **media:** the Record window and the audio editor.

    Every worker's work was accepted as written. The xml review's depth-scan finding (a hostile feed could have hung iOS) was fixed and tested.
  - **What iOS does now.** Phase 0 builds nothing for the iPhone's user. Every conversion that needs a helper program, editing in another app, recording, the audio editor, and Word 97-2003 export throw `IOSUnavailable`, whose sentence says the feature "is not available on iPhone yet". The stand-ins are listed in each lead's report, for phase 1.

- [x] **Documentation of iOS phase 0** (`docs/ios-phase0`, merged as 9d6228bb, November 4; Sonnet agent `docs-ios0`; brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-11-03/docs-ios0.md`). - [x] **Pages a book's generator left without numbers, and the book's pages in Go to Page's hint** (`fix/placeholder-pages`, merged as 9b7f7c77, November 4; the orchestrator).
  - **The request.** The user, November 4: "I was reading the book "His Master's Voice" in the science fiction folder and its page button says "Page pagenum_000000 of 1 button" which is weird and I think the voiceover hint for the go to page text field should include the number of pages in the book."
  - **The cause.** The book's EPUB, read in the library with the user's yes, read-only:
    - its navigation document's page list has one entry, "pagenum_000000", the id it links to, at the cover;
    - the page-break element there has an empty `title` and `aria-label="Page "`, with no number;
    - its NCX says `dtb:totalPageCount` 0.

    TextMouth took the entry's text for the page's name, and with no number among the labels the total was the count, 1.
  - **The fix.**
    - `MarkdownPageMarker.namesNoPage`: a label that is blank, a lone period, or only the word "Page" names no page.
    - The converter passes over such an `aria-label` or `title`. An element whose attributes say only "Page" is a page by its text only when the text is a page number (`isPageNumber`), and its other words are still read.
    - A page list entry whose text is only the id it links to or carries gives no label, unless the id is a page number; an NCX pageTarget's `value` stands in.
    - A list that labels none of its pages is passed over, for the NCX's and then the page-break elements.

    The book now has no pages: no page button, and Next Page says "This book has no pages."
  - **The re-read.** The EPUB and HTML page readers are at 1.0.2, so every EPUB and HTML book is read again once at the next launch, its places following their words, with no copy kept of text an earlier reader wrote (V05), as at 1.0.1. The pages change only in books that have either pattern, and their text only where such a page-break element holds its page's number, which is then the page's label and no longer read (ST02).
  - **The hint.** "This book has 300 pages, from i to 300. Type a page as the book numbers it, such as 12 or xii.", the number being the N of "Page 12 of N"; "This book's only page is 1." for a book of one page.
  - **Checks.**
    - Eleven new tests and the updated ones in EPUBPageListTests, MarkdownPageMarkerTests, PagesUpgradeTests, and GoToPageTests (the hosted field's test now pins the new hint).
    - The strict `make typecheck-ios`.
    - The full suite: 9333 tests, 75 skipped, 13 failures, all in two classes: OnDiskContractTests' pin of the page readers' versions, which the branch then raised to 1.0.2, and WindowsMatrixTests' audio, which stopped advancing during a 52-minute run. On the final tree those two classes and the four above passed together: 171 tests, 2 skipped, 0 failures.

- [x] **Page labels without their leading "Page", and no page button in a book of one page** (`fix/page-prefix`, merged as be21c243, November 4; the orchestrator).
  - **The request.** The user, November 4, answering question 1 of `humanQuestions/2026-11-04.md`: "Yes please remove the prefix "page" for books with it and can you actually hide the page button if there is only one page in the book?"
  - **The labels.**
    - `MarkdownPageMarker`'s normalization, shared by every format's reader and by decode, takes a leading word "Page" off a label when a name follows it (`withoutPagePrefix`): "Page 12", "page xii", "PAGE: 3", and "Page12" are the pages 12, xii, 3, and 12. "Pages 4" and "Pageant" stay.
    - A content.md written before reads its "Page 12" as page 12.
    - `namesNoPage` and `isPageNumber` ask after the word is taken off.
  - **What is read.**
    - The HTML converter keeps each page's label as its source spelled it (`PageStart`, and `HTMLPageTargets.sourceLabels` for a page list's), and text no more than either form is not read. So "Page 12" under "Page 12" stays unread.
    - A number under "Page 12" is now the page's own and is not read, the change the user agreed to; it replaces stable020's `testANumberUnderALongerLabelIsRead`.
    - A caption "Page 12" under "12" alone is still read.
    - DAISY compares a page's text with its NCC entry's raw label, so its reading is unchanged.
  - **The re-read.** The EPUB and HTML page readers are at 1.0.3, since only their text can change. Other formats' labels lose the word whenever content.md is read.
  - **The button.** `ReaderViewModel.hasSeveralPages` decides the status row's page button. `hasPages` still decides Next Page, Previous Page, and Go to Page…, which work in a book of one page. The setting's hint says "for books of more than one page".
  - **Checks.**
    - The sixteen page test classes: 432 tests, 2 skipped, 0 failures.
    - The strict `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings, link ok.
    - The full suite: 9337 tests, 75 skipped, 0 failures.

- [x] **A book of one page has no page to move to** (`fix/one-page-commands`, merged as 0a788518, November 4; the orchestrator).
  - **The request.** The user, November 4: "Is next page and go to page buttons or just shortcuts? If they are buttons I think they should be hidden, if a shortcut I think it should just warn the user and not do anything." They are Navigate menu commands with keys; the status row's page button was already left out of such a book (be21c243).
  - **The change.**
    - `ReaderViewModel.pageCommandHasPages()` says `ReaderPageWording.onlyOnePage` ("This book has only one page.") and moves nothing in a book of one page. That covers Next Page, Previous Page, Go to Page…, and a skip by the Page unit.
    - `readerHasPages`, which dims the three menu commands, is `hasSeveralPages`.
    - The pickers' choices and the unit cycle leave Page out unless it is in force.
    - Where Am I? leaves the page out, and its setting's hint says "for books of more than one page".
  - **Checks.** The page tests: 60 tests, 2 skipped, 0 failures. The strict `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings. The full suite: 9338 tests, 75 skipped, 0 failures.

- [x] **Kindle books in the MOBI format** (`readers/mobi`, merged as 7d6c4174, November 5, version 0.3.0; the agent mobireader, Sonnet, reviewed by Opus). FMT42.
  - **The request.** The user, November 4: "I want on the todo for parsers for importing drm free .mobi, .azw3, and .kfx books to at least import them and convert them to the intermediate reading format", then "go ahead and start agents on the new parsers / readers for .doc / .rtf .mobi".
  - **What it reads.**
    - `BookFormat.mobi` (raw value "mobi", "Kindle Book (MOBI)", extensions mobi, prc, and azw), read by `MOBIParser` the same in both apps.
    - MOBI 6 compressed with PalmDOC or HUFF/CDIC, and the MOBI 6 part of a hybrid book.
    - PalmDOC (TEXtREAd) books as plain text, or as HTML when an html or body tag is in their first 4,096 bytes.
    - Chapters from the NCX index, with deeper entries as headings, or one for each page break when there is no index. A book's head is never read.
    - No pages: `PageReaderVersions.current(for: .mobi)` is nil.
  - **Refusals.**
    - Copy protection, checked first: the encryption field, or a DRM offset that is not 0xFFFFFFFF.
    - KF8 only.
    - An unknown compression.
    - A damaged book, including a text that comes up short of its declared length. A longer text is cut at that length.
  - **The base.** It brings `core/own-readers`: `OwnDocumentReaders`, the seam the RTF and Word readers plug into; `Scripts/agents/regenerate-project.py`; and the briefs in `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-11-04/`.
  - **The review** (Opus, `build/orchestrator/readers1104/mobi-review.md`) found 0 blockers, 3 should-fix (real-file coverage), and 6 nits.
    - Fixed: the .azw MIME type; a comment; and foliate-js's MIT licence. Three files follow `mobi.js` closely. The licence is in `Vendor/foliate-js/LICENSE` and ships in `Licenses/foliate-js`, with a test in `BundledLicenseTests`.
    - A search for real samples (`mobi-samples-report.md`) screened 40+ real books with a decoder of its own:
      - none decodes to less than it declares;
      - two Mobigen dictionaries decode 166 bytes more, which the reader cuts;
      - it found a TEXtREAd book whose text is HTML, which the reader would have read tags and all. Now fixed: the HTML route, and the head left unread.
    - Five real books are pinned by text digests in `MOBIRealBookTests`:
      - libmobi's HUFF/CDIC hybrid, whose digest equals its uncompressed twin's;
      - libmobi's Windows-1252 book;
      - libmobi's TEXtREAd HTML book;
      - Gutenberg's Ghosts, a kindlegen hybrid;
      - Langenhoven's Kootjie Totjie, with two trailing entries.

      The three libmobi files are LGPL-3.0, with `libmobi-COPYING.LESSER` and `libmobi-COPYING` beside them.
  - **At the merge.**
    - readers/mobi's committed project no longer copied liblouis's tables. `regenerate-project.py` had put back half of xcodegen's reordering. The project was regenerated whole (2f599593), and the script was fixed (`tools/regenerate-fix`, merged as e750c3ca): it now compares the copy phases by meaning.
    - `LibraryViewDefinitionTests` used "mobi" as an unknown format (fixed, ca86737f).
    - Merged with it: `fix/corpus-limit-race` (508f5281) and `tools/train-derived` (2a8bf158). The train's cache moved to `build/orchestrator/train/DerivedData.noindex`, and a train now builds again without the branches that broke its compile.
  - **Version 0.3.0.** 0.2.1 to 0.2.6 hide a Kindle book but keep the library (V03). 0.2.0 and older can't open it. The changelog says so.
  - **Checks.** The trains: 1104-2330, 2060 tests, 0 failures. The full suite: 9428 tests, 75 skipped, 1 failure (the view test, fixed; `LibraryViewDefinitionTests` then 18 tests, 0 failures). The strict `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings, link ok.
  - **Follow-ups.**
    - The MOBI header's locale when EXTH 524 is absent (N2).
    - The copy-protection check also reads the DRM offset (N3, briefed so). calibre and KindleUnpack check only the encryption field, so if a DRM-free book is ever refused as copy-protected, look there.
    - Metadata reads up to 64 MB of the file (N5).
    - APNX pages.
    - KF8 and AZW3 (FMT48).
    - The iOS app ships no Licenses folder yet: LAME's and foliate-js's licences must go into it before iOS ships.

- [x] **TextMouth's own readers of rich text and Word 97-2003** (`readers/rtf`, `readers/doc`, `readers/rtf-switch`, `readers/doc-switch`, merged as b3937d29, cbd5278d, 30dadc38, and a6d08a82, November 5, version 0.3.1; the agents rtfreader and docreader, Sonnet, each reviewed by Opus). FMT47.
  - **The request.** The user, November 4: "small Text Mouth readers for .doc /odt etc might be good for both so formatting extraction is under our control", and "go ahead and start agents on the new parsers / readers for .doc / .rtf .mobi".
  - **The readers.** Both plug into `OwnDocumentReaders`, which `TextutilDocumentReader` asks after the source check and the password check, and their HTML goes straight to `HTMLToMarkdownConverter`.
    - **Rich text.** `RTFDocumentReader` and ten `RTF*` files read .rtf files and RTFD packages, from the RTF 1.9.1 specification:
      - code pages, double-byte text, and \u with its \uc fallback;
      - headings from the style sheet, with textutil's size guess when none is named;
      - bold and italic, lists, tables, field results, and shape text boxes;
      - pages at \page, \pagebb, and a \sect that starts a page;
      - hidden text, headers, footers, and footnotes are left out.
    - **Word 97-2003.** `CompoundFile` and the `WordDocument*` files read the compound file and the Word document:
      - headings from heading styles and outline levels;
      - Word's emphasis toggles, tables, and typed bullets;
      - field results without instructions;
      - footnotes and endnotes after the main text;
      - pages at page breaks, page break before (direct or by style), and page-starting sections;
      - tracked deletions are left out, and an empty main story falls back to the other stories;
      - Word 6 and 95 go to textutil, and are refused on iOS.
  - **The Mac switch.** Both readers are on in both apps. Rich text, RTFD, and .doc are at page reader 1.1.0, so their books are read again once. An older TextMouth leaves a book stamped by a newer reader as it is, so no caution was needed. `OwnDocumentReaders.$forcesTextutil`, a test-only task-local, keeps the tests that pin textutil's reading testing textutil. One golden was recorded again for RTF and seven for .doc, each difference named.
  - **The evidence.**

- [x] **A library in the cloud fetches its files and opens by itself** (`fix/cloud-library-fetch`, merged November 6 in version 0.3.2; the agent cloudfetch, reviewed by fetchreview and fetchverify).
  - **The report.** The user, November 6, after a restart with the library in Google Drive's Stream mode: "Some of the library's files are in Google Drive and aren't on this Mac yet." and "Library unavailable"... "Like being in Google Drive is kind of the point? What broke." Nothing in the code had changed: the check, on the main thread with downloads refused so that it can't hang, asked the sync service for the files and then waited for Try Again. Which file was only in the cloud, and why, is unknown: the restart or low disk space (under 10 GB free) are the likely causes. The user went back to the local library that night, so the two libraries have diverged.
  - **The fix.** When the check names files only in the cloud, they are read off the main thread with downloads allowed, at most eight at once, each to its end (Google Drive adopts partial reads, so one byte does not bring a file), and the check runs again, up to six passes. The screen says Getting Your Library, and the library opens by itself. Retries at 5, 15, and 30 seconds and a stall limit of 60 seconds end in Library Not Available with what to check; a late read still opens the library, unless another library was chosen. Books opened from Finder meanwhile are held and opened, or named if they couldn't be. Once a cloud library opens, config.json, reading_stats.json, substitutions.json, and the feed records are fetched in the background and taken up by their services, never saved over. A main-thread lstat at launch and a feed package's marker lookup no longer wait on the network.
  - **The evidence.** Full suite 9668 tests, 77 skipped, 2 failures, both timing in tests (fixed in de32acfb, those classes rerun: 54 tests, 0 failures); `make typecheck-ios` 0 errors, 0 warnings, link ok. No real dataless file was used: `testTheLibraryFetchBringsAnEvictedFileBackWhole` and `testTheLibraryFetchListsAnEvictedFolder` wait for the user's yes on a throwaway folder.
  - **Follow-ups.** Substitution edits made to the stand-in rules while the file is in the cloud are dropped on arrival, with a log line. The Finder hold has no unit test, since it lives in ContentView. An earlier line of CLOUD-STORAGE-RESEARCH said FileManager's download call was unverified on Google Drive; the October 3 real-drive tests had run it on book files.

- [x] **One policy for every document reader, OpenDocument and Word 2003 XML, and KFX** (`readers/rich-followups`, `readers/word-followups`, `readers/odt`, `readers/odt-switch`, `readers/kfx`, combined on `core/readers-1106` and fast-forwarded to main as ba0ea510, November 6, version 0.4.0; the agents richfix, wordfix, odtreader, kfxreader, and docsreaders, with first reviews on Opus by richreview, wordreview, odtreview, and kfxreview, and verifiers on Sonnet).
  - **The request.** The user, November 5: "Figure out the hidden text and other things for the existing rtf readers", and of OpenDocument and Word 95: "Are the odt and word 95 parsers writable or do you just not have samples?" November 4: "small Text Mouth readers for .doc /odt etc might be good for both so formatting extraction is under our control, same for kfx." November 6, on KFX test books: "If you can figure out how to use Kindle Previewer from the CLI you can test it and try making a file..."
  - **The policy** (`Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-11-05/common.md`, the orchestrator's decision). A document reader reads what a person sees in the document's ordinary view, in reading order. Hidden text is not read, web-hidden text is, and a hidden paragraph mark joins nothing. A tracked deletion is not read, an insertion is, and moved text is read once. Notes follow the main text with no reference marks, and only if a person would see the reference. Comments, headers, and footers are not read, and field codes never are. A text box is read right after the paragraph holding its anchor; in Word 97-2003, after the notes.
  - **Rich text and DOCX.** `\deleted` and `\mvf` are not read, and `\plain` undoes `\deleted`. RTF and DOCX read their notes. DOCX: `w:vanish` by the toggle rule of ECMA-376 17.7.3; `mc:AlternateContent` follows `Requires`, so an emoji's Fallback is read and a box is read once; emphasis goes through `MarkdownEmphasis`, so no asterisk or tilde is spoken (83 of 1,472 real files spoke them on main); a damaged notes part never refuses a book. `ImportCancellation.throwIfCancelled()` is the shared check.
  - **Word 97-2003.** `sprmCFVanish` and `sprmCFRMarkDel` as toggles, `Pcd.prm` (`Prm0`, `Prm1`, `RgPrc`), `PlfLst` and `PlfLfo` lists counted by `DOCXListCounter`, main-story text boxes, `sprmPHugePapx` and `sprmPTableProps`, and a note's reference checked through `PlcffndRef`. Numbers are silent behind `WordDocumentReader.speaksListNumbers`, off until the user answers `humanQuestions/2026-11-06.md`. Three real files that were refused now read.
  - **OpenDocument and Word 2003 XML (FMT27, FMT53).** Own readers, on the Mac too, sharing `OfficeXMLReader`, `OfficeParagraph`, and `OfficeBlockWriter` and reusing the RTF block types. Picture descriptions are read in OpenDocument. A document that names even one heading keeps exactly those, so the Mac's switch rule became: at least textutil's headings in a document that names none. `XMLEscaping`'s quadratic append is fixed, which `.rtf` could reach on main. `XMLInputGuard` refuses a start tag over 64 KB and any document type declaration in any encoding.
  - **KFX (FMT52).** `TextMouth/Parsers/KFX*.swift` from `KFX-FORMAT-NOTES-2026-11-05.md` and the public Ion specification, behind a licence wall: the plugins are GPL 3 and TextMouth is MIT, so the implementer and reviewers ran the tools but never read them. Copy-protected books, fixed-layout books, and Kindle Create packages are refused. A section begins a chapter only when a contents entry lands at its start or it starts with a heading, and no heading is ever made up. Test books come from Amazon's Kindle Previewer 4.0.4, unpacked from its installer with no account, and calibre 9.18 from its disk image, with throwaway settings (`Scripts/make-kfx-sample.py`); the user's calibre is 5.36, too old for the plugins.
  - **The evidence.** Full suite at e7e736f4: 10243 tests, 78 skipped, 0 failures. After the last test-helper change and the version, the classes touched: 184 tests, 0 failures. `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings, link ok. The project's copy phases are the same by meaning. Real-file comparisons: 525 RTF, 1,472 DOCX, 346 Word 97-2003, 511 OpenDocument, and 24 Word 2003 XML files; KFX word for word and paragraph for paragraph with the EPUB reader on the same books. Reports, reviews, and verifications are in `build/orchestrator/followups1105/`.
  - **Follow-ups.**
    - With the user: should list numbers be heard; should picture descriptions be read in Word and rich text (`humanQuestions/2026-11-06.md`). The real-drive cases of `CloudDriveIntegrationTests` wait for a yes.
    - Word 6 and Word 95: `readers/word95` and `readers/word95-switch`, built on this, under review.
    - Unverified KFX: a store-bought container, multi-container sets, container version 1, Kindle's folder forms. `KFXPrivateBooksTests` reads the user's own books from `build/kfx-private/`, reporting counts only. `.kpf` is refused. The iOS plist has no document types for MOBI or KFX.
    - A KFX paragraph that begins with a floated span followed by more than one text node splits, adding a pause. A heading-less, unlisted file such as a dedication joins the chapter before it.
    - `MarkdownMarkerWriter` is slow with many pages in one paragraph (20,000 pages in 2 MB took 116 s), for any format.
    - DOCX and EPUB should adopt `XMLInputGuard`'s start tag limit: `XMLParser` is quadratic inside one tag. DOCX reads no table-style or `w:docDefaults` `w:vanish`, and no `w:trPr/w:hidden` row. Word 2003 XML skips a hidden row.
    - Word 97-2003: a custom note mark is read, a box anchored in hidden text is read, and ordinal and word number formats read as Arabic while numbers are off anyway.
    - OpenDocument: the pipeline peaks at 2.0 GB on a 64 MB part. `uiwriter/tdf135056.odt`, whose words are in page-anchored boxes, reads 1 page where its file says 17 (textutil reads 1). An .odt already in a library keeps its author Unknown until Replace Existing.
    - A very large rich text table is refused as too large at about 790,000 cells; main read it using gigabytes.

- [x] **Version 0.4.6: End of heading waits for the heading playback starts in** (`fix/end-of-heading-silent-start`, merged 5862bce0 into `integrate/046` on November 11, 2026; main fast-forwarded and tagged `v0.4.6`). The iPhone interface tests of `ci/ios-ui-tests` found it, and finding 3 of that branch's Opus review traced it. A sleep timer set to End of heading at a Project Gutenberg EPUB's image-only cover stopped playback at once: Play starts past the cover, at the very heading the timer waited for. No reader version changed, so no book is read again.
  - **The fix.**
    - `headingAfterPlaybackStart` takes the heading after the first word Play would say. That word comes from `playbackStartPlace()`, built on `speechStartFromCursor()`, which searches with the new `LongChapterPreparation.never`, so no long chapter is prepared on the main actor. While that place isn't known, the cursor's heading stands for now.
    - `retargetSleepTimerHeading(toPlayStartingAt:)`, in `startSpeakingAsNowPlaying`, settles the heading where speech starts, so `play()` and `continueWaitingPlay()` both reach it. A book waiting for its chapter's speech counts as playing.
    - `hasSpeakableText(in:fromSpokenOffset:)` asks `firstSpeakableOffset`, so what says nothing is decided in one place. Audiobooks are untouched.
  - **The evidence.**
    - Full suite at 88b749a8: 11,987 tests, 81 skipped, 0 failures.
    - `make build` (Release): succeeded, signed with the TextMouth Developer certificate, 23 MB, with no compiler warning.
    - 12 new tests in `IntegrationSleepTimerTests` and `SpeechPreparationCacheTests`. GitHub runs with a fix taken out (42984571224, 42994298772, 43000464970, 43003774129) failed 11 of them; the 12th is a helper's table.
    - Trains 1111-0205 (781 tests) and 1111-0344 (890 tests) had 0 failures.
    - The Opus review said merge after fixes 1 and 2: a waiting Play still met the bug, and choosing the heading prepared a long chapter on the main actor. Both were fixed. The narrow check said merge with follow-ups.
  - **Follow-ups, each as it was at 0.4.5.**
    - `restartPlaybackAtCursor` (through `speakCurrentChapter`) doesn't reach the retarget. It can still stop at once when the book is paused with the cursor in a silent chapter tail, the next chapter is long and not yet prepared, and the heading is still the cursor's.
    - A move back, after a timer set while playing, keeps the heading ahead, so playback goes on through the heading that was playing. Refresh the saved heading on a move back.
    - A finished book's End of heading timer holds no heading, and Skip Backward then Play doesn't refresh it. How that timer then ends isn't traced.
    - No test builds a next book's window already open, whose long chapter is still being prepared.
    - The iPhone's `PlaybackUITests` sets End of the book. Switch it to End of heading once `ci/ios-ui-tests` is on main.
  - **Reports:** `build/orchestrator/endheading/endheading-report.md`, `endheading-review.md`, `endheading-fix-report.md`, and `endheading-check.md`.

- [x] **Builds and tests on GitHub Actions** (`ci/github-actions`, merged 20c38b46 on November 10, 2026; tools, a workflow, and documentation only, so no version change). The user: "I had the idea to take the build preassure off my Mac using GitHub Actions (maybe in a special "TestBuild" branch that you could commit and push too." The repository is public at github.com/AERobertAI/TextMouth.
  - **What it does.**
    - A push to `TestBuild` runs `.github/workflows/test-build.yml` on free `macos-26` runners with Xcode 26.0.1: it plans the shards on Linux, runs up to four macOS shards with a build cached once a day, and joins the results.
    - The token is read-only, it uses no secrets, and every action is pinned. Nothing from a pull request or a fork runs it, and `check.yml` keeps to main and pull requests.
    - The train runs there with `build-train.py run --remote` or `loop --remote`, taking no heavy-work lock, and `--soon N` sends a train N minutes after a request instead of on the hour.
    - The full suite runs there with `run_suite.py --remote`.
    - One command, `Scripts/agents/test_command.py`, serves the Mac and the runner, and the Mac's own runs are byte for byte as before.
    - `github_run.push` pushes only `<commit>:refs/heads/TestBuild`, from tracked files. It refuses anything under `build/`, `humanQuestions/`, or `humanReports/`, in the tip or in any commit origin lacks, and, once the workflow is on origin/main, any change to the workflows.
    - `Scripts/agents/runner-skips.conf` lists 114 entries, about 145 tests, that can't run on the runner (most read or play audio, which hangs a runner's test host). Each has its reason, they still run on the Mac, and a remote result names every requested one as "Not run on GitHub, no verdict".
  - **The evidence.**
    - `Scripts/agents/tests`: 300 tests.
    - The remote train passed twice: 164 tests in 4.3 minutes, and 94 in 3.3 minutes with 15 "Not run on GitHub" lines.
    - An Opus review asked for five fixes: a rerun judged by an earlier run's log, a privacy check that missed quoted names and history, unnamed skipped requests, whole-class skips, and the workflow a branch could change. A narrow check proved the privacy, rerun, and workflow fixes against throwaway repositories and found the Mac's commands unchanged.
    - The stale TestBuild caches were cleared on GitHub; they had filled 9.8 of its 10 GB.

On the merged tip it took 23.2 minutes and failed on two cancel timings (`OfficeXMLGuardTests.testCancelLandsInTheMiddleOfARead`, `OwnReaderCancellationTests.testCancelPartwayThroughALargeWordDocumentIsHeardAtOnce`) and three one-off hangs of the host. The 114-entry list hasn't had a clean run. **Clean three times in a row in 0.4.5** (`ci/suite-clean`); one-off failures remain, so the Mac's suite is still the gate.
    - A branch that breaks the workflow would requeue every 15 minutes without a limit.

- [x] **Quitting while work is held** (`fix/quit-while-held`, merged f82bec41 into `integration/0.4.4` on November 10, 2026, in 0.4.4). Two faults of 0.4.2, found by the outside review of the transcript branch:
  - **A quit asked again from the main queue while work was held never ended.** Quit Anyway, a save, an editor, the Record window, or a library move left TextMouth open with its main actor stopped. Every re-ask now goes through `QuitRequest.ask()`, on a run-loop timer in the common modes.
  - **A second Command-Q, or the Dock's Quit, during a hold cut the work off.** Now `QuitFinisher` cancels a person's quit, finishes the work, and asks again. A panel, "Quitting TextMouth", says what it is finishing, and VoiceOver announces it.

    - Train 1110-0000 passed (3,344 tests).
    - The Opus review held it for a blocker: the quit reason was read under `'why '`, where the SDK's `kAEQuitReason` is `'why?'`, so a log out with work under way would have been cancelled. The test and the probe had pinned the same wrong keyword. Both now take the SDK's constant, and the narrow check proved the fix by breaking it.
  - **Follow-ups.**
    - An editor's "quits once" mark stays set if a library move or a Record take cancels its request.
    - The source scan misses `forceTerminate()`, `Selector("terminate:")`, `abort()`, and `kill(`, none used today.
    - The probe's `old-second` control flaked once.
    - A session end that meets unsaved edits is still cancelled, as before.
    - `kAEQuitAll` isn't among the session's reasons.
    - Downloads stopped for a quit that is then cancelled stay stopped for the session.
    - A real loginwindow quit is untried.

- [x] **Older import and reader follow-ups, the second sweep** (`fix/followup-sweep-2`, merged 78412bab into `integration/0.4.3` on November 9, 2026, in 0.4.3).
  - **What changed.**
    - Unmarked UTF-16 and UTF-32 are read, by rules that no stray NUL in 8-bit text sets off: rule 4 needs at least 8 ASCII anchors, making up 90%. They never overrule a recorded encoding.
    - An HTML file declaring ISO-2022-JP is read as such.
    - A book folder that is a link imports as the folder it names.
    - Clearer words for a book outside the data directory, and for a damaged DOCX or DAISY archive. A refused SMIL keeps the "text beyond audio" notice. An empty DOCX title or creator falls back.
    - HTML's page reader is split from EPUB's.
  - **The evidence.** Train 1109-2003 passed its classes. An Opus review found that the first rule 4 turned about a third of Cyrillic, Greek, or Hebrew UTF-8 files with two stray NULs into Hangul and Han, on the launch re-read; it asked for four fixes. A harness on the real decoder showed 0 flips in 2,000 placements per script after the fix, and 492 to 779 before. A narrow check found no flip from 1 to 8 NULs in 18 cases.
  - **Follow-ups.** Short Chinese, Japanese, or Korean UTF-16 with few spaces, digits, or line ends, and Indic UTF-16, aren't recognised. Subtitles and `.m3u` playlists in unmarked non-Latin UTF-16 now read right, with no version, since nothing reads them again.
  - **Reports:** `build/orchestrator/followups1105/sweep2-report.md`, `-fix-report.md`, `-review.md`, `sweep2check-report.md`.

  - **How it reads.** `KF8Layout` reads the skeleton, fragment, and flow tables, following mobi.js. `KF8Book` makes a section of each document: titles from the contents (NCX), headings at its anchors, pages from page-break elements, since KF8 keeps no page list. A piece that opens with no heading and that no contents entry names continues the chapter before it only when its title repeats the previous piece's, or is the heading the next piece opens with, compared exactly after `readableLabel`; the first piece with words never continues. The .mobi reader's version is 1.0.0, so .mobi books are read again once.

Train 1108-0800 passed every class the branch asked for at efd812fb. A narrow check said merge with follow-ups; its should-fix, five false sections in Pride and Prejudice and a second licence section, went to a fix round whose first rule was too broad and was narrowed. Eight parts of the rule, each broken in a scratch copy, each fail a test. Pride and Prejudice has 66 chapters; Moby Dick 135 chapters, the Epilogue, and one licence section; toc-epilogue matches its EPUB; every real book's words equal its EPUB's.
