  - **What changed.** The build train runs every hour, on the hour. It waits for the heavy-work lock first in line, so no other job takes the lock before it, for up to 50 minutes, and never runs without it: until now a train that had waited 20 minutes went on beside the job holding the lock. It takes its requests once it holds the lock, and merges the commit each request names, not the branch's newest. `status` says what a running train is doing. A type-check waits up to three hours for the lock, as `run_locked.py` does.
  - **What it asks of agents.** Making each train count, in `docs/agents/working-with-agents.md`, gives the user's request in their words: review the diff before asking, ask once for everything, make each test tell as much as it can, work while the train runs, fix every failure in one round, and batch heavy jobs. Every brief points at it.

Of 2,244 real files, 9 read differently, each at a symbol, and the other 2,235 exactly as before. 34 deliberate breaks caught. The check found two guards no test pinned (a symbol in a note's custom mark, and in `w:del` or `w:moveFrom`); 3abffbef adds its four tests.
  - **Follow-ups.** A `w:rFonts` in a paragraph mark's properties has no test of its own (harmless). No real `.wordml` file holds a symbol. A table style's font, a theme's font, and `w:cs` and `w:eastAsia` fonts aren't read.

- [x] **The markup guard's large stack** (`fix/markup-guard-stack`, merged be080b7b on November 8, 2026, in version 0.4.2). SwiftSoup's parse, the depth check after it, and the release of a refused tree run on a thread with a 490 MiB stack, enough for the deepest tree the memory limit allows, so whether the count before the parse is exact no longer decides whether a book can crash TextMouth. If the system refuses that stack, 122 and then 30 MiB are tried, then the caller's thread. Reports: `build/orchestrator/followups1105/markupstack-report.md` (Part 2), `stackcheck-report.md`.
  - **The evidence.** Full suite on the merged 0.4.2 branch (36ee74ca): 11,151 tests, 81 skipped, 0 failures; `make typecheck-ios` 0 errors, 0 warnings. Train 1107-1800 passed the tip (3,299 tests, 0 failures, the import and feeds areas). In child processes, nested HTML to 50,000 levels and XML to 990,000 parsed and were refused by the second layer with no crash, where SwiftSoup alone on a 512 KiB stack overflowed at 3,000 to 5,000. The check confirmed every parse sits inside `onLargeStack`, the limits are read on the caller's thread, and the fallback in a harness runs the body once and returns the same error. Ordinary books cost about 55 µs more a chapter.
  - **After the check** (the orchestrator): 9bb2f521 `withExtendedLifetime(job)`; 89096319 the fallback's comment; 95d018ad the policy test holds each parse and its depth check to the big stack.
  - **Follow-ups.** A 490 MiB secondary-thread stack is unverified on an iPhone, and pages that can't be committed when touched are memory pressure, which the fallback doesn't catch. The policy tests don't strip block comments, and the one-way-in test matches fixed names only. Documents too small to nest deeply (about 1 KB) could parse on the caller's stack, saving a thread each; 20,000 feed fragments went from 0.60 s to 1.05 s.

- [x] **Markup that could crash TextMouth** (`fix/markup-guard` with `fix/markup-guard-mobitest`, merged 50f59418 on November 7, 2026). Every SwiftSoup parse in the app goes through `MarkupGuard`. Three ways a damaged or crafted book, web page, or feed crashed TextMouth on main, all older than this week, are closed: a broken `<?` declaration trapped SwiftSoup 2.13.9 (`XmlTreeBuilder.swift:120-121`); markup nested past about 108 levels (debug) or 552 (Release) overflowed the HTML-to-Markdown converter's recursion on a 512 KiB stack; and a document of millions of tags exhausted memory.
  - **How.** Before the parse, one pass rewrites only the trapping declarations, as `<!---->`, and counts nesting (`HTMLTreeModel` follows SwiftSoup's tree builder mode by mode, slips included; XML exactly), memory units (limit 1,000,000), attributes on one tag (1,000), and work. After the parse, `MarkupGuard.requireDepth(of:atMost:)` walks the tree with its own stack and refuses one deeper than 128 levels (512 for feed discovery) before the converter sees it. MOBI's own nesting check is gone: MOBI goes through the guard at conversion. Each refusal says what is wrong and what to do.
  - **The evidence.** Full suite 10,631 tests, 78 skipped, 0 failures. `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings. 355 real files and 62 web pages read byte for byte as before, none newly refused; the deepest real file nests 12 levels and the deepest page 36 (GitHub). Differential proofs, in child processes: every one-to-three-tag unit of 108 tags at N = 40 (5,085,936 units, both builds) and at N = 2,000 in Release, with samples in debug, found no count below the tree and no fault. No page reader line changes, so no book is read again.
  - **Follow-ups.** The second outside check reported reproducing a document whose parsed tree is deeper than the first layer's count, and was stopped by the model's safeguards before it measured the gap; the guard's own agent was stopped the same way when given the case, so nobody has measured it. The second layer still stands before the converter. `fix/markup-guard-stack` (in progress) runs the parse, the depth check, and a refused tree's release on a thread whose stack covers the deepest tree the memory limit allows, so the count's exactness no longer decides a crash. Also: 1.7% of declaration rewrites weren't needed (the words are unchanged); EPUB's generic sentence for a refused chapter; a file nested 108 to 128 levels can still overflow a developer's debug converter (the app is a Release build). The guard's trains ran only its own classes until train 1107-1700, which is how the full suite first caught the MOBI test: a branch under every reader trains the import and feeds areas, and `MarkupGuardTests` is now in `[import]`.

- [x] **Word 6 and Word 95, and symbols by font** (`readers/word95`, `readers/word95-switch`, merged 6e5697c0 on November 7, 2026). TextMouth's own reader reads Word 6 and Word 95 documents, on the Mac too, in place of textutil. An Opus review held the Mac switch for three faults: every Word 6 symbol read as "(", one code page for a whole file, and lost headings. A verify then found that **Word 97 files on main read every symbol as "(" too** (6,684 runs in 340 real files), and that a symbol in a font other than Symbol needed reading by its font. A narrow check settled the last round. Reports: `build/orchestrator/followups1105/word95-review.md`, `word95-verify.md`, `word95-check.md`, and the round reports beside them.
  - **What it reads.** The code page by the run: the run's own mark, then its font's character set, then the document's default. Far East double-byte text is the document's choice. Symbols by font: Symbol and its clones through the Adobe table, WordPerfect's typographic, math, and Greek codes that real text settles, a space for a picture font, and U+F0xx in an ordinary font through the Symbol table, as textutil does. Headings by style, tables, bin pages, notes, text boxes, outline numbers (silent, as numbers are).
  - **The evidence.** Full suite 10600 tests, 78 skipped, 0 failures. `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings. 312 real Word 6 and 95 documents: no file reads worse than textutil, and the reader finds more headings overall. 340 real Word 97 documents: every difference from main is at a symbol or a U+F0xx character.
  - **Versions.** `.doc` at 1.3.0, so every .doc book is read again once. `.docx` and `.wordml` stay at 1.2.0.
  - **Follow-ups.** The mutation proofs of the last round's tests never ran. A checkbox typed in an ordinary font reads as a Greek letter, as in textutil. WP codes 0x37 and 0x23 stay spaces. Numbers carried by a style, custom note marks, and Wingdings text aren't read right. `w:sym` in DOCX can now use the Symbol table (`readers/followups-1106`'s item 4). No GovDocs file is in the repository.

- [x] **EPUB 3 export (FMT28)** (`export/epub3`, merged 20347879 on November 7, 2026). Export's Convert to menu makes an EPUB 3 of a text book, after Word 97-2003 and before BRF. An Opus review found 6 blockers (a pin the suite didn't update, an invalid contents list when levels rise after a skip, annotated headings never reaching the file, a false accessibility sentence, odd links failing EPUBCheck, a full disk reported as success) and a verify found the checksum check didn't compare; all fixed. Reports: `build/orchestrator/followups1105/epubexport-report.md`, `epub-review.md`, `epubexport-fix-report.md`, `epub-verify.md`.
  - **What a file holds.** Real headings, a contents list nested by level with one list to an item, contents entries for a book's annotated headings, a page list and page breaks where the book has pages, the language and its direction by script, a stable identifier (the book's library id), and accessibility metadata that states only what is true, with no conformance claim. Pictures, styles, the cover, and links other than web and mail are left out.
  - **Shared changes.** The HTML export's contents list nests one list to an item where levels skip; `languageTag` checks the BCP 47 grammar, for HTML and PEF too; every export keeps words that follow a marker on the same line. An EPUB's `dc:language` of `und` reads as no language.
  - **The evidence.** Full suite 10476 tests, 78 skipped, 0 failures. `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings. EPUBCheck 5.2.1 clean on 135 sample books and 195 hard ones; each reads back in TextMouth with the same words, pages, and headings.
  - **Follow-ups.** TextMouth re-importing its own export of a long headingless text shows "Chapter N" rows (accepted; a reader change for the next time EPUB's line is raised). A chapter with a title and no blocks leaves the contents (Alice's "Cover"). An annotation's level reads back as its contents depth, and one in a table cell moves before the table. ZIPFoundation rewrites its directory per file, slow past tens of thousands of files. Not opened in Apple Books or heard with VoiceOver in a reading system.

- [x] **The reader work's own follow-ups** (`readers/followups-1106`, merged 42c2cff7, main at ac7d207e on November 7, 2026). From the November 6 merge's list. An Opus review found 2 blockers (the 64 KB start tag limit refused a real government DOCX, and the hidden row rule dropped words Word shows), and a verify found the encoding check's holes; all fixed. Reports: `build/orchestrator/followups1105/readerfollow-report.md`, `followups-review.md`, `followups-verify.md`, `readerfollow-last-report.md`.
  - **What changed.** `MarkdownMarkerWriter` and the DTBook and HTML converters are linear in pages (20,000 pages in one paragraph: 116 s before, milliseconds now), byte for byte the same. `XMLInputGuard` counts attributes per start tag (1,024), refuses an internal DTD subset and any encoding outside an allow-list, and guards DOCX, EPUB, DAISY, SSML, PEF, the ODT manifest, feeds, and OPML (feeds without the subset rule). `o:gfxdata` is stripped before a DOCX part reaches `XMLParser`, so a picture over about 7.5 MB no longer loses the words after it (main lost them). DOCX reads hidden text from `w:docDefaults` and table styles by MS-OI29500's toggle rule; a hidden row's visible words are read in DOCX and Word 2003 XML (the orchestrator's decision, from §17.4.20's note; nothing checked in Word). `.docx` and `.wordml` at 1.2.0. The iPhone has a plist of its own (`Scripts/ios/sync-info-plist.py`, `make check-ios-plist`).
  - **The evidence.** Full suite 10329 tests, 10245 passed, 78 skipped, 1 failed: `ReaderCloudDownloadTests.testABookCheckedAfterItsPackageMovedOpensFromWhereItIsNow`, "the test runner exited with code 0", as VoiceOver touched the host and the user's own TextMouth started. It passed in 20 earlier runs and in all 3 runs of its class alone afterward, so it's counted as environmental. `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings. 1,849 real files' encodings all allowed; 1,717 of 1,723 DOCX identical to main (the others are POI's refused external-entity file and 5 built toggle cases).
  - **Follow-ups.** `w:sym` and U+F0xx in a Symbol-font `w:t`, once `readers/word95`'s Symbol table is on main. `tblStylePr wholeTable` isn't read. A refused SMIL silences the "text beyond audio" notice. `docs/architecture/shared-helpers.md` line 76 and `state/imports.md` line 108 describe the old guard. Large DOCX read 5 to 9% slower.

- [x] **Older recorded bugs a person could still hit** (`fix/followup-sweep`, merged 755ba2e9 on November 7, 2026). Of about 55 items in the 46 follow-up lists below, 22 had been fixed since without the list saying so, 6 were still real and small, 9 are real and left (below), and 14 aren't worth doing. An Opus review and a Sonnet verify found 3 blockers, 8 should-fix items, and a regression, all fixed. Report: `build/orchestrator/followups1105/sweep-report.md`, with `sweep-review.md` and `sweep-verify.md`.

    - Move Book Up and Down say why they can't move, and are dimmed with another kind of window in front. A feed's item moves in the Reading List and is refused only in a folder.
    - "Bookmark N added" names the bookmark's own number, and a failed add or remove says the book has left the library.
    - An emptied title, or one of only spaces, is never saved.
    - `skipDescendants()` on a file or link hides the contents of the next folder listed beside it: fixed in `AudioBookParser.audioFiles`, two walks in `FileImportService`, and `MaintenanceFileAccess`, where it hid every multi-track audiobook's tracks from the check for files still in the cloud.
    - New Book from Text says when Paste, Open in Editor, or Reload fail, in a notice as well as aloud. Reload reads an editor's older encodings by guessing, and says when the text may be misread.
    - A missing EPUB says it's missing, with no path read aloud, and names the control that saves to the library only.
  - **Decided.** A book whose folder holds a symbolic link gets its lost tracks back, and a place in it may land in another track after an index rebuild. The orchestrator accepted this as rare; it goes in the changelog.
  - **The evidence.** Full suite 10261 tests, 78 skipped, 0 failures. `make typecheck-ios`: 0 errors, 0 warnings, link ok. Each test was proved by breaking its fix in a scratch copy.
  - **Not verified.** Speech with live VoiceOver, the menu's dimming in the running app, and the view wiring of New Book from Text.
  - **Done items in the lists below,** by the list's line number before this entry: 3734, 3746, 3763, 3774, 3790, 3805, 3827, 3849, 3889, 3895, 3901, and 3908. Read each list by its items: some of its other items stay open.

    - An m4b chapter marker inside one file stops an End of heading timer a little late.
    - A cancelled render doesn't stop `AVSpeechSynthesizer.write()`.
    - A book whose path is outside the data directory gets a misleading message when moved. **Done November 9** (`fix/followup-sweep-2`).
    - A book folder that is itself a symbolic link scans as an error. **Done November 9** (`fix/followup-sweep-2`).
    - A DOCX with an empty `dc:title` uses it as the title, and ZIP damage in DOCX or DAISY shows ZIPFoundation's raw error. **Done November 9** (`fix/followup-sweep-2`).

menu item stays enabled for audio books (the reader ignores it); the global Settings
speed help text could say it applies to text books; heading-navigator audio timings use
the character placeholder rather than media duration; `HeadingNavigatorView` still keeps
a private copy of the containing-heading rule; `Book.estimatedTimeRemaining` and
`Book.percentComplete` repeat one offset fallback; `moveZipBooksToFolder` fails silently
on a refused folder name; `LibraryView` navigates to a refused rename's path; orphan
adoption takes folder spelling from disk (repaired at next launch); the rename sheet
cannot start a normalization-only rename; reading positions in chapters that regained
dropped paragraphs may shift back by up to the restored length (T06); log content policy
for Q07 is still open; agent commits carry the subagent model's attribution trailer.

**Open follow-ups noted by the September 29 agents** (not yet fixed): DAISY: a damaged
DAISY text book still imports "successfully" because `generateCacheContent` swallows
extraction errors (S02/I06); `importDAISYAudioZip` leaves empty package folders after a
failed conversion; consecutive inline fragments become separate paragraphs; no warning
channel exists for partial content (logged as `DAISY_INCOMPLETE_CONTENT`). Settings: slider
ranges are still written out in the settings and reader views instead of referencing
`SettingsPolicy`; `@AppStorage` copies read outside views refresh only after relaunch. Index
recovery: edits made while recovering do not carry into an index restored by Try Again;
Change Location and Reset to Default share one guard that was tested only through Rebuild
Directory. Plain text: direct-path text is now normalized like the cache, so `.txt` positions
and skipped-heading IDs shift after the 3.0.6 rebuild (T06); CRLF files still read each
line as a paragraph (T08); `#hashtag` becomes a heading. Silent chapters: a pre-rendered next
chapter can play stale audio if its skips or substitutions change after rendering (P03);
`stop()` does not cancel a running pre-render; `SpeechService` has no defined result for
empty text. Hosted runs print macOS `AUCrashHandler` "proxy error" lines from speech
synthesizer teardown; they are not failures.

**Open follow-ups noted by the September 30 agents** (not yet fixed): the substitution
editor's Test button can use a stale compiled regex (`runTest()` borrows the shared
service's per-rule cache; test with a throwaway service instead); other hosted suites still
post real VoiceOver announcements (a silent default poster under XCTest would stop that);
the footer says "1 rules"; Delete and Reset to Defaults have no confirmation or undo.

the migration alert instead (`FolderSafetyTests`, `LibraryMoveTransactionTests`,
`DeletionSafetyTests.testFailedPackageDeletionKeepsBookInLibrary`; inert models or the index

test's precondition (27 headings in the previous cache) no longer matches any available copy.

once the extraction folder is removed; the visible Cancel button in the import overlay landed the
same night (it stops ZIP batches only); extraction now happens in an "Archive Extraction" folder inside the data directory.

**Open follow-ups noted by the routing work (October 3)**: a cold launch with a Finder open was
not verified in the real app (it relies on AppKit's documented event order); when only readers are
open and the book is already in the library, a Library window shows first because SwiftUI re-shows
one before the delegate hears of the file; `ContentView.onOpenBook` still uses the "book-reader"
literal; a Library window with a sheet up cannot present a second panel; with no key Library window
the first registered one is chosen rather than the frontmost.

**Open follow-ups noted by the cache-fingerprint work (October 3)**: EPUB metadata write-back rewrites
the source file, so that book's cache is rebuilt once afterwards with the same text (the write-back could
re-fingerprint the file); `separateSharedTMBPackages` leaves the fingerprint in place when it resets the
other analysis fields (harmless, the cache is rebuilt); audio indexes are not fingerprinted; `md5Hash` and
the library title are not updated when a replaced source is detected; a text book whose source exists but
cannot be read is loaded by the statistics pass once per startup; the first-launch hashing pass over the
real library (about 627 books) and whether copying the data directory keeps file dates were not verified.

now spoken as asterisks in every format; a cross-format rule could speak it once as a section break.

Still open after the now-playing-followups merge (October 10): `AsyncBridge.waitForResult`, waited on from the main thread, makes the Thread Performance Checker print a priority-inversion backtrace for each silent audiobook a test makes (110 in one run; `Task.detached(priority: Task.currentPriority)` there would end them, board #1581); `pause(playingPauseSound:)` still tells Now Playing "paused" when it paused nothing, so a pause reaching a stopped reader takes the AirPods (#1498's shape, rare now); `showEditedMetadata` (`ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates.swift`) guards with playing or paused, so a finished book holding the controls keeps its old title after a library rename (`holdsMediaControls` would match the saves' rule); `clearNowPlaying(ownerID:)` takes nothing down for a reader shown but not active, as a stop from behind another window; and a design question for the user: focusing a window that never played, while another book shows paused, still sends the next AirPods Play to the focused window's book.
Still open after the full-summary merge (October 10): the reader toolbar's Where Am I? button can no longer reach the full summary by quick clicks, since the three-second rule is gone; a "Full Summary" VoiceOver action on it would restore that (board #1593, `ReaderView.swift`); W pressed within 0.3 seconds of a Where Am I announcement's natural end records the book as not playing, and the pending resume then plays the book under the new announcement (older than this branch, `whereAmIAnnouncementFinished()`); `FullSummaryReaderTests`' fixture helpers repeat the private ones in `WhereAmIAnnouncementEndTests`; `OffscreenTestWindow` never presents SwiftUI sheets, so no hosted test can see one open; `LibraryInfoSpeaker` repeats Where Am I's utterance handling (a live check, a refused synthesizer replaced), which one speaker of short announcements could serve.
Still open after FR11's branch A2 merge (October 10): the removal dialog and its completion message still use the library's words for an episode or article ("Moved … to the Trash"), and an item whose package was kept does not yet say its progress in its row, both for branch C (`RemovalWording.swift`, `FeedLibraryPresentation.swift`); an index row whose package was deleted outside TextMouth stays on the shelf, which a launch repair could drop; a crash inside the restore leaves a hidden `.import-…tmb` or `.replaced-…tmb` folder in the feed's folder that nothing removes (index recovery skips hidden names, so it never becomes a second row) (`FeedItemPackage.swift`); `writePendingBookInfoNow`'s doc says to call it off the main thread, and the removal calls it on the main thread, briefly; a restored book keeps its old title when the feed has since renamed the item (`FeedEpisodeImporter.swift`); Unsubscribe now strands kept packages too, for branch D (#1576); kept packages accumulate with nothing to remove them in bulk (`FeedLifecycle.swift`); a test through the app's own deletion path for a feed item, which the injected deletion hid, would have caught #1591.
Still open after the October 10 documentation pass: `FeedWording.unsubscribed(title:bookCount:)` still announces that an unsubscribed feed's downloads "stay in the library", and the Unsubscribe question still says they stay in the folder (for branch D, with #1576); at launch `FeedIndexFiles.read` reads each feed's index from the main thread with no check that it is on this Mac, so in a cloud data directory a feed index only in the cloud may make the main thread wait for its download (unverified; passed to the A1 reviewer); Remove from Library… on an episode acts as Delete Download while its question and last message still speak of a book (for branch C).
Still open after FR11's branch B merge (October 10): branch C must replace the old controls and then remove `FeedSettings.keepEntries` and `autoDownload` (their stored keys kept for the move), `Feed.keepEntries(under:)`, `FeedCenter.setKeepEntries`, `FeedThresholdWording.keepEntries`, and `FeedSettingsWording.autoDownload`, listing `feedKeepEntries` and `feedAutoDownload` in `ConfigService.Portability.retiredStoreKeys` (with the line of `ShortcutConfigFileTests` that pins that list); item rows do not yet say a waiting item's reason after "Waiting to Download" (`FeedLibraryWording.itemRowLabel`, `FeedRows.swift`), and a waiting item offers Download but not Cancel Download (`FeedCommands.swift`); `FeedLifecycle.removeDue` is not registered as storage work, so a data-directory move is not held off (as before); the `.constrainedNetwork` download message is no longer reached (`FeedDownloadManager.swift`); the README still describes `feedKeepEntries` and `feedAutoDownload`.
Still open after the playback-robustness merge (October 10): after a library rename a paused book that is not shown keeps its old title in Now Playing's cache, surfacing if the shown book closes (`NowPlayingService.swift`, `ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates.swift`); the playback watchdog's idle recovery (`DIRECT_WATCHDOG_RECOVERY`) has no cap of its own, so an utterance that starts but goes idle before its first word would be spoken again at one place without end (never seen in a log) (`SpeechService.swift`); a Play during Where Am I? for a book paused before it is dropped rather than held and started as the announcement ends (`ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift`); a sleep fade on direct speech goes on from the last word the voice reported, so it may repeat a word.
Still open after the feed-index-review merge (October 10): in the rare race where a feed's index leaves the Mac between the launch check and the read, that session's bookmark and setting changes reach only the records and the next launch's index rows win (`FeedIndex.swift`); Clean Up Transcript is still offered for episodes, Book Info names an episode's folder as the top level, and the Remove menu title still reads "Remove from Library..." on feed items (for branch C); the Remove failure heading says "books" for feed items (`LibraryViewModel+BookOperations.swift`); the summary bar's "(N books not yet opened)" counts episodes as books (`LibrarySubviews.swift`); Delete Folder's question names a feed only when the folder itself is the feed's (`RemovalWording.swift`); after a crash mid-return, a moved package whose MD5 matches a library book is not adopted (`FileImportService.swift`); the not-downloaded reason says "library index" when it waits for a feed's index (`DataDirectoryAvailability.swift`); `FeedCenter.unsubscribe` called alone still strands downloads, only the runner returns them (branch D). (The edited article's content.md under the automatic rules, a record-less feed folder from another Mac, and a no-op unsubscribe went to `feedfollowups`.)
Still open after the menu-and-window-shortcuts merge (October 10): Space in a sheet is still Play / Pause, though with Full Keyboard Access Space presses the focused button (`KeyBinding.dialogKeys`); a hidden menu's items still answer their keys, so any future fixed menu key must use `menuCommandKey` (`MenuCommands.swift`, `MenuVisibilityManager.swift`'s header should say so); three list-joining helpers do one job (`LibraryCloudWording.list`, `AudioFilterChain.list`, `ReadingStatsTile.spokenList`); `TextEditingKeys`' Edit-menu keys repeat part of `KeyBinding.systemCommands`; the `everyFieldCustomized` config fixture has no entry in the new object form; `ReadingCheckpointTests.testClosingTheReaderWritesItsLastCheckpoint` failed once under load and passed alone (for AGENTS.md's load-sensitive list).
Still open after FR11's branch C merge (October 10): `FeedItemPolicyTests` (about line 325) should tear down with `await FeedTestWorld.finish(next)` once `fix/feed-followups` merges (board #1695); Feed Info does not redraw when the global settings change while it is open (as with the days before); Speak Info and Command-I do nothing on a feed item's row that is not downloaded, so a kept place is heard only in the row's label (`LibraryInfoRow.swift`); a waiting reason shows on screen starting lower case, worded to follow "Waiting to Download," in speech (`FeedRows.swift`); the user may want other words for a number of 1 ("newest 1 downloaded"); `TranscriptionPresentation.cleanUp(_:library:window:)` does not itself refuse a feed item, though nothing offers it one.
Still open after the feed-followups merge (October 10): downloading an article again writes its text anew, so an old edit the rules kept is replaced then (`FeedEpisodeImporter.restoreBook`; keeping the earlier content.md as an `.edited-` copy when it differs would close it); an accepted Edit Text edit leaves no marker, so an article edited but never opened still goes whole under the rules (only edits made before feed items lost Edit Text); Delete Episode leaves a `.replaced-<name>` folder a crash set aside until Unsubscribe removes the feed's folder; `isPackageContentName` (`FileImportService+Packages.swift`) still spells bookInfo.json and content.md literally; `updateLocalVoiceAndSpeed` stores a speed without checking its format's range, so an article given 1.5 reads back as 50 from bookInfo.json (no control sends such a value today).
Still open after the later October 10 documentation pass: Feed Info's folder help says "the books already downloaded stay where they are", false since A1, as does `FeedCenter.setFolder`'s comment; `FeedItemPolicyControls`' note says a change "takes effect" after ten seconds though the list changes at once, and the Only-the-newest hint says played items are left out though a played item still downloaded stays listed; the Record window's button hints name fixed keys whatever key is set (`RecordingWording.swift`, sent to `whereamikeys`); `LogContentPolicyTests.eventTags` pins none of the feed tags added since A1; Library Not Available always blames "the library index", even when only a feed's index or folder is in the cloud (`DataDirectoryAvailability.swift` about 234); Delete Folder… on a feed's folder can begin "contains 0 books" (`LibraryView.swift` about 899); the Move dialog's title counts episodes that will not move (`LibrarySheetViews.swift` about 505-539); a mixed Remove announces nothing when it succeeds, and a failed Remove of an episode still calls it a book; in Recently Opened and the Reading List the summary bar's unopened, cloud, and kept counts still call episodes books (`LibrarySubviews.swift` about 112-191); Speak Info says "Not in a folder" for a feed item whose record is not loaded; a new download of an article loses the text the rules kept, with no `.edited-` copy; with Move to Trash on, Unsubscribe puts the feed's own folder, named by an identifier, in the Trash, and an article's Delete Download makes two Trash items; the README's "Try Again, which Return presses" on Library Not Available is probably false, since that screen is not a sheet (unverified).
Still open after the feed-download-progress merge (October 10): whether Cancel should also stop the rule's downloads waiting for a network or for room is the user's call (`humanQuestions/2026-10-09.md`, question 5); `FeedRows.swift` (lines 13 and 160) and `FeedStore.swift` (line 90) comments still describe a download's progress redrawing rows or being written (#1742); `besideDownloadPercentage` (`LibrarySubviews.swift`, `FeedLibraryPresentation.swift`) is now a misleading name for feed items; rows cannot tell a queued download from the one under way; a closed Library window keeps the section's 5-second timer while downloads run; a resumed download's earlier bytes inflate the byte rate; an article's page fetch shows no progress before "adding it to the library"; `FeedDownloadTests.testADownloadThatStopsPartwayGoesOnFromWhereItStopped` failed once under load (resume data with no bytes) and passed alone, for AGENTS.md's load-sensitive list.
Still open after the reading-list-followups merge (October 10): a chapter download that finishes while Where Am I? speaks starts the chapter under the announcement (`ReaderViewModel+CloudDownload.swift`, `downloadDidFinish(.chapter)`; sent to `whereamikeys`); while the next book downloads, the finished book's Play says "Still starting" for the whole download, where "still downloading" might be clearer; the menu and keyboard moves say "Moved X above Y" while the VoiceOver actions say "X, 3 of 12", and the keyboard says nothing at either end of the list (one wording is the user's call); an open routed to a reader window with no Library window open is not refused while the library moves (`LibraryWindowRouter.swift`, read from the code); the legacy test target's `#else` branches in six more files cannot compile there; `PDFExtractionDiagnostics.listed(_:)` and `AudioFilterChain.list(_:)` are two private copies of one list-joiner; the 5-second elapsed-time rule exists twice (#1460); the edit-mode bar's "N of M selected" can count books the list no longer shows after other changes; Import Book's hint does not say that at the Reading List the books also join the list.
Still open after the where-am-i-and-keys merge (October 10): `FeedEndToEndTests.testAnEpisodeFromItsFeedToTheDeletionOfItsDownload` and `FeedLifecycleTests.testADownloadOpenInAReaderWaitsUntilItCloses` race under load since FR11's branch B moved file removals off the main thread (each checks an item's state the moment its row is gone, while the state settles a step later; board #1821), for `fix/feed-policy-review`, and on AGENTS.md's load-sensitive list until then; Play/Pause during Where Am I? while an audiobook's chapter still downloads ends the announcement and leaves the reader waiting, where a text book's waiting play pauses; the Now Playing session's `isCurrentlyPlaying` does not count a play waiting for its chapter's speech or download, so the AirPods leaving, a recording, or another book starting do not pause it; Edit Text taken during Where Am I? leaves a book that was playing stopped (older); a sleep timer started with "start playback" during Where Am I? goes through `togglePlayPause()` and leaves the book paused; a package move while a downloaded chapter is held restarts it even if Where Am I? still speaks (rare); a Play in the moment after Where Am I? ends with a chapter held starts it at once, with the play sound; `FullSummaryReaderTests` and `SpeechPreparationCacheTests` still wait out the real end-sound delay; `DAISYPackageImportTests` depends on the front window as the Add Feed test did (#1777); `action(holding:in:)` is used only by tests; the legacy `TextMouthTests` target is not in the scheme, so its tests never run.
Still open after FR11's branch D merge (October 10): OPML import counts an unsubscribed feed as "subscribed already" and leaves it unchecked, and export lists unsubscribed feeds (`OPMLImportSheet.swift`, #1814); `cancelAll(of:)` and both `remove(_:)` (FeedDownloadManager, FeedStore, FeedFetchState) have no app caller now, and their docs, `spokenProgress()`'s, and FeedStore's class doc still describe the old Unsubscribe (#1815); with every feed unsubscribed, Refresh All says "There are no feeds to refresh." and its hint says it checks every feed (#1816); the Unsubscribe question says "places, bookmarks, and transcripts" while its announcement says "places and bookmarks"; `finishedBookKind(of:)` counts a moved episode as an episode only while its record keeps the item (bookInfo.json's feed link could serve); records are written whole from memory, so a second Mac running at the time keeps checking until its next launch and its next write of the record undoes the other Mac's Unsubscribe; a crash between the index write and a kept package's removal in `deleteKeptPackages(of:)` leaves the place kept (nothing lost); Move to Library's success announcement may be cut off as its sheet closes; `feedItemsLeft(titles:)` could mention Move to Library…; two test docs describe the old Unsubscribe (`LibraryInfoWordingTests` about 198, `FeedRecordTests` about 388).
Noticed by the P07 and P08 design note (October 10): the player's asset lacks precise timing and `seekPlayer` clamps to an estimated duration that can be short for some MP3s (`AudioPlaybackService.makeItem(playing:)`, read from the code); m4b chapter starts drift 1.4 seconds by chapter 30 (`chapterStartTime`); `jumpToTime`'s comment describes a convention the code does not follow; Stats' Recently Finished keys rows by book ID, so the user's list repeats one ID nine times; a looping book stopped by its timer says "Finished"; Go to Percent 100 then Play counts a finish (`clampedNavigationPosition`); each repeated finish restarts a feed item's delete-after-played clock (`FeedLifecycle.bookDidFinish`); end-of-book trace lines never reach the debug log; Go to Time's three fields are known to VoiceOver only by their placeholders (`JumpToTimePanel`).

From team audiofmt (October 18): the newly converted formats' books are titled by their file names, where `afinfo -i` could read their tags (`ImportAudioConverter.swift`, question 7); a downmixed book's level could be raised as far as its peak allows (question 10); `Tone.wav` and `tone.wav` in one playlist, one file on a case-insensitive volume, become two tracks, so playlist entries should be deduplicated by file identity (`M3UPlaylist.swift`, board #3163); Cancel cannot stop the `afinfo` check that runs before a conversion, seconds at most; a ZIP batch's failure alert is titled "Error" even when it added books; a linked `manifest.json` in an imported folder is copied as a link (`importAudioFolderWithReport`, textfmt's #3147); the import fuzz does not cover the converted formats (`ImportFuzzTests`); WAVs holding ADPCM or μ-law (question 8) and trusted folders for playlists (question 9) wait for the user.
From team export (October 19): check in Word that its navigation pane lists the RTF export's headings (`RTFExportWriter.swift`); a .doc loses U+202A to U+202E and U+FFFD through Apple's RTF reader, while the RTF keeps them (`WordDocExportWriter.swift`); when the system gives no scratch folder on the target's volume, the exclusive copy across volumes removes what is at the target after a copy that failed for a reason other than "already exists", which could be another app's file landing there in that instant (`copyExclusively` in `BookExportService.swift`); contents entries with no heading of their own, the navigation annotations, could become HTML anchors and RTF bookmarks (`ExportDocumentWalker.swift`); three older escapers could use `XMLEscaping` (`FeedOPML.swift`, `FeedTextConverter.swift`, `SSMLReader.swift`); swift-markdown parses recursively, so a content.md nested thousands deep could overflow the stack, in the reader too (`MarkdownParser.swift`); progress counts files, not progress within a long book (`BookExportService.swift`); an audiobook's Transcript.md could take the same Convert to choice (`BookExportKind.convertsText`).
From team braille (October 19 and 20): move through a braille book by its pages, whose numbers the analyzer already finds (`BraillePageAnalyzer.swift`, question 3 of `humanQuestions/2026-10-19.md`); read a book again with another code (`Book.brailleTable`); offer the Mac's own liblouis tables, VoiceOver's liblouis 3.34.0 set in `/System/Library/ScreenReader/BrailleTables/LiblouisBrailleTranslator.brailletable`, in the Braille tab (question 4), which the catalog's accepted tables would then include; pass emphasis to liblouis when exporting, so italics become UEB indicators (`TextMouthBraille/main.c`, `BrailleTranslator.swift`, question 2); store a PEF's `dc:language` (`FileImportService.swift`); running heads, Roman-numeral page numbers, and words divided at a line's end are not read yet; lists: one that begins a page right after a paragraph that filled the page before still joins it (about 1 place in 31), consecutive one-line items in cell 1 merge, items at depth 3 or deeper read as headings, and BANA's list page-break rules (8.3.5b to d) and nested-list margins (8.5.1b) are not followed (`BraillePageAnalyzer.swift`, `BrailleLayout.swift`); a word longer than a line is cut with no hyphen or continuation indicator, though never inside a number (`BrailleLayout.swift`); Stop during a long translation waits for the helper, within its time limit (`BrailleTranslator.swift`); a book's last heading can be split by a page break; XcodeGen orders the three copy-files phases differently on each run (`project.yml`); Finder's Open With for `.pef` (`Info.plist`); unchecked by hand: the braille options and the Braille tab with VoiceOver, ten Settings tabs at 780 points, a real embosser or display, a real BARD or Bookshare BRF, and `make build` signing the helper.

From racefix (October 21): a load that succeeds while its package moves can lose what it reads last for the session (tags, manifest pauses, transcript, EPUB metadata); Play during a move, for a book not playing when the move began, reads the moving files (`pauseAudioForPackageMove()` sets aside only playing audio; `isPackageMoving` can guard it); a missing audiobook folder is reported as a damaged file, and a missing EPUB's message reads out its full path (`BookContentAnalysisService.swift:553`); in a cloud library, a move made on another Mac is not announced, so that load still fails; a tracks book's manifest look is storage work (`BookManifestEditing.swift:895`), so any storage hold keeps its reader on Loading; `LibrarySessionLeaseTests.testALeaseEndedDuringARefreshKeepsNoFile` took 6.3 s against 5-second timeouts once on a busy Mac.

MacBrailleTableCheckTests.testFrenchGermanAndSpanishPassAndRoundTrip reads the helper's memory every millisecond while it runs, and three short runs can end before the first reading (0 bytes): make the measurement independent of timing. For iOS phase 1, from the reviews:
- the lenient XML reader's <!ENTITY scan misses UTF-16 documents and refuses the string in a comment or CDATA;
- iOS cannot save Book Info edits into an EPUB (updateOPFMetadata throws);
- VoiceOver focus on iOS is a stand-in;
- empty iOS bodies leave their buttons visible: the OPML file choosers, Storage's Choose Folder, New from Text's Open in Editor, and Quit on the library-unavailable screen;
- ImportOrigin(window:) is nil on iOS;
- Copy Details always says copied;
- a recording start failure is not spoken;
- PlaybackMemoryGuard.quitAtOnce() still calls _exit on iOS;
- scope NowPlayingService's iOS aliases AudioDeviceID and AudioObjectPropertyListenerBlock;
- WatchedProcess's Limits, KillReason, and Outcome are copied in its iOS stand-in;
- SoundEffectService plays sounds from /System/Library/Sounds, which iOS does not have;
- the iOS app shares the Mac's bundle identifier;
- the formats iOS cannot read yet, routed by `FORMAT-LIBRARIES-RESEARCH-2026-11-04.md`, Part 1:
  - Word 97-2003 by FMT47's own reader, and no .doc export on iOS (RTF or DOCX instead);
  - RTF and RTFD by `NSAttributedString`;
  - OpenDocument and Word 2003 XML by own XML readers;
  - Ogg by Xiph's BSD decoders, vendored into the iOS app only (the Mac keeps afconvert; the user, November 4: "libraries compiled into the IOS app but not Mac OS is fine"), with libvorbis at commit 1b75110b or later;
  - braille by `AXBrailleTranslator` if a device test shows it gives an app tables, else liblouis;
  - the rarer audio by iOS's own decoders.

## S — Storage, persistence, and recovery

- [x] **S01 — Recover unreadable library indexes without replacing the evidence.** *Repaired September 29; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [LibraryPersistenceService](TextMouth/Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift) `loadFrom` returns an empty `Library` on decoding/read failure. Startup repair can subsequently save that replacement. Preserve/quarantine the failing index, report the error, and offer recovery with an explicit state. Acceptance: truncated JSON, unreadable storage, and unknown enum values leave the original bytes recoverable and do not silently reset folders/order/state.

- [x] **S02 — Make every import commit conditional on readable content and durable metadata.** *Repaired October 2; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [FileImportService](TextMouth/Services/FileImportService.swift) has import paths that suppress analysis and `bookInfo.json` write failures, then return a successful `Book`. Original deletion can follow. Stage a package, validate its content and metadata, then publish success; clean up failed staging. Acceptance: corrupt documents/audio, full disk, and denied metadata writes preserve the source and never publish an unreadable “successful” import. F08 only addresses failures reported by the batch operation.

- [x] **S03 — Verify legacy migration destinations before deleting source content.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. `FileImportService.migrateBookToTMB` skips copying when a destination already exists, removes the source, and suppresses metadata/cache failures. Verify content identity and commit metadata first; retain a recovery copy on uncertainty. Acceptance: an existing different destination and a metadata-write failure both preserve the original file.

- [x] **S04 — Coordinate imports, moves, deletion, extraction, and data-directory migration.** P1 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 5 · Risk. [Library persistence](TextMouth/Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift), [migration](TextMouth/ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift), and the serial import queue use separate executors. Writers can retain old paths while other writers resolve the changing global root. Add a storage transaction/generation boundary and quiesce affected work. Acceptance: move/delete/migrate during a deliberately blocked import, cache rebuild, and debounced save; no files reappear or land under the wrong root. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **S05 — Merge completed analysis with the latest durable book state.** *Repaired October 2; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. `LibraryPersistenceService.saveContentAnalysis` merges a pending snapshot if present, otherwise uses the older `Book` passed into the parser. A newer snapshot may already have finished writing. Merge only analysis fields into the latest state, using revisions to reject stale work. Acceptance: finalize a newer bookmark/position/metadata write before releasing an older analysis result, then reload the package and verify all newer fields.

- [x] **S06 — Keep Remove from Library Only removed across pending saves and restart.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [Book operations](TextMouth/ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+BookOperations.swift) suppress metadata-removal errors, while queued saves and background analysis can recreate the `bookInfo.json` used for orphan adoption. Introduce a durable exclusion/tombstone or equivalent retirement state. Acceptance: remove with a queued write, open reader, failed metadata removal, and active extraction; restart and rebuild must honor the removal while retaining the content file.

- [x] **S07 — Detect filesystem-equivalent folder-name collisions.** *Repaired September 28; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. [Library](TextMouth/Models/Library.swift) compares folder paths as case-sensitive strings, while common macOS volumes do not. `Reports` and `reports` can become separate rows sharing storage. Use volume-aware equivalence, including Unicode normalization, and handle existing collisions. Acceptance: case-sensitive and case-insensitive volumes, case-only renames, and composed/decomposed names produce distinct safe outcomes.

- [x] **S08 — Validate the entire subtree when removing a folder and reparenting its children.** *Repaired September 28; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. `Library.removeFolder(movingContentsTo:)` lacks the destination collision/depth checks used by other folder operations. Validate child-name conflicts, self/descendant destinations, and maximum depth before publication. Acceptance: duplicate child names and a subtree that would exceed four levels leave both disk and model unchanged.

- [x] **S09 — Surface persistence and cleanup failures consistently.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. Library/config/statistics writes, some `Book.saveBookInfo` calls, and original-file deletion report only to logs or use `try?`. Carry actionable errors to the owning view and distinguish “readable in memory” from “saved.” Acceptance: unavailable storage during edits, shutdown flush, and original deletion yields accurate status and a retry path without false success.

- [x] **S10 — Harden migration publication against destination changes and aliases.** P2 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 4 · Risk. [DataDirectoryMigrator](TextMouth/Services/TextMouthPaths.swift) checks destination emptiness before a potentially long copy, then removes that destination at publication. Revalidate/coordinate publication and explicitly support or reject source/destination aliases. Acceptance: create a destination sentinel while copying and test an independently located source-root symlink; no newly arrived data is overwritten. F20 handles containment, not concurrent publication. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

## I — Import orchestration and audio packages

- [x] **I01 — Queue import decisions as well as file operations.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [Import](TextMouth/ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Import.swift) and [duplicate handling](TextMouth/ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Duplicates.swift) store one pending duplicate, original URL, and ZIP-folder request. Serial disk work does not wait for these decisions; later completions can replace them. Use a queued import-result state machine. Acceptance: several duplicate/ask-to-delete/multi-book ZIP imports preserve every decision, URL, and open-after-import intent. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **I02 — Import loose DAISY packages with their dependent files.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. Generic import copies individual files. An OPF needs its DTBook/SMIL/audio siblings; a directory package encountered inside a generic ZIP also needs one package-level traversal. Acceptance: loose OPF, NCC-based folder, and a ZIP containing multiple packaged books remain readable after the extraction/source directory disappears, with each book imported once.

- [x] **I03 — Preserve DAISY audio that crosses files within one navigation section.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 5 · Confirmed. [DAISYAudioConverter](TextMouth/Parsers/DAISYParser+Audio.swift) builds one segment from the selected clip/file for each navigation reference. Later clips in other files are copied but can be absent from the manifest's playback sequence. Represent a logical section as multiple clips. Acceptance: one heading spanning three audio files plays every clip in order with correct navigation and duration. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **I04 — Validate actual media duration against segment ranges.** *Repaired October 2; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. [AudioBookParser](TextMouth/Parsers/AudioBookParser.swift) now rejects malformed references/ranges, but explicit positive end times are still trusted without checking the media. Verify supported decodable media, start/end bounds, and tolerances for encoder padding. Acceptance: corrupt media, a start past EOF, and an end far past EOF fail clearly; legitimate DAISY padding remains supported.

- [x] **I05 — Bound archive and recursive-import resource use.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Risk. [FileImportService](TextMouth/Services/FileImportService.swift) has no explicit total expansion, entry-count, nesting, or cancellation budget. Confirm ZIPFoundation's current path protections separately; this audit does not assert an unverified ZIP-slip vulnerability. Acceptance: large/nested archives terminate predictably, preserve the source, clean staging, and keep the interface responsive.

- [x] **I06 — Report partial EPUB/DAISY chapter extraction.** *EPUB and the shared report repaired September 30; DAISY and DOCX adoption of the report remains a follow-up; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. Chapter loops in [EPUBParser](TextMouth/Parsers/EPUBParser.swift), [MarkdownCacheService](TextMouth/Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift), and DAISY conversion skip missing/unreadable members. Returning the surviving chapters can look like complete success. Preserve tolerant reading where useful while surfacing omissions and preventing source deletion. Acceptance: remove the middle chapter from a fixture and obtain a precise warning/failed-import result.

- [x] **I07 — Preserve all text in DAISY 2.02 inline fragments.** *Repaired September 29; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. `DAISYParser.convertDAISY202FragmentElement` descends into only the first child of some inline containers; siblings and surrounding text can disappear. The user's pre-existing edits to this file were left intact. Acceptance: a SMIL fragment pointing to a span with text plus multiple emphasized children retains all words once, in order.

## P — Playback, navigation, and lifecycle

- [x] **P01 — Integrate sleep-timer boundaries with real playback.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 5 · Confirmed / Design. Reader completion callbacks call `notifyDocumentEnd` at chapter ends and then advance unconditionally. Heading notifications mainly follow explicit navigation, while the timer deliberately waits for two heading-boundary notifications. Define chapter/document semantics and the first-boundary rule before changing them. Acceptance: natural TTS and audio playback, manual jumps, end-of-book loop, and continue-next stop at the promised boundary without starting another chapter after expiry. *Repaired October 3 for chapter and book ends; the End of current heading timer still never fires during normal playback (board #787). See the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **P02 — Carry continue-next autoplay intent into the next reader.** *Repaired October 2; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [Reader playback](TextMouth/ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift) announces “Starting” and posts `.continueToNextBook`; [ContentView](TextMouth/Views/ContentView.swift) opens a window, but the new reader has no matching load-completion autoplay intent. Route a consumable request through creation/loading, including already-open readers and no available library window. Acceptance: finishing a book actually starts the next eligible book exactly once.

- [x] **P03 — Invalidate pre-rendered speech when its inputs change.** *Repaired October 2; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Risk. [SpeechService](TextMouth/Services/SpeechService.swift) and reader transforms can retain rendered audio while voice, substitutions, punctuation pauses, or skipped sections change. Give rendered artifacts a complete input signature and reject stale completions. Acceptance: block rendering, change each input, release it, and compare spoken audio selection/offsets with the current text and settings.

- [x] **P04 — Observe AVPlayer item failures and expose recoverable playback errors.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [AudioPlaybackService](TextMouth/Services/AudioPlaybackService.swift) marks playback active without observing item failure/status transitions. Missing or corrupt files can leave an apparently playing reader silent. Acceptance: delete/corrupt a track after indexing, press Play, and receive accurate stopped/error state with a usable retry or repair action.

- [x] **P05 — Prove chapter completion is delivered once per playback generation.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Risk. Audio clip-boundary and natural-end observations can converge at the same endpoint. Verify observer removal, seeking, chapter reload, and stale asynchronous notifications using the actual callback path. Acceptance: no chapter is skipped or advanced twice when clip end equals file end or Stop/Seek races with completion. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **P06 — Advance safely when filtering removes all speakable text.** *Repaired September 29; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. `ReaderViewModel.speakCurrentChapter` checks raw empty text before filtering; all-skipped/substituted-away text can reach speech without a useful completion. Define and test the empty-result transition, including all remaining chapters being skipped. Acceptance: no stuck Playing state, infinite loop, or unearned statistics session.

- [x] **P07 — Store a deliberate end-of-book position.** Merged 57d04ab9, October 10 (see the merge entry above). P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Design. Completion commonly saves the last reported word/time rather than the exact endpoint. Decide how completed books show 100%, how Replay behaves, and whether loop counts as “finished.” Acceptance: text, single-file audio, segmented audio, and resume-after-finish agree across the library and reader.
  *Design note merged October 10 (12a9ed4b, `END-AND-JUMP-RESEARCH-2026-10-10.md`); its questions wait in `humanQuestions/2026-10-10.md` (11 to 21).*
  *Answered October 10: yes to (11) an exact end, finished across relaunches, (14) a rewind not counting
  again, and (16) the books already counted put at their end once. Changed: (12) "yes except the reader,
  those controls with the info should still be there": the library row, Speak Info, Where Am I?, and Copy
  Reading Position say Finished, and the reader's own controls keep their percent and time; (13) "I mean
  maybe this could be a setting but not by default, it should just say "fished" if there not enabled to
  move to the next book or start over... Maybe this mode should be added to the menu with the modes for
  what to do at the end of a book?": by default Play on a finished book says "Finished" and does nothing
  else (no replay, no count), and a new end-of-book choice beside Stop, Loop, and Continue to Next Book
  makes Play start a finished book over; (15) "yes, only one finish per book for now": Books Finished
  counts each book once.*

- [x] **P08 — Define one time-coordinate convention for audiobook jumps.** Merged 50cdd78c, October 10 (see the merge entry above). P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Design. [Jump code](TextMouth/ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Jump.swift) uses media time for audio while displayed total/remaining time can be speed-adjusted listening time. Decide which coordinate an entered time means and label it. Acceptance: at 0.5x, 1x, and 2x, a time copied from the display round-trips to the expected position.
  *Design note merged October 10 (12a9ed4b, `END-AND-JUMP-RESEARCH-2026-10-10.md`); its questions wait in `humanQuestions/2026-10-10.md` (11 to 21).*

  taking 1:02:03, 62:03, 3723, or 12:04.3, (20) Copy Reading Position adding the place's time, and (21) a
  text book's times at its words per minute, labelled. Changed: (18) "Maybe, maybe a setting for more
  info, make sure it works on both audio and text.": naming the speed beside time left and total ("3:44:01
  left at 2.0x") is a setting, off by default, for audiobooks and text books alike.*

- [ ] **P09 — Validate direct/rendered speech transitions on supported voices and routes.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 5 · Manual / Risk. [AudioEngineService](TextMouth/Services/AudioEngineService.swift), SpeechService cancellation, timeout, background buffer callbacks, route changes, and Swift concurrency warnings need lifecycle coverage beyond pure policy tests. Acceptance: repeated pause/resume/seek/voice changes and disconnect/reconnect preserve the cursor, stop old audio, and release render resources.

- [x] **P10 — Refresh open readers after shared book edits and moves.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. Readers retain their own `Book`, metadata, and audio-path snapshots. Library edits/moves do not comprehensively refresh these snapshots. Publish revisioned updates and coordinate active playback before path changes. Acceptance: move/rename/edit a book from another window, then continue playback, edit a bookmark, and reopen; metadata and file paths stay current. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

## T — Text extraction, indexing, and coordinates

- [x] **T01 — Validate persisted content-index structure and media paths.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [BookContentIndex](TextMouth/Models/BookContentIndex.swift) validity largely checks version/format/kind. Reconstruction trusts counts, offsets, audio durations, and paths; `compactMap` can shorten chapter-path arrays independently of chapters. Add structural/containment/finite-value checks, media existence checks, and allocation bounds before the fast path. Acceptance: malformed but decodable indexes fall back to safe re-analysis without crashes, outside-file access, or chapter misalignment.

- [x] **T02 — Preserve literal plain text through the cache pipeline.** *Repaired September 29; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. TXT cache content is parsed as Markdown, so literal Markdown or HTML-looking syntax can change or vanish. Define an escaped literal-text representation that still preserves the intended plain-text heading heuristics. Acceptance: source examples containing backticks, tags, asterisks, brackets, and angle-bracket comparisons retain their words and order.

- [x] **T03 — Preserve HTML block/table boundaries and escaped text.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [HTMLToMarkdownConverter](TextMouth/Parsers/HTMLToMarkdownConverter.swift) emits raw text into Markdown and flattens some structures; adjacent cells/blocks can run together and source punctuation can become markup. Preserve semantic separators, preformatted whitespace, and literal text. Acceptance: nested divs, tables, pre/code, and markup characters retain distinct words in final spoken text.

- [x] **T04 — Fail or explicitly report malformed XML instead of returning partial documents.** *DAISY half repaired September 29; DOCX half September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. DOCX and DAISY SAX helpers commonly ignore `XMLParser.parse()` and `parserError`. NCC HTML also needs deliberate tolerant-HTML parsing rather than an XML declaration prepended to malformed HTML. Acceptance: truncated XML and ordinary non-XHTML NCC markup produce either correct complete text or an actionable failure/partial-content warning.

- [x] **T05 — Finish DOCX structural fidelity.** P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed / Risk. [DOCX numbering](TextMouth/Parsers/DOCXParser+Numbering.swift) appends level formats without honoring sparse/out-of-order `ilvl` identities. Styles and converter state also need fixtures for inheritance, numbering overrides, and nested tables. Acceptance: compare declared levels, continuation/start values, inherited headings, and nested cells against source structure. Preserve the existing tested policy for lists interrupted by non-list paragraphs unless deliberately revised. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **T06 — Relocate reading positions, bookmarks, and skipped headings after re-extraction.** Merged cf542090, October 13 (see the merge entry above). P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 5 · Design / Risk. Numeric offsets and heading IDs can change when text/chapter extraction improves. Add versioned text anchors or an explicit migration/reporting policy. Acceptance: re-extract a book with inserted code, removed duplicate links, and corrected chapter boundaries; saved locations either follow the same content or are clearly reported as needing review.
  *Design note merged October 10 (4a17f774, `TEXT-ANCHORS-RESEARCH-2026-10-10.md`): today's numbers land on other words for most places after an extraction change (80.7% in the real steps that moved any); text anchors placed none wrong and reported 0.3%; 46 of the user's 52 positions and all 5 bookmarks predate their books' current text. Four branches planned; ten questions wait in `humanQuestions/2026-10-10.md`, all recommended yes.*
  *Answered October 10 in `humanQuestions/2026-10-10.md`: yes to (1) anchors, (3) silent moves, (4) skipped
  headings by level, label, and text, (5) Edit Text moving bookmarks, skipped headings, and a closed book's
  place, (6) backfilling from each book as it reads now, (7) a place past its chapter's end going to the
  end, (9) an anchor build that changes no extraction, and (10) Replace keeping places, later. Changed: (2)
  no Places to Review list: "no, or at least I don't care enough about those places to spend time fixing
  them... is there any improvements to the algorithm you can make to make it smarter (like Git or
  Diff...?) to try pushing it to 0. Also how long does it take to find the text and make a best guess?
  Can the character possition ever save over mmarkdown that can change?"; (8) a bounded diff when the text
  is rebuilt: "Maybe it should save /cash text before and after places (like a bigger amound like 10000
  chars before and after) before updating the cash, then try to restore the places, then delete the old
  text?" So the design changes: when a book's text is rebuilt the old text is still on disk, so TextMouth
  keeps a snapshot of about 10,000 characters around each place before the cache is replaced, aligns old
  and new by diff to move each place, falls back to the anchors where the diff cannot decide (and on paths
  with no old text, such as another Mac's rebuild), puts any place still unplaced at its best guess with a
  debug-log line and no announcement, and then deletes the snapshot. The user's questions, from the note's
  measurements: a place whose anchor still matches is checked in microseconds; when one fails, the book's
  words are indexed once (about a quarter second per million characters in unoptimized scratch code under
  load, so about a tenth of a second for a typical book) and each place then takes hundredths of a
  millisecond; and a place saved as its surrounding words no longer depends on offsets into Markdown that
  can change, the offset being only a hint. A short addendum measures the hybrid before the build.*
  *Addendum merged October 10 (1268f4a5, `TEXT-ANCHORS-ADDENDUM-2026-10-10.md`): at every rebuild the old
  content.md is kept aside and every place moved by a patience diff over finely split words, the saved words
  deciding where there is no old text, then a silent best guess with a `PLACES_RELOCATED` debug-log line; 0
  of 57,472 real places on wrong words. Build in five branches, A (`feature/text-anchors`), B
  (`feature/text-anchors-capture`), C (`feature/place-diff`), D (`feature/place-relocation-at-rebuild`), and
  E (`feature/place-relocation-at-load`); A, B, and D start after `fix/book-ends-finished` merges. The next
  extraction change waits until D and E are in. Branch A merged October 10 (2597e5c8), B (b3bba99c), C (20bc9d76); D and E next.*

- [x] **T07 — Verify cached text and its index describe the same content.** P2 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Risk. [BookContentAnalysisService](TextMouth/Services/BookContentAnalysisService.swift) trusts versioned cache/index metadata without a content fingerprint or complete chapter-boundary cross-check. Acceptance: edit `content.md` or replace a source while retaining metadata; stale metrics/coordinates are detected and regenerated predictably. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **T08 — Add encoding and line-ending coverage.** *Repaired October 2; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed / Risk. Several text/HTML import paths use UTF-8 exclusively, and plain-text heading heuristics need CRLF/CR fixtures. Detect supported BOMs/declared encodings or report unsupported encoding explicitly. Acceptance: UTF-8/UTF-16, a supported legacy encoding, and all common line endings preserve content and heading boundaries.

- [x] **T09 — Retain short embedded PDF text when OCR is unavailable or less accurate.** P2 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [PDFTextParser](TextMouth/Parsers/PDFTextParser.swift) uses a character-count threshold to choose OCR. A legitimate short embedded line can be replaced by failed OCR; mixed pages with enough embedded text may bypass OCR for the scanned remainder. Acceptance: short title pages, image-plus-caption pages, and mixed scanned/text pages retain all recoverable text with useful extraction diagnostics. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [ ] **T10 — Validate PDF reading order and heading heuristics on a representative corpus.** P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 5 · Manual / Risk. Check multi-column order, rotated pages, geometry-dependent sorting, OCR language coverage, and the outline-heading duplicate-removal heuristic. The existing heuristic intentionally removes some duplicated navigation text. Acceptance: retain unrelated short body lines, avoid duplicated headings, and document unavoidable layout limits with fixture comparisons.

- [x] **T11 — Unify fallback HTML/EPUB heading coordinates and navigation titles.** P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Verified September 8. Direct and cached EPUB parsing share EPUB 3 navigation, declared NCX, legacy guide, and recognized visible-TOC recovery with canonical paths, fragments, and nested levels. Chapters missing a start heading receive a navigation entry without added speech. Direct HTML records real DOM offsets while preserving literal text. A copied *The Helliconia Trilogy* reproduces **27 headings / 122 chapters** before repair and **127 headings / 122 chapters** afterward: every chapter is reachable, the five extra subheadings remain, and reading text plus existing heading IDs are unchanged. Cache `3.0.4` rebuilds stale caches/indexes. `EPUBHeadingRecoveryTests` covers import, cache/index migration, preserved state, reader navigation, and direct parsing. General bookmark relocation after changed extraction remains T06.

## A — Accessibility and interface consistency

- [ ] **A01 — Run the VoiceOver, braille, and keyboard acceptance matrix below.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Manual. [VoiceOverFocus](TextMouth/Views/VoiceOverFocus.swift) contains deliberate AppKit/runtime workarounds and delayed focus retries. Native semantics and automated policy tests cannot establish actual announcement/focus behavior. Preserve the existing workarounds until replacement behavior is demonstrated on supported macOS versions.

- [x] **A02 — Make long-book search cancellable and responsive.** *Repaired September 28; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [BookSearchView](TextMouth/Views/Reader/BookSearchView.swift) searches all chapters synchronously from a computed body dependency on each edit; arbitrary regular expressions can make it much worse. Debounce, cancel obsolete work, bound result production, and handle expensive regex evaluation. Acceptance: typing and VoiceOver navigation remain responsive in a large book; old results cannot replace a newer query.

- [x] **A03 — Provide keyboard-accessible substitution editing and reordering.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed / Manual. [SubstitutionsSettingsView](TextMouth/Views/Settings/SubstitutionsSettingsView.swift) uses a row tap gesture and advertises drag reordering. Add explicit edit and move-up/down actions with names and position announcements; avoid merging the toggle into an ambiguous accessibility element. Acceptance: edit, enable, move, and delete every rule with keyboard and braille controls.

- [x] **A04 — Verify bookmark keyboard focus beyond the first row.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 3 · Manual. [BookmarkPanelView](TextMouth/Views/Reader/BookmarkPanelView.swift) explicitly uses `.focusable(index == 0)` and a first-row focus binding. Confirm normal keyboard navigation to later rows and restoration after add/edit/delete before changing the focus policy. Acceptance: all rows and context actions are reachable without pointer input or unwanted focus jumps.

- [x] **A05 — Route app-wide actions when a reader is focused or no Library window exists.** P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed / Risk. [ContentView](TextMouth/Views/ContentView.swift), [LibraryWindowRouter](TextMouth/Services/LibraryWindowRouter.swift), and [AppDelegate](TextMouth/App/AppDelegate.swift) need end-to-end coverage for New from Text, Finder open, Open Book, and continue-next across scene creation. Some actions depend on a subscribed Library scene. Acceptance: cold launch, only readers open, all windows closed, and multiple libraries each handle an action once. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **A06 — Restore initial folder/category sorting consistently.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed / Manual. [LibraryView](TextMouth/Views/Library/LibraryView.swift) loads sorting on navigation changes; verify the initial root/category selection also restores its saved setting. Manual ordering and ascending/descending controls need an explicit consistent rule. Acceptance: reopen every category/folder with saved sorting and confirm the displayed order and selected controls agree.

- [x] **A07 — Report actual operation outcomes in sheets and storage status.** *Repaired October 2; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. [StorageSettingsView](TextMouth/Views/Settings/StorageSettingsView.swift) sets “Done” after calls whose errors are handled elsewhere; move/rename sheets can dismiss and clear selection after a failure. Return operation results and keep errors attached to the originating workflow. Acceptance: blocked migration, folder collision, and failed write leave accurate status and a usable retry.

- [x] **A08 — Make removal wording match the operation and preference.** *Repaired October 2, including the main Remove Book dialog and the menu title; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 2 · Confirmed. [DuplicateFinderView](TextMouth/Views/Settings/DuplicateFinderView.swift) labels an action as removal from the library while calling file deletion directly. Other messages imply permanent deletion even with Trash enabled. State exactly whether files are retained, trashed, or permanently deleted, and honor the chosen confirmation policy. Acceptance: visual and spoken labels match every removal mode and failure.

- [x] **A09 — Keep metadata writeback errors and destinations clear.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. [LibraryView](TextMouth/Views/Library/LibraryView.swift) suppresses some EPUB writeback errors; [Reader bookmarks/metadata](TextMouth/ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Bookmarks.swift) can update its local metadata before a failed file write. Editor wording refers to the original EPUB while writing the managed copy. Acceptance: a failed write retains coherent library/reader state and clearly identifies the file that was or was not updated.

- [x] **A10 — Audit fixed sizing, redundant choices, and spoken hints.** Fixed in 704b1b53, October 10 (see the merge entry above); two questions for the user remain (`humanQuestions/2026-10-09.md`, 6 and 7), and live VoiceOver, Zoom, and Increase Contrast checks stay manual. P3 · Impact 2 · Difficulty 3 · Manual / Confirmed. Test large text/zoom, high contrast, narrow windows, long titles, and localized strings across sheets. The heading filter can expose both “H1–H6” and “All” with the same tag. Several macOS hints assume double-tap input. Acceptance: no clipped essential controls, duplicate selection semantics, or instructions that require one input device.
  *Audited October 10 by a read-only Opus agent (`a10audit`): Settings may clip most of its controls (a fixed 700 by 540 window of non-scrolling forms); the substitution editor's capture-group hint says "backslash 1" where only `$1` works; four "Double-tap" hints, the shortcut recorder's "click", and the book row's "Press Enter"; two Exit edit mode buttons; values read twice, "slider" in six labels, captions repeating values, plural slips, a stepper without its unit, the heading filter's "H1–H1", save-to-defaults labels naming speed only; `LazyVStack` lists under VoiceOver's cursor; smaller wording. The fixes are on `fix/a10-accessibility` (brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-09/a10fixes.md`); the Sort menu's reversal and the speed sheet's three controls are the user's questions 6 and 7 in `humanQuestions/2026-10-09.md`, with two checks only the user can make. The TODO's example ("H1–H6" beside "All") no longer exists.*
