## C — Configuration and listening statistics

- [x] **C01 — Apply configuration atomically during startup and synchronization.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [ConfigService](TextMouth/Services/ConfigService.swift) decodes synchronously but defers assignment; [SettingsViewModel](TextMouth/ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift) immediately checks `hasExistingConfig` and writes preferences back. Field-by-field `@Published` updates can also queue partial snapshots. Load the initial value before consumers initialize and publish one complete snapshot. Acceptance: an externally edited config survives cold launch without transient/default rewrites or repeated replays.

- [x] **C02 — Validate externally editable numeric and enum settings at one boundary.** *Repaired September 29; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. UI sliders enforce ranges that decoded config/model values do not consistently enforce. F16/F25 cover particular crash paths; fade duration, timer minutes, punctuation level, playback settings, and model offsets still need a systematic validation policy. Acceptance: extreme finite values, negatives, unsupported enum values, and missing fields cannot crash, allocate unbounded resources, or silently reset the entire configuration.

- [x] **C03 — Serialize file-watcher state and distinguish self-writes from external edits.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Risk. The config watcher reads actor-owned `isSaving` from a utility queue and suppresses reloads during a time window; an external edit in that interval can be missed. Compare revisions/content on the owning executor and rearm after replacement. Acceptance: editor atomic saves during repeated app saves are applied exactly once without feedback loops.

- [x] **C04 — Complete configuration mirroring and refresh open settings views.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 2 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. Some playback-control AppStorage preferences are absent from `AppConfig`; storage controls initialize local `@State` snapshots that do not comprehensively follow external edits. Define which preferences are portable and update all views from the same state. Acceptance: config round-trip and external edits preserve every supported preference and refresh already-open settings.

- [x] **C05 — Checkpoint active listening time.** *Repaired September 30; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. [ReadingStatsService](TextMouth/Services/ReadingStatsService.swift) autosaves finalized statistics, while the active session remains in memory until ended. A crash during a long session can lose the whole interval. Persist incremental checkpoints without double-counting on resume/restart. Acceptance: a simulated crash after successive checkpoints loses at most the stated checkpoint interval.

- [x] **C06 — Attribute statistics across day boundaries and calendar changes.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed / Design. Sessions are credited to their ending day, and date keys use a default-configured `DateFormatter`. Define timezone/calendar behavior, split midnight-spanning sessions, and keep persistent keys stable. Acceptance: midnight, daylight-saving transitions, non-Gregorian user calendars, and travel do not corrupt day totals or streaks.

- [x] **C07 — Refresh live statistics without requiring playback to stop.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P2 · Impact 2 · Difficulty 2 · Confirmed / Manual. Statistics getters include elapsed session time, but the UI needs an explicit observation/timeline tick while the underlying published snapshot remains unchanged. Acceptance: an open statistics view advances during playback and stays accessible without repeatedly stealing focus.

## Q — Test safety, tooling, and maintainability

- [x] **Q01 — Isolate all hosted-test side effects, including legacy migration and preferences.** *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.* P1 · Impact 5 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. `TextMouthPaths.root` is isolated, but [sandbox migration](TextMouth/ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift) still computes the real home/default root, and legacy app-support helpers can read real storage. Several services use `UserDefaults.standard` and shared singletons. Inject roots/defaults and disable real legacy migration during hosted tests. Acceptance: seeded sentinel files/preferences outside the test sandbox remain byte-for-byte unchanged, including when the default production root is absent.

- [x] **Q02 — Make complete strict-concurrency checking an actionable build requirement.** P1 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 5 · Confirmed. `project.yml` currently uses minimal checking. Complete checking reports non-Sendable AVFoundation captures and actor-isolation warnings, including reader-window teardown. Fix ownership/executor boundaries with callback tests before promoting the requirement. Acceptance: production builds pass the chosen strictness without hiding real races behind blanket `@unchecked Sendable` annotations. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up. Swift 6 language mode adopted the same evening.*

- [x] **Q03 — Add reproducible CI and deliberate dependency locking.** P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. [project.yml](project.yml) uses version ranges and `.gitignore` excludes `Package.resolved`; no required repository CI gate was found. Pin the application dependency resolution and run hosted tests plus a Release build on supported Xcode/macOS. Acceptance: a clean checkout resolves the reviewed versions and fails the gate on compile or test errors. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **Q04 — Extend production integration coverage beyond policy helpers.** P2 · Impact 4 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. Many tests validate pure calculations or grouped compatibility scenarios. Prioritize actual import decisions, failed persistence, window routing, timer expiry, render invalidation, and reader loading rather than counting more equivalent assertions. Acceptance: each repaired P1 workflow has a test that fails at the real integration boundary before its fix. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **Q05 — Establish large-library and long-book performance baselines.** P2 · Impact 3 · Difficulty 4 · Manual / Risk. File-size sorting can recursively scan packages from UI-driven getters; heading lists deliberately keep rows alive for VoiceOver; imports, search, and rendering can retain large allocations. Measure main-thread stalls, memory, cancellation, and wake/resume. Preserve intentional accessibility behavior while optimizing. Acceptance: documented representative fixtures and repeatable before/after measurements, including the opt-in playback profile. *Done October 3: the performance baselines tests; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **Q06 — Replace deprecated API usage after behavior is covered.** P3 · Impact 2 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed. ZIPFoundation optional archive initializers, synchronous AVFoundation metadata APIs, older SwiftUI `onChange`, and dynamic selector construction produce warnings. Migrate against official APIs and keep failure/ordering behavior covered. Acceptance: a reviewed warning baseline with no accidental behavior change or unexplained warning suppression. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **Q07 — Bound and harden diagnostic logging.** P2 · Impact 2 · Difficulty 3 · Confirmed / Risk. *Partially repaired September 28: log writes cannot crash the app and rotate at 2 MB; see the September 28 follow-up.* [TextMouthPaths](TextMouth/Services/TextMouthPaths.swift) and [VoiceOverFocus](TextMouth/Views/VoiceOverFocus.swift) append frequent diagnostics without retention limits; some file-handle operations use nonthrowing legacy APIs. Rotate/cap logs, handle storage failures, and decide which book text/path details are necessary. Acceptance: full disk, unwritable logs, and prolonged playback do not disrupt reading or grow logs without bound. *Remaining half repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*

- [x] **Q08 — Reduce duplicated ownership/persistence logic and document the architecture.** P3 · Impact 2 · Difficulty 4 · Confirmed. Large services/view models repeat metadata construction, async-to-sync result boxes, extraction loops, and ordering rules. Refactor behind the regression coverage, in small commits. Add a maintained Markdown setup/user guide and an ownership diagram for reader, media, timer, and storage services. Acceptance: documented build/test commands and clear authoritative state owners, with old behavior preserved unless an audited defect justifies a change. *Repaired October 3; see the September 28 follow-up.*
- [x] **Q09 — Keep the installed app small.** Asked October 3: the user wants 25 MB or less and never more than 50. The installed app was 31 MB, all of it the arm64 binary (about 11.5 MB of code and an unstripped symbol table). An agent is stripping the Release build with a separate dSYM, dead-code stripping, measuring `-Osize`, reporting each package's share, and adding a size guard to `make check` that fails above 50 MB. *Done October 3: 32.7 MB to 11.0 MB; see the September 28 follow-up.*

## Behaviors checked and deliberately preserved

- Per-book settings override global defaults. The existing audio schema stores a multiplier in `localWPM`; the audit keeps compatibility while separating UI/runtime units.
- Duplicate detection and Keep Both are intentional. Two books with equal content hashes can be distinct library entries.
- Folder depth is limited to four. Ordinary slashes in a user-entered name become hyphens. Reserved path components and `.tmb` names cannot serve as ordinary folders.
- Trash failures must remain failures; they do not authorize a fallback to permanent deletion. Successful data-root migration intentionally retains the source as a recovery copy.
- Orphan adoption is a recovery feature. S06 concerns its interaction with explicit removal and pending writes, rather than removing recovery itself.
- There is one global sleep timer with book ownership. Pausing its owner freezes a minutes countdown; explicit cancellation is global; fade duration is captured when the timer starts. The two-boundary heading policy has existing tests and needs the P01 design decision before alteration.
- Media-control focus and active playback ownership are separate. Explicit release of headphone controls and Bluetooth-disconnect suppression are intentional.
- The reader's sliding display window and speech chunking support performance; they are not evidence that the full document was truncated.
- Return-to-position is intentionally one saved departure point. A history stack is a separate feature request.
- Plain-text heading heuristics and duplicate navigation stripping are intentional. Changes need content-preservation fixtures instead of removing heuristics indiscriminately.
- Automatic restoration of all old reader windows is explicitly disabled at launch. The app remaining open after its final window closes is deliberate macOS behavior.

The DOCX outline-level correction follows [Microsoft's OOXML outline-level documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.outlinelevel?view=openxml-3.0.1): level 9 carries no outline heading level. Markdown leaf handling was checked against the [official Swift Markdown visitor implementation](https://github.com/swiftlang/swift-markdown/blob/main/Sources/Markdown/Visitor/MarkupVisitor.swift).

## Manual acceptance matrix for A01/P09/Q05

These checks have **not** been performed with VoiceOver, braille hardware, or Bluetooth audio in this audit. Record OS version, device, voice, and exact steps when completing them.

- [ ] VoiceOver and braille: Library → reader → search/headings/bookmarks → return; activate non-first and offscreen rows; inspect rotor identity; verify initial focus, Escape, Command-W, and focus after errors.
- [ ] Keyboard only: every primary action, context action, slider, reorder operation, and sheet is reachable; text entry does not trigger unintended playback shortcuts.
- [ ] Multiple windows: keep Book A playing while editing/closing Book B; change folders/metadata; expire/cancel/restart a timer; close all Library windows and use Finder open.
- [ ] Audio lifecycle: built-in output and Bluetooth headphones; disconnect while playing/paused/rendering; system sleep/wake; media keys; release/reclaim media controls; no overlapping audio or stale callbacks.
- [ ] End behavior: stop, loop, and continue-next for text, single-file audio, embedded audio chapters, multi-file ZIPs, and DAISY segments; combine with each sleep-timer mode.
- [ ] Real documents: representative EPUB 2/3, DAISY 2.02/3, DOCX lists/tables, scanned/mixed/column PDFs, very long Markdown, and large Unicode-heavy books.
- [ ] Display/accessibility settings: increased contrast, large text/zoom, long titles, window resizing, and relevant macOS 14/current-system differences.
- [ ] Storage failures: work in disposable libraries; disconnect/unmount storage during import/save/move; recover from a corrupt index; confirm original files survive reported failures.

## Performance review (October 3)

Four review agents measured the app on this Mac (Apple M3, 8 GB) against a copy of the live
`library.json` (643 books), copied packages, and generated inputs; their reports are in the session
scratchpad (`research/perf-*.md`). Times were taken while other builds ran, so they are upper bounds;
the growth rates and ratios are the evidence. Items are ordered by what the user hears first.

- [x] **PERF01 — Substitution rules rebuild the chapter once per match.** P1. `TextSubstitutionService.transform` applies matches back to front with `replaceSubrange` on the string being changed, so each range conversion rescans the chapter: 3.4 s frozen before the first word on a 1-million-character chapter, 12 s at 2 million, 80 s at 5.2 million (46 of the user's books have a chapter over a million characters). Fix: build each rule's output in one forward pass with ranges converted against the unchanged input (measured 1M: 4.5 s to 0.22 s, identical text and position maps); oracle test against the old algorithm over random texts with emoji, combining marks, CRLF, adjacent matches, and empty replacements. *Repaired October 3: one forward pass per rule, identical text and offset maps (2M characters: 13.2 s to 0.54 s; the em-dash rule 9 s to 68 ms).*
- [x] **PERF02 — Speech is prepared on the main thread three or more times per chapter.** P1. `preparedSpeech` runs the whole pipeline with no cache at Play, when the look-ahead starts, when it finishes, at the chapter end, and on every global speed, pitch, or voice step: about 36 ms of silence at each 40K-character boundary, 3.4 s for a 1M next chapter. Fix: cache each chapter's `ChapterSpeech` keyed by its inputs (skipped sections, rules revision and switch, repeated-symbol settings, pause level), carry the look-ahead's speech to the chapter end, refresh on voice changes only, prepare oversized chapters off the main actor. *Repaired October 3: `ChapterSpeechCache` keeps each chapter's speech with exactly the inputs it was made from (last four chapters plus the look-ahead), speed and pitch steps re-prepare nothing, oversized look-ahead chapters prepare off the main actor (a 40K boundary 10 ms and four preparations to 0.1 ms and one).*
- [x] **PERF03 — A session's first Play waits 3 to 4.5 s for the voice to load.** P1. The process's first `speak()` loads the voice; a silent one-word `write()` costs the same once and the next utterance starts in 15 ms. Fix: warm the voice through `SpeechRenderCoordinator` when the first reader opens and on each voice change, discarding the audio. *Repaired October 3: each voice is warmed once per session with one silent word through the render queue when a text reader opens and on voice changes.*
- [x] **PERF04 — Rendered speech is held as raw floats, copied, and kept for two chapters.** P1. 2,319 bytes per character: 93 MB for a 40K chapter, 232 MB at the 100K limit, a 2.3× peak while pieces are joined, and a copy of the rest of the chapter on every seek (27 to 51 ms, +88 MB). Fix: schedule the rendered pieces in order instead of joining them, copy only the piece holding the cursor on a seek, store pieces as 16-bit (or render to a temporary CAF played with `scheduleSegment`). *Repaired October 3: rendered speech kept as 16-bit slices fed to the player off the main thread (43 MB instead of 87 for a 40K chapter, rendering peak 44 MB instead of 216, seeks 6 ms with no copy).*
- [x] **PERF05 — Pauses and repeated symbols work one character at a time.** P2. `applyPunctuationPauses` is quadratic on non-ASCII text (1.9 to 3.1 s at 500K characters); `RepeatedSymbolRule.runs` tests every grapheme against a `Set<Character>` (341 ms at 5.2M on every preparation). Fix: one pass over UTF-16 code units for pauses (checked identical: 500K in 37 ms); scan symbols by Unicode scalar. *Repaired October 3: pauses and repeated symbols read the chapter in one walk (pauses at 500K: 2.3 to 5.1 s to 13 to 24 ms; the symbol scan 5.5 times faster), oracle-tested against the old code.*
- [x] **PERF06 — Direct speech starts with 5,000 characters and waits between chunks.** P2. First-word latency grows with the first utterance (111 ms at 5,000, 391 ms at 20,000) on every Play, seek, and skip, and each chunk boundary waits for `didFinish` on the main queue (about 0.1 s of silence mid-sentence every 100 s at 500 wpm). Fix: a first chunk of about 1,000 characters; queue the next utterance when the current one starts, keeping the per-utterance identity checks. *Repaired October 3: direct speech starts with a 1,000-character chunk and hands each next chunk to the synthesizer as the current one starts.*
- [x] **PERF07 — The voice list is never cached and runs on the main thread.** P2. `SpeechService.availableVoices` takes 76 to 114 ms cold and 65 ms warm; `SettingsViewModel` calls it at launch and on each language change, and the reader's voice sheet calls it twice per evaluation while the reader republishes eight times a second. Fix: one shared list refreshed on `availableVoicesDidChangeNotification`, loaded after the first window shows; the sheet computes its lists once on appear. *Repaired October 3: `VoiceCatalog` lists the voices once off the main thread after the first window and relists on the system notification (launch listing 0.06 ms instead of 70 to 92 ms; sheet redraws 0.02 ms instead of 122 to 133 ms).*
- [x] **PERF08 — Every reader builds a speech engine up front, audiobook readers too.** P3. The first `AudioEngineService` costs 142 ms (2.5 s once with the audio system cold). Fix: create it lazily at the first render or engine play, never for audiobooks. *Repaired October 3: the speech engine is made on first need and never for audiobooks; `releaseSpeech()` frees it on close.*
- [x] **PERF09 — Each audiobook track starts on a new player.** P3. A new `AVPlayer` takes about 250 ms until its clock moves at every track change. Fix: keep the output running with `AVQueuePlayer`, enqueueing the next track as the current one starts; keep the end-notice generations. *Repaired October 3: the service rolls straight into a file the reader names (2 to 12 ms per track change) and each chapter start now queues the next chapter's file, not at the last chapter, not while this book's End of document sleep timer is active, not when the next file is only in the cloud, with the reader taking the chapter in the same turn so the cursor never jumps. Service side done October 3 (`setFileAfterCurrent(_:)`, 2 to 12 ms per track change); the reader must still pass the next chapter's file (not at the last chapter, not with the End of document sleep timer set, not when the next file is only in the cloud).*
- [x] **PERF10 — The library list derives its books seven or eight times per update, re-sorting each time.** P1. Each publish re-evaluates `LibraryView`'s body, which evaluates `sortedBooks`/`booksForCategory` seven or eight times: 27 ms per update with the user's sort (Date Added), 47 ms by Author, about 330 ms by File Size (every book is stat'ed each time). Fix: cache the derived list in the model keyed by category, folder, sort, search, and a revision that only membership or sort-key changes bump; precompute sort keys and store file sizes per revision; keep folder counts in a dictionary; derive once per body. *Repaired October 3: `LibraryListCache` keeps each derived list until a book is added, removed, or moved or a sorted or searched field changes (one list per update instead of eight; a position update sorts nothing).*
- [x] **PERF11 — The live reading position publishes the whole library once a second.** P1. `updateLiveReadingPosition` copies all 643 rows and invalidates every Library window and every reader's window view; a 10-second `refreshTick` re-renders them even when nothing plays: about 100 s of main-thread time per listening hour in All Items. Fix: keep live positions in a small separate observable keyed by book ID, read only by the rows' progress text and the summary bar; write them to the library at checkpoints and on close; remove `refreshTick`. *Repaired October 3: `LiveReadingPositions` holds one observable entry per playing book, so a live position redraws one row (2 library publishes per listening minute instead of 60).*
- [x] **PERF12 — The startup extraction batch commits each finished book to the main actor separately.** P1 (the next install re-extracts about 632 books because the installed app predates cache formats 3.0.5 to 3.0.7). Each book costs a row change, a save, and a list update (27 to 47 ms): 17 to 30 s of main-thread work, several times a second. Fix: buffer summaries and apply them in one mutation and one save at most every 0.5 to 1 s, plus at batch end and at quit; move extraction progress into its own observable. *Repaired October 3: finished books are applied together at most every half second with progress in `TextExtractionProgress` (one library update per 60-book batch instead of 60).*
- [x] **PERF13 — Launch spends about 165 ms on the main thread reading the index and checking files.** P2. Decoding the index takes 9 ms; the startup repair 117 ms, 70 ms of it the removal-marker check, which `lstat`s every ancestor up to the data directory when the marker is absent; the cache filter makes five stat calls per text book (38 ms). Fix: stop the ancestor walk at the first existing ancestor that is not dataless or memoize per folder; run the cache filter on the batch's utility queue; later, plan the repair off the main thread. *Repaired October 3: the removal-marker walk stops at the package (2 lstat calls per book instead of 5.9) and the cache filter runs off the main thread (12.5 ms to under 0.4 ms on main).*
- [x] **PERF14 — A folder rename or move rewrites every record on the main thread.** P2. `commitLibraryTransition` renames each package and rewrites its full record (read, decode, re-encode with the content index, write): renaming a 527-book folder is estimated at 0.5 s of silence for VoiceOver. Fix: run the disk phase off the main actor as held storage work, announce when it finishes, then publish; with PERF15 each rewrite is about 1 KB. *Repaired October 3: folder edits move their packages off the main actor as held storage work and announce when 25 or more moved (a 527-book rename blocks the main thread 20 to 75 ms instead of 633).*
- [x] **PERF15 — Every 30-second checkpoint rewrites the whole index and the whole record.** P1. Each checkpoint (and each pause, skip, navigation, and chapter end) re-encodes all 643 rows into the 694 KB `library.json` (13 ms) and decodes, re-encodes, and rewrites the book's `bookInfo.json` with its content index (0.8 ms for the median 17 KB record, 16 to 70 ms for the largest at 690 KB): about 83 MB of index plus 2 to 83 MB of records per listening hour, each a full upload in a cloud library. Fix: keep the content index in its own package file written only by analysis (a record-format migration; older builds rebuild once); write `library.json` for position-only changes at pause, stop, close, quit, and every few minutes, with launch adopting a newer record position; stop pretty-printing the index (16% fewer bytes). *Repaired October 3: each package's content index lives in its own `contentIndex.json` (migrated on first save; an older build re-analyses once), `library.json` is compact and takes reading checkpoints every five minutes with a `reading-checkpoints.json` journal that launch reconciles, so a checkpoint writes 838 bytes instead of 1.3 MB (about 6 MB per listening hour instead of 157).*
- [x] **PERF16 — The cloud refresh works out where the library lives once per package.** P2. `BookCloudStorageService.state(of:)` recomputes the library location (two resource reads, realpath, lstat) for each of 644 packages: 84 to 88 ms per refresh even in a local library, every minute while a window is visible and every 2 s during a download. Fix: read the location once per refresh and stop early when the library is local; survey only downloading books at the fast cadence. *Repaired October 3: a refresh looks the library's location up once (`state(of:in:)`), stops at once for a local library (82 ms to 0.2 ms), and reads only the downloading books at the two-second cadence.*
- [x] **PERF17 — config.json saves run synchronously on the main actor.** P3. 0.46 ms per changed save (every slider step) and a read outside `withoutDownloading` that would block on a dataless file. Fix: coalesce saves on a background queue with a short trailing delay, flush at quit, read without downloading. *Repaired October 3: config.json saves coalesce on the main actor with a 250 ms delay (one write after a slider drag instead of twenty), outside edits during the delay merge, and the file is read without downloading.*
- [x] **PERF18 — HTML to Markdown is quadratic in emphasis and links.** P1. `HTMLToMarkdownConverter.emphasize`/`wrap` keep a live `unicodeScalars` view while `insert` mutates the result, so every `em`, `strong`, `i`, `b`, or `a` rebuilds everything converted so far: 13.5 s for a 1 MB HTML file with one emphasis and one link per paragraph (56 ms without them); 49% of a 7 MB EPUB's extraction, 14% of a typical one. Fix: find boundaries in a scope that ends before mutating so `replaceSubrange` runs in place, or detach the tail and append pieces; byte-identical golden tests. *Repaired October 3: emphasis and links convert in time proportional to their own words (1 MB of HTML 13.5 s to 0.13 s), byte-identical.*
- [x] **PERF19 — EPUB documents are parsed twice and every element's attributes are materialized.** P1. The navigation pass parses each document for its title and contents, conversion parses it again, and each walk asks every element `hasAttr("hidden")`, which SwiftSoup 2.13 answers by materializing all attributes: about 45% of a typical EPUB's extraction. Fix: convert each document from the same DOM right after its contents scan and reconvert only documents a fragment target names; check `hidden` only in documents whose source contains the word. *Repaired October 3: each EPUB document is parsed once and asked about `hidden` only where the word appears (a 60-document EPUB 900 ms to 152 ms with the other fixes).*
- [x] **PERF20 — Each archive member is found by rereading the ZIP's central directory from the start.** P2. `Archive.subscript(path:)` is a linear scan that re-reads a record and local header per step: a 1,447-document EPUB reads 2.1 million records (65% of its 17.7 s preparation) and 57 ms of its every open. Fix: build a `[String: Entry]` index once per opened archive beside `ArchiveEntryReader` (first match wins), plus a lowercased one for DAISY's fallback. *Repaired October 3: archive members come from an index read once per opened archive (2,005 lookups 6.8 s to 10 ms; a 1,000-document EPUB 6.2 s to 0.3 s).*
- [x] **PERF21 — Word counts look up every character's Unicode properties.** P2. `Chapter.wordCount` splits on `Character.isWhitespace` and allocates a Substring per word: 64 ms per MB (1.8 s for the largest book, 32% of a plain-text extraction). Fix: decide ASCII bytes directly and read a Character only around non-ASCII bytes, allocating nothing (measured 5.5 ms per MB, identical counts on 15 books and 31 edge strings). *Repaired October 3: words are counted without reading every Character or allocating a substring (5 MB 357 ms to 35 ms, no extra heap), identical counts.*
- [x] **PERF22 — Opening a book re-splits and re-parses all of content.md every time.** P2. `loadExistingCache` builds an array of every line, joins and trims each chapter, and runs swift-markdown and `PlainTextExtractor` over every chapter although the verified index holds every length, heading, and count: 24 to 72 ms per MB, with single-chapter books peaking at 10 to 24 times their text size in memory. Fix now: split on Substrings (2.7 to 4.4× faster, identical sections); later walk cmark's nodes directly, or store each chapter's reading text beside content.md under its fingerprint (about 500 MB more disk, an index-version bump). *Repaired October 3 (first half): content.md splits over Substrings, one copy per chapter (a 1,500-chapter cache 58 ms to 15 ms; a 20 MB chapter 182 ms to 44 ms and 46 MB less); the Markdown parse still sets the open's time and peak, so the direct cmark walk or a stored reading text remain open. Second half the same evening: a large book's reading text is kept in this Mac's Caches folder and read instead of parsed when its content matches, and the opened chapter's speech is prepared off the main thread at open; reopening the 28 MB anthology 773 ms to 110 ms, a 20 MB one-chapter book 947 ms to 53 ms, peak memory +345 MB to +48 MB, Play's main-thread step 3.96 s to 0.04 ms.*
- [x] **PERF23 — Escaping and plain-text heading detection work a scalar, or a copy, at a time.** P3. `MarkdownLiteral.escaped` appends one scalar at a time (374 ms for 20 MB); the all-caps rule uppercases every short line (837 ms for 20 MB). Fix: byte-level escaping (51 ms) and a cheap check that rejects lines holding a scalar that changes when uppercased (385 ms); both matched today's output on 51 inputs. *Repaired October 3: escaping works a byte at a time (20 MB 424 ms to 68 ms) and a line containing a lowercase ASCII letter leaves the capitals rule early (842 ms to 367 ms), identical output on 51 inputs.*
- [x] **PERF24 — DAISY audio import reads each audio file whole.** P2 (memory). `copyAudioFile`/`copyRemainingAudioFiles` read up to 256 MB into one `Data`; loose files are rewritten rather than cloned. Fix: stream ZIP entries to disk with `ArchiveEntryReader.stream`; copy loose files with `FileManager.copyItem` (an APFS clone); byte-identical audio. *Repaired October 3: DAISY audio is copied from an archive a megabyte at a time and loose files are cloned (a 64 MB file 141 MB of heap to 1.5 MB).*
- [x] **PERF25 — `normalizeMarkdownWhitespace` runs a regular expression per line.** P3. 50 ms per MB and 11 MB of heap per MB for DOCX and DAISY text. Fix: one scalar loop over exactly the characters `\s` matches, with tests for tabs, NBSP, and U+2028. *Repaired October 3: Markdown whitespace normalizes in one pass over exactly the characters the expression matched (5 MB 229 ms to 15 ms).*
- [x] **PERF26 — Hosted-test sandboxes are never deleted.** P2 (disk). `TextMouthRuntime` leaves `$TMPDIR/TextMouthTests-<pid>` behind after every run: 1,025 dead sandboxes (about 450 MB) were removed by hand after the disk hit 100% full during these reviews. Fix: delete the sandbox at process exit and sweep sandboxes whose process is gone at the next run; keep one when `TEXTMOUTH_KEEP_SANDBOX` is set. *Repaired October 3: each hosted-test run deletes its sandbox when it ends and sweeps sandboxes whose process is gone (comparing the process start time, since a reused pid once kept one alive); TEST_RUNNER_TEXTMOUTH_KEEP_SANDBOX=1 keeps one.*
- [x] **PERF27 — Closed reader and Library windows are never released.** P1 (memory). SwiftUI keeps a closed window, its view graph, and the `@StateObject` reader alive (reachable through an AppKit notification observer; `leaks` finds no cycle in the app's code): five readers opened through the router and closed were all still in `NSApp.windows`, each keeping its text, headings, index, two synthesizers, an audio engine, and the last chapter's rendered audio because `close()` never calls `discardRenderedAudio()` (about 58 MB for a 30,000-character chapter, up to 190 MB at the render limit); reopening a book adds another 6.4 MB that never comes back. Fix: make closed windows cheap, since they do not die: `close()` discards rendered audio, drops the content, headings, offset maps, look-ahead, and text window, and releases the player item; a closed Library window shows a placeholder instead of the list; file a minimal reproduction with Apple. Test: open and close a real reader window and assert no content, no rendered audio, and under 1 MB of growth. *Repaired October 3: a closed reader empties itself in `close()` (text, headings, offset maps, rendered audio, the text window, and speech through `releaseSpeech()`; 12 MB to 0.02 MB above a tiny book) and a closed Library window shows a plain "Library" placeholder (33 ms to 2 ms per library change); SwiftUI itself still keeps about half a megabyte per closed window.*
- [x] **PERF28 — Each library publish re-renders every Library window's whole list.** P1. One body pass derives the list eight times, copies it into `Array(enumerated())`, builds every row's context menu and cloud commands whether visible or not, and copies every book in each folder tree just to count it; in Manual Order each row's menu sorts the folder twice: 54 ms per publish in All Items, 243 ms in a 134-book Manual Order folder, 51 ms for a closed window that is still alive; first layout 279 ms in All Items. Fix: derive once per body and pass the array down, `ForEach` over the shown rows, count folders in one pass, take Move Up and Move Down from the ordered list, make `BookRow` `Equatable`, show a placeholder while the window is closed (the model-side cache is PERF10). *Repaired October 3: `LibraryBookListRow` is the unit SwiftUI compares, the list is derived once and folders counted in one pass, so a change redraws one row (a 134-book manual-order folder 144 ms to 9 ms per change; All Items 32 ms to 14 ms; first layout 510 ms to 162 ms).*
- [x] **PERF29 — `refreshTick` republishes everything every 10 seconds for nothing.** P2. Nothing shown depends on the clock (time left comes from positions and words per minute, both already published), yet every observer re-renders: 52 ms per tick per All Items window, idle included. Fix: delete `refreshTick`, its timer, and `LibraryRefreshTimer`; use a `TimelineView` if a clock-relative value ever appears; test that a row's time left follows a live position with no tick. *Repaired October 3: `refreshTick`, its timer, and `LibraryRefreshTimer` are gone (zero idle publishes).*
- [x] **PERF30 — The heading and voice sheets observe the whole reader.** P2. `HeadingNavigatorView` and `BookVoicePickerView` (and the bookmark and sleep-timer sheets) hold the reader as an `@ObservedObject`, so each playback publish, every 0.12 s, re-runs their bodies: 46 ms for the heading sheet with 179 headings (0.38 s of every second of main thread) and 36 ms for the voice sheet, which also calls `availableVoices()` twice and makes a new synthesizer on every init. Fix: give the sheets snapshots (headings, skipped IDs, cached heading items) plus an observable that publishes the current heading only when it changes; `Equatable` row subviews; the voices from the shared list (PERF07). *Repaired October 3: `ReaderSnapshot` republishes only real changes and `SheetOverOneReader` makes the heading, voice, and bookmark sheets equal for the same reader, so they no longer redraw per playback publish (heading sheet 19 ms to 1.7 ms; voice sheet 24 ms to 0.5 ms).*
- [x] **PERF31 — The cloud refresh publishes once per book and reads every package in a local library.** P2. `BookCloudStates.refresh` assigns `states[bookID]` per book, copying the `@Published` dictionary and publishing each time (644 publishes for one refresh, quadratic copying), and a local library still asks `state(of:)` for every package. Fix: assign the merged dictionary once; read the library location first and stop when it is not in the cloud (the storage side is PERF16). *Repaired October 3: a cloud refresh records its states in one assignment (two publishes instead of 644).*
- [x] **PERF32 — Storage settings stat the data directory inside their body.** P3. `StorageSettingsView.body` calls `directoryExists` twice and filters the books on every publish, so during playback it touches the file system on the main thread once a second, which can block on a network or unplugged volume. Fix: check off the main thread on appear and after each operation; keep the answer in `@State`. *Repaired October 3: Storage settings check the data directory off the main thread into state.*
- [x] **PERF33 — VoiceOver focus code can crash after a window closes.** P1 (crash, not performance). `VoiceOverFocusTriggerView` and `StandardVoiceOverFocusTriggerView` force-unwrap `window.contentView!` up to 1.2 s later and capture the window strongly; a content view removed within that delay crashes the app (hit at `VoiceOverFocus.swift:449` in a test teardown). Fix: `guard let` and a weak window, with a test that closes the window inside the delay. *Repaired October 3: a VoiceOver focus request on a closed window is dropped instead of force-unwrapping, and no longer keeps the window alive.*

## Feature requests (October 3)

- [x] **FR01 — Speaking styles for repeated symbols.** Requested and shipped October 3. A "Repeated
  Symbols" group at the bottom of the Text settings tab: style (Say how many, the default, reads a run of
  4 or more as "7 asterisks"; Leave out replaces the run with a pause; Repeat each symbol reads every
  one), "Collapse runs of at least" (2 to 99) with a "Never collapse: read every symbol" checkbox that
  stores -1, and an editable Symbols field with Reset to Default (ASCII symbols, bullets, dashes, the
  ellipsis, box-drawing lines; sentence punctuation, quotes, and brackets are left out by default because
  voices already compress them). A run is one symbol repeated two or more times, directly or across single
  spaces. `RepeatedSymbolRule` runs in speech preparation before punctuation pauses and substitution
  rules, keeps the offset map exact (a cursor inside a separator resumes inside its count), changes
  nothing on screen or on a braille display, takes effect for the next chapter, and invalidates look-ahead
  audio. Mirrored in `config.json` as `repeatedSymbolStyle`, `repeatedSymbolThreshold`, and
  `repeatedSymbolSet`. `RepeatedSymbolRuleTests`, `RepeatedSymbolSpeechTests`,
  `RepeatedSymbolSettingsViewTests` (68 new). Not verified with live VoiceOver on the checkbox and Reset
  button, in the real Settings window layout, or by listening.
- [ ] **FR02 — Keep the data directory in a cloud drive (iCloud Drive or Google Drive) with on-demand
  download.** Requested October 3. Research first: see `CLOUD-STORAGE-RESEARCH-2026-10-03.md`. Feasible
  without an Apple Developer account; today's code is unsafe in a cloud folder (main-thread reads,
  maintenance that downloads every book, single-Mac assumptions); the recommended design keeps today's
  layout in a visible cloud folder, moves per-Mac files out, computes each book's cloud state at runtime,
  and ships in five phases. The user answered on October 3: free this Mac's disk first, backups and a second Mac later (safe but not a priority); iCloud Drive, Google Drive, and Dropbox all supported, with a small test library; cloud notes only when the directory is in a cloud location; any path (hidden or not, internal, external, or cloud) must be settable, with an error screen offering Retry and Quit when the directory is unavailable; the full cache syncs; mirror and streaming modes both; an open book must never be evicted or break offline. The plan is in `CLOUD-STORAGE-PLAN-2026-10-03.md`; the working draft's first wave is on main (the shared contract, the file-status and book-download services installed at launch, list notes and commands, the reader download flow with a working copy for open audiobooks, library identity and the Library Not Available screen, and maintenance that never downloads); the opt-in real-drive tests are on main and pass on Dropbox, Google Drive, and iCloud Drive (see `CLOUD-STORAGE-MANUAL-TESTS-2026-10-03.md`); iCloud Drive syncs a book package as one item, so the service treats a package that carries sync flags while its files carry none as the item for state, the upload refusal, Remove Download, Download Now, and progress; the settings and move flow landed too (any folder as the data directory, Move Library Here, Use This Library with a relaunch, moves off the main thread with a progress sheet and Cancel, refusals while books are open or only in the cloud, Choose Another Library and Use Default Location while the library is unavailable), so the whole working draft is on main; Keep on This Mac, byte progress within one file, the iCloud Drive and Google Drive runs, iCloud packages as one item, and the docs have since landed; two-Mac conflict merging and the live VoiceOver pass with the manual matrix remain.

  *Third wave merged October 4: Edit Audio… (the Book menu; first among a row's audio commands) opens one track in an editor window of its own, VoiceOver starting on Position, with an Audio Editor group of keys in Settings > Keyboard that work only in editor windows (Space/K play and pause, J and L or Comma and Period by a second, Shift by ten, Option by a tenth, G go to a time, N and Shift-N to the next or previous pause, I and O mark the selection, Option-N selects to the next pause, C the chapter, M drops a marker, Shift-M lists markers and pauses, Delete or Command-X cuts, Command-V pastes, T trims, Shift-S inserts silence, Shift-C sets a chapter marker, F applies a filter preset with a preview, R punches in from the chosen microphone, Command-Z and Shift-Command-Z undo and redo, Command-S saves, Shift-Command-E exports, Option-Command-Z reverts, Command-I shows Audio Info with the book's own original policy), every command also a labelled button in four groups; a waveform for sighted helpers hidden from VoiceOver; closing with unsaved edits asks. Two engine save bugs fixed (the scratch folder deleted by a save while the session used it; a take's packet padding written past the edits' end). 89 new tests; the 14:28 test-host crash fixed (weak captures). Not checked by hand with a real microphone, audible playback, or live VoiceOver. Open: markers are not saved when the window closes; quitting does not ask about unsaved edits; no editor menu; LibraryTranscriptionCommand should be renamed; MP3 through LAME landed the same evening.*

- [x] **FR05 — Transcription with Apple's on-device speech APIs.** Requested October 3;
  researched the same evening (`TRANSCRIPTION-RESEARCH-2026-10-03.md`): macOS 26's
  SpeechAnalyzer transcribes files on the Mac at about 65 seconds of audio per second
  with a model the system downloads and shares (English installed, 139 MB), every
  word timed, no permission prompt, 7.5 to 11 percent of words wrong on the two
  DAISY fixtures; macOS 14 and 15 have only the older recognizer with a weaker model
  and a prompt that returns after every build. Recommended: keep the transcript in
  the audiobook's package and show it as the book's text while it plays, so the
  cursor, VoiceOver, the braille display, and Search in Book follow the audio. Waits
  for the user's answers to seven questions.
  *First wave merged October 4: `transcript.json` in the package (the language, the model, the build, every sentence and word with times and confidence, each audio file fingerprinted just before it is read); `TranscriptionService` on SpeechAnalyzer for macOS 26 with progress, cancellation within a second, one transcription at a time, and never a download; the transcript becomes the book's text, aligned sentence by sentence through a transcript layer of the content index and written as content.md, so the reader shows the sentence being heard and search, export, and editing work on it; a per-user `transcriptionLocale`. 68 new tests; the two DAISY fixtures transcribe in 24 s and 51 s. Still to build: the Transcribe Audio command, the progress sheet, the offer after import, the language setting's view and model download with consent, Delete Transcript, sentence following in the reader, the experimental cleanup.*
  *Second wave merged October 4: Transcribe Audio… (context menu, VoiceOver actions, the Book menu) with the length, the estimate, the language and model status, a model download only on its button, progress in the same sheet with Cancel and announcements at each quarter and the end, a "Transcribed" row note, Transcribe Again warning about edits; the Transcription group in the Playback tab (`transcriptionLocale`, `offerTranscriptionAfterImport`); the offer after an import; Delete Transcript with a confirmation; Edit Text taking a transcribed audiobook's edit into transcript.json (sentences keep their times; a changed chapter count is refused and kept); transcript.json never taken for part of the book (#966). On macOS 14 and 15 the command says it needs macOS 26. Not verified with a real model download or by hand with VoiceOver. Left: the experimental cleanup, Where Am I quoting the current sentence.*
  *Cleanup study merged October 4 (`TRANSCRIPT-CLEANUP-RESEARCH-2026-10-04.md`): the recognizer measured on 38,834 words of known text; a human narrator's errors are almost all conventions (announced pages, figures, compounds, names, spelling), about 0.3 percent truly misheard, confidence cannot pick them out, and corrections learned from four books were right 13 times and wrong 90 on the fifth, so TextMouth ships no rules of its own. Clean Up Transcript (Experimental)… applies the user's rules from `transcript-cleanup.json` (Edit Rules… makes the copy) with a preview, keeps `transcript.json.before-cleanup` for Undo Cleanup, and English (United Kingdom) as the transcription language gives British spelling. 62 new tests.*

  Info's editing, keeping Open, a read-only Book Info, Export, Transcribe Audio, Delete Transcript, the
  reading list, and Show in Finder, with Command-Delete doing Delete Download; (5) Move to Library…,
  after which the feed lists the item "In Library" and never downloads it again. Changed: (6)
  Unsubscribe: "Hmm I kind of see unsubscribe as a delete option... or at least I don't think the
  folder should become a normal folder (what if the user wanted to resubscribe?) I would say just keep
  it a feed with rules kept except you can't check it or manually check it for new items... If you
  understand me, with with subscribe / resubscribe toggling action in the contetxt menu for feeds and
  changing the info menu... Clearing out old deleted episodes itself seems fine." So Unsubscribe no
  longer ends a feed or turns its downloads into books: the feed keeps its record, its folder, its
  settings, and its downloads (still feed items in `Feeds/<feed ID>/`), its rules stay in force, and
  only checking stops: no refresh at launch, hourly, or in Refresh All Feeds, and no Check Feed while
  it is unsubscribed. The feed's context menu, its VoiceOver actions, and Feed Info offer Unsubscribe…
  or Resubscribe by its state, and Feed Info and the feed's row say it is not checked. Unsubscribing
  deletes the packages kept for removed downloads (their places and bookmarks), as the user accepted;
  Resubscribe checks the feed at once. Branch D builds this in place of the addendum's section 4
  "Unsubscribe"; how a feed is removed entirely, now that Unsubscribe keeps it, is asked in
  `humanQuestions/2026-10-09.md`. Branch A1 (`feature/feed-item-index`) launched October 9 and merged de0839c5 on October 10, and branch A2 (`feature/feed-item-records`) merged 4887c21b the same night, branch B (`feature/feed-item-policy`) merged 87d8857c, and branch C (`feature/feed-item-policy-ui`) merged 5686cb2d, and branch D (`feature/move-to-library`) merged 30eab7f9 (see the merge entries above).*
- [x] **FR12 — Double-press shortcuts, and every shortcut in config.json with a record of the
  user's edits.** Done in five branches: A (094dd885), B (ed23f19c), C (3cf2c820), and D with E (2c3b3eac, October 10; see the merge entries above). Requested October 7 evening, answering whether a second quick press of Speak Info
  (I) or Where Am I? (W) while the brief summary speaks should switch to the full one: "1. Yes if its not in the continue to next book mode  anyway. 2. Yes that sounds liek a good idea, I would want this to go with a general expansion of the keyboard short customization to allow users to set double press keys and a time out at the same time I think all built in shortucts should just be editable in the same json as the user settings, maybe using the same edited records used for the meta data fields to no override user settings with new or changed shortcuts"
  Today `KeyboardShortcutManager` saves the whole map of every action's bindings under
  `customKeyboardBindings` once anything is customized, so a default added or changed later never
  reaches an action the user never touched; config.json mirrors the map as `keyboardShortcuts`.
  Wanted: a key bound to a double press, with its timeout settable; every built-in shortcut listed
  and editable in config.json; a record of which actions the user edited, as `Book.handEdits` records
  metadata fields, so a new or changed default reaches every unedited action and never overrides an
  edited one; and Speak Info's and Where Am I's second press, while the brief summary speaks,
  switching to the full summary (yes, built with the double press). Added the same evening: "also
  fhre the shortcuts to have customizable items for full info and wear am i summaryies in shortcut
  section...": the Shortcuts section lists Speak Info's and Where Am I's brief and full summaries as
  actions of their own, each with its own keys. A design note with the open
  questions comes first (`KEYBOARD-SHORTCUTS-RESEARCH-2026-10-07.md`): a key with both a single and a
  double action either waits out the timeout before its single action or acts at once and lets the
  double press take over, which suits an escalation such as a brief and a full summary but not two
  unrelated actions.
  *Design note merged October 7 evening (1631d2eb, `KEYBOARD-SHORTCUTS-RESEARCH-2026-10-07.md`): a
  record of only the actions the user edited, every other action following the running build's
  defaults; every action in config.json, an unedited one as `"default: w"`; double presses
  written "w twice" with one double-press time of half a second, the single press acting at once
  when the double is its full summary and waiting otherwise; Speak Full Info (I twice) and Where Am
  I? Full Summary (W twice) as actions of their own; three branches, then the fixed menu keys. Ten
  questions wait for the user.*
  *Answered October 7 evening in `humanQuestions/2026-10-07-evening.md`: yes to all ten ("3 seconds is
  pretty long"). Added from the Shift-Command-T question: "I feel like new from text should only work in
  Library view and sleep timer only in reader view, maybe view specific shortcuts with a field should be made
  possible": shortcuts scoped to a kind of window (Library, reader, audio editor, or everywhere), a default

- [x] **B01 — Speech stops and cannot restart on text that holds markup.** Merged aec5c91b, October 7 (see the merge entry above). Reported October 7:
  "suddenling my audio is randomly pausing and I can't restart it". Diagnosis from the logs: the user was
  reading *Rainbows End*, whose silent messages are marked `<sm>…</sm>` in the text; macOS 26.3.1's
  speech engine parses a plain AVSpeechUtterance holding tag-shaped text as SSML, refuses a chunk whose
  closing tag has no opening tag (26,687 `SSMLParserError` lines in one minute of the unified log, each
  retried with the fallback voice, which is the user's own Samantha), swallows everything after an
  opening tag with no closing one, and leaves the AVSpeechSynthesizer instance unable to speak again,
  while a fresh instance speaks at once (rendered offline with `write`, no sound). Fix: a same-length
  guard that turns the brackets of tag-shaped runs into U+2039 and U+203A before any utterance, so
  offsets stay exact and lone "less than" signs are still spoken; a cancelled live utterance replaces the
  synthesizer and retries once, then stops with a spoken reason. Agent `speechguard`, branch
  `fix/speech-markup-guard`. Workaround until then: quit and reopen TextMouth, and add substitution rules
  that replace `<sm>` and `</sm>` with nothing.
- [x] **B02 — The AirPods' rewind after a finished book does nothing.** Merged f5273ba2, October 7 (see the merge entry above). Reported October 7: "after a book
  "finished" trying to use the rewind track on my Airpods didn't seem to work to hear the last little
  bit". Remote skip and previous-track commands go to `NowPlayingService.activeOwnerID` (the playing
  reader, else the focused one) and fail without one; a finished book shows Now Playing stopped and may
  own nothing, and a skip at the book's end may do nothing (P07). Wanted: after a book finishes, a
  backward skip from the AirPods or the keyboard moves back by the skip unit from the end and plays.
  Agent `airpods`, branch `fix/airpods-rewind-after-finish`.
- [x] **B03 — Command keys are taken from text fields.** Merged 5b137a63, October 7 (see the merge entry above). Found October 7 evening by the FR12 study,
  read from the code, no key pressed: `KeyboardShortcutManager.shouldHandle` leaves only plain keys to a
  focused text field, so Command-Delete in the Library's search field removes the selected books
  (asking first only when `confirmBeforeDelete` is on), and Command-A selects every book rather than
  the field's text. Wanted: a focused text field keeps every key it edits with, Command keys included.
- [x] **B04 — Shift-Command-B is bound twice in the user's shortcuts.** Ended by FR12's branch A, merged 094dd885, October 7 late (see the merge entry above): Next Bookmark returns to its default. Found October 7 evening by the
  FR12 study, reading the user's config.json read-only: Next Bookmark holds Shift-Command-B, its
  default until March 24, frozen there by the whole-map save, and Show Bookmarks' default is the same
  key; the manager takes the first match from a dictionary, whose order can change between launches,
  so the key may do either. Workaround until FR12's record lands: set Next Bookmark to
  `["option+shift+command+b"]` in config.json. FR12's first branch ends it.
  *Answered October 7 evening: no change to config.json now: "I can wait for the full fix, I basically
  don't really use bookmarks." FR12's branch A ends it.*
- [x] **FR13 — Recent becomes Recently Opened.** Merged 4887c21b, October 10 (see the merge entry above). Requested October 9, answering FR11's question 3
  about Recent: "Yes, I actually forgot recents was recently opened so maybe add a todo to rename it to
  that or similar wording?" The View picker's fourth category, `LibraryCategory.recent`, whose name is
  its raw value ("Recent", saved with each window's category), is called "Recently Opened" wherever it
  is shown or spoken (the View picker, its menu items, entering it, the summary bar, Speak Info, the
  README), and a category saved as "Recent" still reads as it, as one saved as "No Folder" reads as
  Unopened (`LibraryCategory.formerNoFolderName`); its sort key `__recent__` stays. After FR11's branch
  A1, which changes what the category lists (each feed's item opened last).
- [x] **FR14 — Delete Feed…, an Unsubscribe that asks only when it deletes, and the feed downloads'
  Cancel as a pause.** Merged 428f4532, October 10 (see the merge entry above). Answered October 10 in `humanQuestions/2026-10-09.md`. (1) Delete Feed…: "Yes,
  make sure its not duplicating another delete action or anything." The feed's row (its context menu
  and VoiceOver actions) and Feed Info offer Delete Feed…, which asks first and removes the feed: its
  record, its downloads, the places and bookmarks kept for removed ones, and its library folder when
  nothing else is in it; the confirmation says how many downloads go and points to Move to Library… for
  one to keep. No other command may do the same for a feed: Delete Folder… on a feed's folder (which
  today asks first and leaves the feed's downloads listed nowhere) becomes Delete Feed… there, so one
  feed has one delete; the functions branch D kept (`cancelAll(of:)`, `FeedStore.remove(_:)`,
  `FeedFetchStates.remove(_:)`) serve it. (2) Unsubscribe asks first only when it would delete something
  (places or bookmarks kept for removed downloads) and otherwise acts at once, its menu title without an
  ellipsis then: "yes". (5) The feed downloads section's Cancel: "yes, why won't TextMouth try
  downloading again on its own? that just sounds like silently unsubscribe kindof... any in progress
  downloads should probably be deleted too, like mp3 parts." So Cancel stops the download under way,
  those queued, and the rule's downloads waiting for a network or for room; deletes every partial
  download (resume data and partial files); and no longer makes the items the listener's, so the
  newest-N rule downloads them again by itself at its next pass after a refresh (the hourly check,
  Check Feed, or the next launch), and the announcement says so. A single item's Cancel Download on its
  row stays the listener's choice for that one item, which the rule then leaves.

- [x] **B06 — Ogg audio through Apple's player can lock up and take the Mac down.** Merged 70a74208, October 14 (see the merge entry above). October 14, 16:22: a few seconds
  into the radio dramas' second track at 1.5x, Apple's Vorbis decoder (`ACVorbisDecoder`, macOS 15.4 and later) locked up
  in AudioToolbox (`AQConverterThread` spinning in caulk's real-time allocator); TextMouth grew to 34 GB, the 8 GB Mac
  ran out of memory (JetsamEvent, WindowServer watchdog, 16:31), and it had to be reset. The user: "I say yes to
  converting everything to m4a or acc (which ever one is losslessly smaller) and go ahead an convert all the ogg files
  already imported and the manafest as well so we don't have to warry about old coppies". Decided: AAC-LC in .m4a at
  the source's bitrate, at every import and once for the library. Agent `oggtoaac`, brief
  `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-14/ogg-to-aac.md`.
- [x] **B07 — A stuck audio system froze the window, and nothing noticed silent playback.** Merged d7f4f527, October 14 (see the merge entry above). The same evening: the
  reader went on showing Playing over silence; Pause played its sound through NSSound on the main thread, which
  blocked for good behind the stuck decoder; nothing watched the memory. A watchdog for stalls and memory, and sound
  effects off the main thread. Agent `playbackwatch`, brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-14/playback-watchdog.md`.
- [x] **B08 — Rendered speech can crash TextMouth when the audio output stops cycling.** Merged 32dc2036, October 14 (see the merge entry above). On October 14, during a
  glitch in which the Mac's audio output delayed every start by seconds, the test host crashed nine times in
  `AudioEngineBehaviorTests` with "player did not see an IO cycle" (board #2491, agent `oggtoaac`):
  `RenderedSpeechFeeder.start` (`TextMouth/Services/AudioEngineService.swift`) traps when the output never cycles. The
  app would crash the same way for a book read aloud while the output is stuck. Replace the trap with a failure the
  reader reports, and test it with an engine whose output never cycles.
- [x] **B05 — When speech stops after a refusal, show what happened and the log lines that help find it.** Merged 6c5b4d9d, October 9 (see the merge entry above).
  Requested October 7 evening, declining an automatic skip past a refused passage: "I feel like VoiceOver
  should just say like "speech interopted for unknown reason" or something or even better popup a dialog
  with the error and any log to help find it, the skip passage thing is a bug group, it shouldn't be worked
  around." Wanted: when speech ends after the engine refused a passage twice (`endDirectSpeechRefused`,
  `onSpeechRefused`), an accessible alert names what happened and where (the chapter, the position), with the
  details that help find the cause (the passage around the offset, the voice, the macOS version, the
  transition log's lines for it) and a way to copy them, so each refusal can be fixed rather than skipped; the
  log files themselves keep to their rule of offsets and lengths, never a book's words.
- [x] **REV18 — Review of the October 16 and 17 merges.** Merged 50faa5d8, October 17 (see the merge entry above). Three read-only Opus reviewers, October 17, asked for by the
  user ("Did you review all the changes or have other Opus level agents review them for any new bugs?"). `rev18a`
  (feedopts, FR19 and FR20) confirmed five: the next episode's wait outlives a later sleep timer or Stop and plays the
  episode anyway (medium); it hands over after the listener started another book (medium); a repeated request moves
  the episode to the back of the download queue; the pre-download passes over a `.waiting` item; with Skip finished off,
  a finished next book replays its own end rule. `rev18b` (oggsafe, importsafe) confirmed two in `OggHeaderReader`: a
  stream starting at a nonzero granule reads long, so its conversion is refused; a file whose first stream is not the
  audio (Ogg Skeleton) reads as unreadable. `rev18c` (fixes, testkit) confirmed four: the storage drill could fill the
  startup disk if its image were detached under it (medium); its sweep detaches a running drill's images; a failed
  library.json write leaves its bytes remembered, so a later equal save is skipped; `storage-drill.sh` prints PASSED
  for options that run no test. Team rev18fix, brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-17/team-rev18fix.md`.
- [x] **REV17 — Review of the October 13 and 14 merges.** Merged c33a6499, October 17 (see the merge entry above). Two read-only Sonnet reviewers, October 16.
  `rev17a` (B06 to B09: Ogg conversion, the playback watchdog and memory guard, rendered speech's catcher) confirmed
  seven: **(a1, high)** `AudioPlaybackService.extractChapters`, `extractFullMetadata`, and `getDuration` open an
  `AVURLAsset` on Ogg with no guard (reached from import before afconvert, the reader, Book Info, the launch refresh,
  packages), and `OggAudioConverter.inspect` opens Ogg in the app with `AudioFileOpenURL`; **(a2, high)** the memory
  guard reads only while something plays, so its 6 GB quit cannot fire after a pause or a stall's stop; **(a3)**
  exFAT answers `renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)` with ENOTSUP, so every Ogg conversion on an exFAT library fails; **(a4)**
  afconvert outlives a quit, force-quit, or the guard's `_exit`, and a cancel before the pid is published is lost;
  **(a5)** WatchedProcess times by the wall clock, so a sleep kills healthy children; **(a6)** `commitTracks` loads
  the index only after converting, leaving gigabytes of hidden conversions on a failure, every launch; **(a7)** the
  hidden temporary name adds 58 characters to the stem (ENAMETOOLONG). Team oggsafe, brief
  `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-16/team-oggsafe.md`. `rev17b` (FR15, FR17, FR18) confirmed five: **(b1, high, data
  loss)** Import Folder…'s folder of tracks is not marked as from a folder, so After Importing a Book's delete trashes
  the whole source folder, files not copied included; **(b2)** a parent folder of DAISY book folders imports as one
  audiobook of all their audio; **(b3)** a Book Info title or author that cannot be written into the manifest is later
  reverted silently; **(b4)** `ChapterPauses.none` differs from the all-zero list, so TextMouth's own generated
  manifest is taken as an edit; **(b5)** saving Edit Manifest for a cloud book downloads its evicted tracks. Team
  importsafe, brief `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-16/team-importsafe.md`.
- [x] **REV16 — Review of T06's branches D and E.** Merged abbd4738, October 13 (see the merge entry above). One read-only Opus reviewer (`rev16`, October 13, brief
  `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-12/rev16.md`) reviewed the app code of branch D (112bb7b3) and branch E (cf542090) and
  confirmed four problems, three reproduced by hosted tests; the findings, with their reproductions, are in
  `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-12/rev16-review/findings.md`. **F1 (medium-high):** at load, a removed section's skip
  moves to another section on weak evidence (`headingResultAtLoad` accepts `findHeading`'s score from the words before
  a heading alone, and the only heading with the skip's label with no words at all), so that section is skipped and
  saved; the rebuild path makes such a skip wait (#2091's risk, which branch E's load path did not guard). **C1
  (low to medium):** the launch's recovery gives the library row moves its record refused (`saveMovedPlaces`'s result
  ignored, the moves not narrowed with `MovedPlaces.taken(by:)`). **C5 (low):** a stop between the batch's record
  commit and the row's update loses the moves, and the row's stale places can then overwrite the record's moved ones.
  **S1 (low):** the launch's sweep deletes Edit Text copies the in-cloud check kept. Found sound: the guarded takes, the
  commits, the copies' order and crash steps, Edit Text's digest check, crash safety, and the cost at launch and open.
  All four go to a fix team before a build with T06's branches D and E is installed.
