## New formats and export (researched October 17)

Researched by team formats on October 17, 2026, in FORMATS-RESEARCH-2026-10-17.md, which gives each item's evidence and sources, and ends with 21 questions (numbered there) whose recommended answers these items assume. The user asked for more format support, "at least we could convert a bunch more audio formats to a standardized format like how we convert the text files to markdown", export of "any book (text or audio) to any other support format, giving the option to export part of a text book as audio too", and, for text, "rtf, dot, .doc, and braille .brf / .brl files (writing the encoder and a decoder for this as well)"; items marked "The user's wish" are those. The items run from the easiest to the hardest, an item that needs another coming after it. Difficulty: S is up to about 150 lines of Swift besides tests; M about 150 to 500, or a new path through pieces that exist; L about 500 to 1,000, a new library or helper, or a new playback path; XL more, or an undocumented format. Impact is for this user, 1 to 5. Sample files are in `TestData/format-samples/<folder>/`, listed in `samples.tsv` with each file's source, licence, and SHA-256; every audio sample is tones, never system-voice speech.

**The user's answers (October 17)** to the research's 21 questions, in section 12 of
`FORMATS-RESEARCH-2026-10-17.md`, change these entries:
- FMT13: textutil with headings by font size, "Do this for now, I would personally like the ability to keep bold and
  other formatting if possible for non heading text in case one day we might want to add the ability to speak
  differently formatted text like with a different pitch or something."
- FMT16: guess the encoding with no question at import, and "if its possible maybe write something to guess if the
  encoding can not be decoded and show a alert when opening a book to ask if its a different encoding (try guessing
  first)"; also "it might be good to show a alert when opening a garbled file alerting the user it might be corrupted or
  DRMed" (a DRM-protected EPUB opened garbled; TextMouth will never remove protection).
- FMT02: refuse with advice the non-Apple formats only: "Why not Pages? The non Apple ones I would aggree with." Pages
  becomes an item of its own: when Pages is installed, ask it to export the document (to DOCX or EPUB) and import that;
  a reader of Pages' own format (zipped, compressed, undocumented) would be a much larger job.
- FMT20 to FMT22: "It should support multiple braille tables"; how BrailleBlaster and other tools get their tables, and
  whether macOS lets an app use its own, is being researched (agent `fmtmeasure`); liblouis is chosen: "0.6 MB isn't
  bad, just go with that if thats all it adds."; first a BRF read aloud as text, then "the ability to encode to be able
  to convert any markdown intermediate parsed format into a braille book"; the BRF export defaults as recommended.
- FMT31 and FMT50: wait on the rarer audio formats ("I do see WebM more then the others"); measure a bundled ffmpeg
  with a test build before choosing between the installed one and a bundled one: "it looks like my current install of
  Text Mouth is 18.3 mb and I think I said I wanted to keep it below 30 mb if possible? Try measuring it using a test
  build." (agent `fmtmeasure`).
- FMT14: lossless sources to Apple Lossless, yes; keeping originals beside converted files is a setting, off by default.
- FMT10, FMT12, FMT24: a playlist or cue sheet uses only files in its own folder for now, "maybe let adding trusted
  folders like ~/Music or something?"
- FMT38: OPML folders, yes, "add a import button to the add feed sheet too."
- FMT43 and FMT44: the speech export dialog has "the rate and pitch in the dialog as sliders set to the current rate";
  its ranges are "headings / chapter ranges, and then I was thinking like timestamp to timestamp"; resuming, "Yes, and
  think about how to optimize this."
- FMT18 and the export order: yes; Markdown stays the internal base of every text book, and HTML is only the first
  step of export, from which EPUB 3, DOCX, RTF, and PDF are written.
- Everything else as recommended (FMT15's rows, FMT07 and FMT08, FMT23 and FMT25, the speech license line).
- **Measured the same day** (agent `fmtmeasure`, `Scripts/agents/briefs/2026-10-17/formats/measurements.md`): an LGPL-only
  arm64 ffmpeg 9.0.2 with every wanted decoder (WMA, Matroska and WebM, Ogg, FLAC, Monkey's Audio, WavPack, AMR, AC-3)
  is about 2.1 MB stripped with `--enable-small --enable-lto`, so the app would be about 20.4 MB, under the user's 30
  MB; the formats themselves are only 0.47 MB of it. liblouis 3.39.0 is 0.17 MB, and its 24 core English tables (UEB
  and EBAE Grades 1 and 2, computer braille) 0.34 MB, about 0.55 MB in all; the tables for VoiceOver's other languages
  are 3.5 MB more (all 478 tables 13.9 MB). BrailleBlaster and NVDA ship liblouis's tables themselves; macOS 26's
  public `AXBrailleTranslator` gave an app no tables. So: FMT50 (bundle ffmpeg) moves ahead of FMT31 (the installed
  one), and FMT21 vendors liblouis with the core English tables, other languages as a pack.

- [x] **FMT01 — Word templates and macro documents (.dotx, .docm, .dotm).** Merged fcb30520, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 2 · The newer forms of the user's .dot. They are the same Office Open XML package as .docx with another main content type, and TextMouth's own Word reader opens fixed paths (word/document.xml, styles, numbering, relationships, core properties) and never the content type, so it reads all three as they are, with real headings; it reads only XML, so a macro project is never touched and no macro runs. Add the extensions wherever .docx is listed (`BookFormat` and the app's document types), with tests that each opens with its headings. About 5 lines plus tests; no size; no new risk. Samples: dotx/, docm/, dotm/ (a real LibreOffice file and a synthetic one each).
- [ ] **FMT02 — Say how to convert what TextMouth refuses.** Difficulty S · Impact 2 · The Nothing Imported alert already says what an archive holds ("It holds 176 .wma files, which TextMouth does not import."). Add a sentence of advice for the formats the research recommends refusing: Pages ("export it from Pages as Word or EPUB"), Keynote (to PowerPoint or PDF), Numbers (to Excel or CSV), WordPerfect and CHM (to Word or HTML), DjVu and XPS (to PDF), a real RAR comic (repack it as CBZ), and copy-protected audio (Audible .aa and .aax, .m4p: "TextMouth cannot open copy-protected files."). One table of extensions and sentences, used for a single file too, its wording pinned by tests; about 60 lines. Samples: pages/ (Pages 4 and 5 files), wpd/WP6.wpd.
- [x] **FMT03 — Safari web archives (.webarchive).** Merged fcb30520, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 2 · A web archive is a binary property list whose WebMainResource holds the page's HTML and its text encoding. Read it with `PropertyListSerialization` (no WebKit, nothing loaded from the network), decode WebResourceData by WebResourceTextEncodingName, and give it to `HTMLToMarkdownConverter`, so headings, lists, and tables arrive as in an HTML book. About 40 lines plus tests. Risks: Safari's archives also hold subframes and many subresources (read only the main resource; cap the size); one written by Safari was not tried. Sample: webarchive/headings.webarchive.
- [x] **FMT04 — A DTBook file on its own (.xml), and NIMAS.** Merged fcb30520, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 2 · The DAISY reader already reads DTBook in a DAISY 3 package, and in a ZIP or folder without an OPF, so a NIMAS package (DTBook, OPF, images) should import today (untested: add a test); a single loose .xml file fails, since `BookFormat` does not list it. Sniff an .xml file's root element for dtbook, refuse other XML by name, and give the reader a one-file provider: about 55 lines plus tests. NIMAS files reach students through schools and Bookshare, never as public files. Samples: dtbook/harbour-standalone.xml, dtbook/nimas-style-package/.
- [x] **FMT05 — Nero chapter lists (chpl) in M4B and M4A files.** Merged 81d6bae4, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 3 · AVFoundation reads an MPEG-4 file's QuickTime chapter track, MP3's ID3 CHAP frames, and FLAC's CHAPTER comments, but not a Nero chpl atom alone (verified with a file whose chapter-track reference was renamed), and some audiobook tools write only chpl (inferred), so such a book shows no headings. When AVFoundation finds none, read the atom by hand (a count, then each chapter's start in 100-nanosecond units and its title), bounds-checked; about 80 lines plus tests. Sample: m4b-chapters/three-tones.m4b holds both a chapter track and chpl; a test can make the chpl-only form by renaming the track reference, as the research did.
- [ ] **FMT06 — Audacity labels, in and out.** Difficulty S · Impact 2 · Audacity's label file is UTF-8 text, a label a line: start, end, and text, tab-separated, in seconds; equal times make a point, others a region, and a line starting with a backslash holds a spectral selection, to skip. Import a label file beside an audiobook as bookmarks (as headings once FMT24 brings headings inside one file), and export a book's headings, bookmarks, or the audio editor's markers as labels, for a round trip with an audio editor. About 60 lines for the format, plus the commands and their wording. Samples: audacity-labels/ (labels.txt and labels-spectral.txt beside a four-second tone).
- [x] **FMT07 — SSML files, imported as text.** Merged fcb30520, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 2 · An .ssml file becomes a text book with its markup removed (paragraphs and sentences kept), never handed to the speech engine as SSML or as text with tags: `AVSpeechUtterance(ssmlRepresentation:)` (macOS 13 and later) turns off the book's rate and pitch, ignored sub aliases, and crashed the research probe on every emphasis element (macOS 26.3.1), and `SpeechMarkupGuard` exists because the engine mistakes tag-shaped text for markup. About 100 lines plus tests; native SSML speech is set aside. Sample: ssml/passage.ssml (it holds the emphasis that crashed the probe, on purpose).
- [ ] **FMT08 — Pronunciation lexicons (PLS) as substitution rules.** Difficulty S · Impact 2 · A W3C PLS 1.0 lexicon's alias entries map onto TextMouth's substitution rules (grapheme to pattern, alias to replacement, whole word, case-insensitive), imported into a book's or the global rules and exported from them. Its IPA phonemes have no text form (Apple takes no lexicon file, and IPA works only per word through `AVSpeechSynthesisIPANotationAttribute`), so phonemes are set aside. About 150 lines plus tests. Sample: pls-lexicon/lexicon.pls.
- [x] **FMT09 — Audio formats macOS already decodes.** Merged 81d6bae4, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 3 · The user's wish ("convert a bunch more audio formats to a standardized format"). Verified on files made here: afconvert and AVFoundation read AC-3, E-AC-3, AMR-NB (.amr and in .3gp), MP1 and MP2, NeXT/Sun .au and .snd, AIFC (IMA4, μ-law), CAF (PCM, ALAC, Opus), Wave64, RF64, BWF, and 3GP with AAC, so only their extensions are missing (aifc, caf, au, snd, w64, ac3, ec3, eac3, mp1, mp2, mpa, amr, awb, 3gp, 3g2, 3ga). Generalize `OggAudioConverter` into one import converter whose plan says, per file, convert to AAC .m4a with the watched afconvert (every coded format, so Apple's rarer decoders stay out of the player, as October 14 taught) or play as it is (plain PCM), each checked by `AudioMediaProbe` as Ogg is; Ogg keeps its own header reader. About 150 lines plus tests; no size. Risks: hostile files in Apple's decoders, contained by the child process; AMR-WB untested. Samples: ac3/, eac3/, amr/, mp2/, au/, aifc/, caf/, w64/, rf64/, bwf/, 3gp/, wav-variants/.
- [x] **FMT10 — M3U and M3U8 playlists.** Merged 81d6bae4, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 3 · A playlist of local files becomes one book of tracks, as a ZIP or folder does, through a manifest written in memory: a heading per entry, titled by #EXTINF or else the file name, in the playlist's order. Decode with `TextFileDecoder` (a legacy .m3u is Windows-1252 or Latin-1, an .m3u8 UTF-8); accept relative paths, backslashes, percent escapes, and file: URLs; refuse an HLS streaming playlist (any #EXT-X- tag, or only http lines) in a sentence, and never fetch a URL. TextMouth is not sandboxed, so a playlist could name any file: use only files inside the playlist's folder (question 11) and name the entries skipped. About 150 lines plus tests. Samples: m3u/ (Latin-1, CRLF, accents, a backslash path, edge cases), m3u8/ (UTF-8, plus HLS media and master playlists), each with three one-second tone tracks.
- [ ] **FMT11 — Chapter markers in WAV, AIFF, and CAF exports.** Difficulty S · Impact 2 · The audio editor's Export says these three formats leave chapters out, but Core Audio writes markers into all of them: `AudioFileSetProperty` with `kAudioFilePropertyMarkerList` succeeded on each, writing cue and adtl, MARK, and mark chunks with the names, and ffprobe read the WAV's back (verified). Write the chapters as markers once the audio is written, drop the warning, and check which players read them. About 80 lines plus tests. Sample: bwf/ (a WAV with two cue markers).
- [ ] **FMT12 — PLS and XSPF playlists.** Difficulty S · Impact 1 · After FMT10, the same import for PLS (an INI file: FileN, TitleN, LengthN; mostly internet-radio links, which are refused) and XSPF (XML: trackList, location URIs resolved against xml:base, title, creator), with FMT10's folder rule. About 130 lines plus tests. Samples: pls-playlist/, xspf/.
- [x] **FMT13 — RTF, Word 97-2003 .doc, its template .dot, RTFD, ODT, OTT, and WordML.** Merged fcb30520, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 5 · The user's wish ("rtf, dot, .doc"). Apple's `/usr/bin/textutil` reads all of them by content, a .dot exactly as a .doc (verified on files made here: 0.04 to 0.16 s and 12 to 15 MB for ordinary files, 1.1 s and 59 MB for a 3.3 MB RTF). Run `textutil -convert html` as a child process under `WatchedProcess`, as afconvert runs for Ogg, so a hang or crash in Apple's readers costs a child and not the import, and give its HTML to `HTMLToMarkdownConverter`: lists, tables, accents, and curly quotes survive. Headings do not: every format comes back with each heading as a bold, larger paragraph (verified, even RTF with Word's heading styles and ODT with real text:h), so add a pass that takes the body's size as the commonest by characters and makes a short bold or larger paragraph a heading, its level by size rank. An RTFD is a folder, imported as one book. About 220 lines with the runner, plus tests. Risks: bold run-in headings and decorative text fool the size rule (FMT47 is the fallback); encrypted and Word 6 and 95 files are untested. Samples: rtf/, rtfd/, doc/ (one made here and two real Word-format files from LibreOffice's tests with Heading 1 to 6), dot/ (a synthetic one with the template bit set, and a real one), odt/, ott/, wordml/.
- [x] **FMT14 — Lossless audio stays lossless, and the original is kept.** Merged 81d6bae4, October 18 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 3 · The user's wish, kept honest. Convert lossless sources to Apple Lossless in .m4a (the same container, tags, and chapters as the AAC path; the editor already exports it) and lossy ones to AAC as today; this lets TextMouth accept Ogg FLAC, refused today, which afconvert turns into ALAC (verified). Keep the original beside the converted file in the package (the Ogg import deletes it, `FileImportService+Ogg.swift`), behind a setting (question 10), offered by Export's Original file. Correct docs/user/what-textmouth-reads.md, whose "so it stays about the same size and sounds no worse" a second lossy encoding cannot promise (ChatGPT's correction holds). About 200 lines plus tests. Risk: converted books take about twice the disk. Samples: caf/ (ALAC in CAF), flac-chapters/three-tones.flac, and the Ogg tones in TestData/audio.
- [ ] **FMT15 — CSV and TSV.** Difficulty M · Impact 3 · RFC 4180 is only informational: write a small state machine (quoted fields with commas, line breaks, and doubled quotes), sniff the delimiter (comma, semicolon, tab, pipe) from the first lines, decode with `TextFileDecoder`, and stream large files. A row becomes a paragraph, its cells joined by ", " and the header row first (today's HTML tables join cells with a space only, which reads badly), with a setting to say each column's name before its cell (question 15) and a row cap with a notice for huge files. About 250 lines plus tests. Samples: csv/books.csv, tsv/books.tsv.
- [x] **FMT16 — Text files in other encodings.** Merged 1cf19935, October 24 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 4 · `TextFileDecoder` reads anything that is not UTF-8 or UTF-16 as Windows-1252, so Mac Roman, Central European, Cyrillic (Windows-1251, KOI8-R), and Japanese or Chinese text files arrive garbled, with no word to the listener. Foundation's `NSString.stringEncoding(for:encodingOptions:convertedString:usedLossyConversion:)` named the right encoding for 11 of 13 short files made here, but not for Mac Roman or KOI8-R, and it never says it is unsure. Use it as a guess after today's checks, name the encoding in Book Info ("Read as Windows-1251"), and add a Reopen with Encoding command (question 2); a letter-frequency check can catch Mac Roman and KOI8-R. About 150 lines plus the command's sheet and tests. Samples: txt-encodings/ (one short text in 13 encodings, each file named for its encoding).
- [ ] **FMT17 — Markers and Ogg chapter comments read as chapters.** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · After FMT05, the chapter sources TextMouth still ignores: a WAV's or BWF's cue and adtl labels and a CAF's mark chunk (afinfo lists them; AVFoundation's chapter call does not, inferred), read with Core Audio's marker list; and Ogg's CHAPTERnn comments, read by `OggHeaderReader` and written into the converted .m4a's chapter track, which the editor's `AudioChapterTrack` already writes. About 200 lines plus tests. Samples: bwf/ (two markers), caf/.
- [x] **FMT18 — Export with conversion: a Convert to choice, with HTML first.** Merged 0db62a38, October 19 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 4 · The user's wish ("exporting any book (text or audio) to any other support format"). The Export dialog only copies today. Give each kind a Convert to choice (text: HTML here, then FMT19, FMT22, FMT28, and FMT29; audio: FMT30), a job runner with the dialog's progress, Stop, and VoiceOver sentences, and names that never replace a file, as now. The first writer is HTML from content.md through TextMouth's own swift-markdown walker (escaped XHTML with h1 to h6 and ids, the language, a title, a contents list), because swift-markdown 0.9.0's `HTMLFormatter` inserts text unescaped, so an & or < breaks the page, and drops heading ids (verified in its source); the same walker feeds every text writer. About 300 lines plus tests.
- [x] **FMT19 — RTF export with real outline levels, and .doc through Apple's writer.** Merged 0db62a38, October 19 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 3 · The user's wish (RTF, .doc). After FMT18. Apple's RTF writer drops heading styles (verified), so write RTF from the walker (a font table, a style sheet with heading 1 to 6 and \outlinelevel, Unicode as \uN), about 120 lines, and check in Word that its navigation pane lists the headings. Offer .doc only through `NSAttributedString`'s .docFormat writer, saying its headings are plain: writing Word's binary format ourselves is unwise. Sample: rtf/word-styles.rtf is the form to write.
- [x] **FMT20 — Reading BRF, BRL, and PEF files.** Merged 3996434d, October 20 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 3 · The user's wish (the decoder). BRF is the 64-character North American Braille ASCII (0x20 to 0x5F, lowercase read as uppercase) in finished pages, usually 40 cells by 25 lines with form feeds; BRL is the same characters unformatted (Orbit Reader's guide); PEF is XML of Unicode braille rows. Map each character to its Unicode cell (the fixed table, verified 64 of 64 against liblouis's own), split pages at form feeds or every 25 lines, take the braille page number from the last line, find headings from centered lines with blank lines around them and from cell-5 and cell-7 lines (BANA's Braille Formats, 2016), and turn uncontracted (Grade 1) UEB into print with TextMouth's own decoder (about 110 lines; the prototype round-trips the sample). Contracted braille needs FMT21. About 400 lines with the page model, plus tests. Risks: BRF has no standard (widths 18 to 80, CRLF or LF, 8-dot files), and headings are a heuristic. Samples: brf/ (Grade 1 Pride and Prejudice chapter 1, two pages, with its print text; a short Grade 2 passage with its contractions named), brl/, pef/.
- [x] **FMT21 — Contracted braille through liblouis.** Merged 3996434d, October 20 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 5 · The user's wish (a decoder and an encoder that work on real BRF files). BARD's and Bookshare's BRF files are contracted UEB (Grade 2), and turning them into print, or print into Grade 2, needs a translator. liblouis is the standard (LGPL 2.1 or later, the UEB tables too): vendor its release in Vendor/liblouis and build it statically as LAME is, with its source, licence, and notice, bundling only the UEB tables (9 files, 239 KB on master; about 0.5 to 0.7 MB with the library), and call it on one serial queue (treat it as not thread-safe). First, a one-day spike: macOS 26 ships liblouis 3.34.0 privately and adds a public `AXBrailleTranslator` (Accessibility.framework), which gave a command-line probe no tables (verified); if an app bundle gets the UEB tables, use it on macOS 26 and liblouis on 14 and 15 (question 5). About 200 lines of Swift besides the library, plus tests. Risk: back-translation is ambiguous (no accuracy figure was found), and a wrong contraction misreads a book. Samples: brf/grade2-short.brf with its print text and contractions.
- [x] **FMT22 — Export to BRF and PEF.** Merged 3996434d, October 20 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 4 · The user's wish (the encoder). After FMT18, and FMT21 for Grade 2. Lay out pages as BANA's Braille Formats (2016) says: 40 cells by 25 lines by default, centered headings with blank lines around them, paragraphs in cells 3 and 1, no word divided at a line's end, the braille page number at the right of the last line, a form feed between pages, CR LF; BRF in braille ASCII, PEF as XML. Settings: cells per line (18 to 80), lines per page, grade 1 or 2, page numbers, and line ends (question 7). Grade 1 can be TextMouth's own (the research prototype encodes, lays out, and writes both formats, with a reader and decoder, in 428 lines of Python); Grade 2 is liblouis. About 250 lines with liblouis, 500 to 600 with our own Grade 1, plus tests. Samples: brf/ and pef/ are the prototype's output, passing its 57 checks.
- [x] **FMT23 — SRT and WebVTT transcripts.** Difficulty M · Impact 4 · Attach an existing transcript to an audiobook instead of transcribing it, on any macOS: a .srt or .vtt beside a track with the same name (lecture.vtt beside lecture.m4a, or per track in a ZIP or folder), or chosen by a Book menu command (question 12). Each cue becomes a `TranscriptSentence` (text, start, end, no word times), and WebVTT's voice tags become "Name: " text, with the file's fingerprint and length recorded as the recognizer records them. Export a transcript as either format, a file per track or one for the book. One cue model, two readers, two writers: about 250 lines plus tests. Whether a saved transcript shows on macOS 14 and 15 is assumed, not checked. Samples: srt/, vtt/ (voice tags, NOTE, both time forms), each beside a four-second tone. **Done** in 0.4.2 (November 8, 2026): see the entry "Transcripts that come with podcasts and audiobooks (FMT23, FMT25)".
- [ ] **FMT24 — CUE sheets.** Difficulty M · Impact 4 · A heading per track for one-file audiobooks and CD rips: FILE, TRACK, TITLE, PERFORMER, and INDEX 01 in minutes, seconds, and frames of 1/75 s (INDEX 00 pregaps ignored); several FILEs map onto the manifest's segments; the sheet is decoded by `TextFileDecoder`; a FILE that does not match falls back to the same stem beside the sheet (sheets often name a .wav later compressed to .flac). Also the FLAC CUESHEET block, which AVFoundation does not read (verified), an APE or WavPack Cuesheet tag (with FMT31), and CUE export of a book's chapters. Paths outside the sheet's folder follow question 11. About 350 lines plus tests. Samples: cue/ (album.cue with a BOM, CRLF, a pregap, and frame offsets; two-files.cue in Latin-1 over two files), flac-chapters/three-tones.flac (a CUESHEET block).
- [x] **FMT25 — A podcast episode's own transcript and chapters.** Difficulty M · Impact 5 · Podcasting 2.0 feeds link a transcript (WebVTT, SRT, JSON, HTML, or text) and a chapters file (JSON, version 1.2.0); TextMouth stores both links on each item (`FeedItem.transcripts` and `.chapters`) and never fetches them. After FMT23, fetch them beside the episode's download under its network rules (WebVTT first, then JSON, then SRT; text and HTML carry no times; question 13), make the transcript the episode's (text at once, on any macOS, with no recognition), and make the chapters its headings when it has none of its own (startTime and title; toc false hidden). About 300 lines plus tests. Risks: third-party text (cap its size, treat it as data, follow none of its links), and a transcript of another edition, since dynamic ads shift times (compare durations). Samples: podcast-transcript-json/, podcast-chapters-json/. **Done** in 0.4.2 (November 8, 2026): see the entry "Transcripts that come with podcasts and audiobooks (FMT23, FMT25)".
- [ ] **FMT26 — FictionBook (.fb2, .fb2.zip).** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · XML books, common among Russian-language public-domain e-books: a title-info description, nested sections whose titles give the heading levels, epigraphs, poems, notes, and base64 images. Read with `XMLParser`, honouring the prolog's encoding (windows-1251 is common), and .fb2.zip through ZIPFoundation. About 250 lines plus tests; no library. - [ ] **FMT29 — DOCX and ODT export with real heading styles.** Difficulty M · Impact 3 · The user's wish. After FMT18. - [ ] **FMT30 — Audiobooks exported in another format, and a book of tracks as one file.** Difficulty M · Impact 3 · The user's wish ("any book (text or audio)"). - [ ] **FMT31 — Audio formats macOS cannot read, through the installed ffmpeg.** Difficulty M · Impact 3 · The user's wish. macOS refuses WMA, WavPack, TTA, Speex, all Matroska and WebM, FLV, and AVI with MP3 (verified), and has no APE or Musepack decoder. `FFmpeg.swift` already finds an installed ffmpeg, and Homebrew's has aac_at, Apple's own AAC encoder inside ffmpeg (verified), so one watched run converts and keeps chapters and tags: `-nostdin -protocol_whitelist file`, the demuxer forced from TextMouth's own sniff of the first bytes, `-map 0:a:N -map_chapters 0 -map_metadata 0 -c:a aac_at` at the rate the Ogg converter would choose, or ALAC for lossless sources (FMT14), under `WatchedProcess`'s limits. Without ffmpeg, the alert says what to install (question 9). Formats: WMA (v1, v2, Pro, Lossless, Voice), WavPack, TTA, APE, Speex, Musepack, RealAudio, and the sound of AVI, FLV, and MPEG-TS; DSD later (huge files that need decimating). About 450 lines, shared with FMT32, plus tests. Risks: ffmpeg's demuxers face hostile files, in a child process; copy-protected WMA must fail with a clear sentence. Samples: wma/, wv/, tta/, spx/; APE, Musepack, DSD, and WMA Pro, Lossless, and Voice are listed by URL only in the research document.
- [ ] **FMT32 — Matroska and WebM audio: the right track, with chapters.** Difficulty M · Impact 3 · The user's wish. After FMT31: .mka, .mkv, .webm, and .weba hold Opus, Vorbis, AAC, FLAC, or AC-3, often in several tracks, which ffprobe's JSON names by language and title. Take the default track, else the first in the system's language, else the first; when there are several, a sheet lists them ("Narration, English"). Copy an AAC track without re-encoding; Matroska's chapters become headings (verified: an .mka's two chapters came through ffmpeg into an .m4a that AVFoundation read); refuse linked segments. About 250 lines plus tests. Samples: mka/tones-2tracks.mka (two Opus tracks, English and German, two chapters), webm/.
- [ ] **FMT33 — PowerPoint slides (.pptx, .ppsx).** Difficulty M · Impact 3 · Lecture slides as a book: each slide's title a heading (else "Slide N"), its text in order, tables a row at a time, pictures' alt text, and optional speaker notes; slide order from presentation.xml's sldIdLst through its relationships, not file names; hidden slides skipped. ZIPFoundation and `XMLParser`, as the DOCX reader is. About 350 lines plus tests; legacy .ppt is out. Sample: pptx/tides.pptx (made with pandoc: four slides, notes, a table).
- [ ] **FMT34 — Pictures of pages, and comic archives (CBZ).** Difficulty M · Impact 4 · Letters, menus, and mail arrive as photographs. ImageIO reads PNG, JPEG, HEIC, and TIFF (a multi-page TIFF gives a chapter per page), and Vision's text recognition, which already reads scanned PDF pages, read every line of the samples made here (verified), except a PNG of text on a transparent background, which gave nothing: flatten onto white first, apply the EXIF orientation, cap the pixel size before decoding, and show progress, since each page takes seconds. A CBZ (page images in a ZIP in natural name order, with an optional ComicInfo.xml) follows at little cost; Vision gives no panel order. About 450 lines plus tests. Risk: untrusted images decoded in the app (ImageIO has had vulnerabilities), so a watched child if one hangs. Samples: png/ (with page-one-transparent.png), jpeg/, heic/, tiff-multipage/two-pages.tiff, cbz/harbour-notices.cbz.
- [ ] **FMT35 — Excel workbooks (.xlsx).** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · After FMT15's way of reading rows: a heading per sheet (hidden sheets skipped), shared strings, booleans, and cached formula values, and dates turned from serial numbers into dates through the styles' number formats and the 1900 or 1904 epoch, or a listener hears "45123"; stream large sheets. About 450 lines plus tests; legacy .xls is set aside. Sample: xlsx/inventory.xlsx.
- [ ] **FMT36 — OpenDocument presentations and spreadsheets (.odp, .ods).** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · The ODF forms of FMT33 and FMT35: a draw:page per slide, with its title frame and notes, and a table:table per sheet, with repeated rows and columns expanded but capped, since a blank trailing run can claim a million rows. About 250 lines each, sharing their XML walker, plus tests. Samples: odp/tides.odp, ods/inventory.ods (written by script; not opened in an ODF program).
- [ ] **FMT37 — LRC and TTML timed text.** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · After FMT23, two more readers for its cue model: LRC (lyrics; [mm:ss.xx] lines, enhanced <mm:ss.xx> word times, offsets; about 80 lines) and TTML or IMSC (W3C XML; clock, offset, frame, and tick times, agents for speakers; about 150 lines). Samples: lrc/ (simple and enhanced), ttml/.
- [ ] **FMT38 — OPML folders.** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · OPML import and export already work, flat and without duplicates; folder outlines, which Overcast, Pocket Casts, and Apple write, are dropped. Keep them as the feed folder each feed goes into, and write them on export (question 14); this needs a group on each feed. About 200 lines plus tests. Sample: opml/subscriptions.opml (nested folders, duplicates, capitals, a link outline, accents, CJK).
- [ ] **FMT39 — FLAC export with chapters and tags.** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · afconvert writes FLAC (verified) but no tags, so write the Vorbis comment block ourselves (title, artist, and CHAPTERnnn and CHAPTERnnnNAME, the convention ffmpeg reads, verified on the sample) into the file's metadata, and add FLAC to the editor's and Export's formats. About 200 lines in all, half of it the comment block, plus tests. Sample: flac-chapters/three-tones.flac.
- [ ] **FMT40 — eBraille (.ebrl).** Difficulty M · Impact 2 · The DAISY Consortium's eBraille 1.0 (August 2025): an EPUB-like ZIP of XHTML whose text is Unicode braille, with headings, lists, and tables as HTML, reflowable to any line length; HumanWare's displays began reading it in January 2026, and BARD still serves BRF. Read the package with ZIPFoundation and SwiftSoup, and its braille as FMT20 and FMT21 do. About 250 lines plus tests. Sample: none (no clearly free file).
- [ ] **FMT41 — MIDI files.** Difficulty M · Impact 1 · macOS ships a General MIDI bank (gs_instruments.dls, 2 MB): render a .mid offline to PCM with `AVAudioSequencer` and `AVAudioUnitSampler` in a manual-rendering `AVAudioEngine`, then encode it to AAC; marker events (FF 06) become chapters. About 300 lines plus tests; rendering was not tried. Sample: mid/tones.mid (three notes, two markers).
- [x] **FMT42 — MOBI (DRM-free Kindle books).** **Done:** merged as 7d6c4174, November 5, in version 0.3.0 (see the merge entry, "Kindle books in the MOBI format"). Difficulty M · Impact 2 · PalmDB records: record 0's PalmDOC header (compression; encryption at offset 12) and MOBI header (EXTH title, author, language; DRM fields at offsets 168 to 180, 0xFFFFFFFF for none), refusing any file with DRM. Most free MOBI files are MOBI 6, not KF8 (verified on Gutenberg's): PalmDOC LZ77 (a 25-line decompressor reproduced the text exactly), the old HTML dialect (filepos anchors, mbp:pagebreak, no h1 to h3), chapters from the NCX index records, and HUFF/CDIC compression in some. About 500 lines plus tests, written from the MobileRead wiki (libmobi is LGPL 3, KindleUnpack and calibre GPL 3: study only). Samples: mobi/pg1952-yellow-wallpaper.mobi (Project Gutenberg, public domain), mobi/yellow-wallpaper-drm-flagged-synthetic.mobi. **Revised November 4** (the user's wish: import DRM-free .mobi, .azw3, and .kfx books, at least into the reading text; `FORMAT-LIBRARIES-RESEARCH-2026-11-04.md`, Part 2): about 700 lines with tests, the extra for the trailing entries, the INDX, TAGX, and CNCX index records, and turning filepos offsets into anchors before the HTML converter reads the text; HUFF/CDIC about 120 more, and can come second. foliate-js's `mobi.js` (MIT, verified) is a permissive reference for the whole reader, to port from its algorithm. Refuse when the encryption field is not 0 or the DRM offset is not 0xFFFFFFFF. Print pages come only from an APNX file beside the book (layout not verified).
- [x] **FMT43 — Speech to audio, stage 1: a book or a range of headings as M4B, M4A, or MP3.** Difficulty L · Impact 5 · The user's wish ("giving the option to export part of a text book as audio"). After FMT18. For each chapter in the range (question 18), render the same reading text the reader speaks (substitution rules, pauses, `SpeechMarkupGuard`, the book's voice, rate, and pitch: question 17) with a fresh `AVSpeechSynthesizer`'s `write(_:toBufferCallback:)`, as `AudioEngineService` does, in the reader's chunk size, appending the buffers to a 16-bit PCM CAF for the chapter as they arrive; then encode and chapter through the editor's renderer: AAC M4B or M4A with a chapter per heading, or MP3 with ID3 chapters. With `say -o` as a proxy the voices render 18 to 128 times real time, so a 10-hour book takes about 5 to 35 minutes and 1.6 GB of temporary disk: check free space first, hold a power assertion, and keep finished chapter files so a stopped export resumes (question 19). Personal Voice is reported unable to render to buffers: refuse it here. One line under the choice says speech made with the Mac's voices is for personal, non-commercial use under Apple's licence (question 20). About 600 lines plus the dialog's part, and tests that never sound aloud. Samples: none (speech may not be kept); the chapter samples check the output's chapters. **Done** in 0.4.2 (November 8, 2026): see the entry "Spoken-audio export (FMT43)".
- [ ] **FMT44 — Speech to audio, stage 2: every format, a selection, and resuming.** Difficulty L · Impact 4 · The user's wish. After FMT43: WAV, AIFF, and CAF with markers (FMT11), Apple Lossless, and FLAC (FMT39); a selection from the reader as well as a range of headings; resuming a stopped export at its next chapter; time and disk estimates before starting; RF64 past WAV's 4 GB. About 400 lines plus tests.
- [ ] **FMT45 — Email (.eml, .mbox).** Difficulty L · Impact 3 · Archived mail, newsletters, and support threads: a heading per message ("Subject, from, date"), then its body. Foundation has no MIME parser: header folding, RFC 2047 encoded words, quoted-printable and base64, charsets, multipart/alternative (the plain part preferred, else the HTML part through the existing converter), format=flowed, attachments listed by name, nested messages, and no remote images; mbox per RFC 4155, which prescribes no escaping, so read mboxrd and mboxo and find the separator by its pattern, streaming line by line. About 850 lines plus tests. Samples: eml/cafe-hours.eml (a quoted-printable ISO-8859-1 plain part and an HTML part), mbox/three.mbox (>From and >>From lines).
- [ ] **FMT46 — EPUB 3 with Media Overlays (read-along books).** Difficulty L · Impact 3 · Publisher narration synchronized to the text: SMIL par elements, each a text fragment and an audio clip. Import such an EPUB as an audiobook in SMIL order, with headings from nav.xhtml and a transcript built from the pars (each fragment's text and clip times), so it follows the narration as a transcribed audiobook does, on any macOS. The DAISY reader's SMIL code carries over, but its `parseTime` reads SMIL timecounts with h, min, or ms as 0 ("500ms" gives 0; `DAISYParser+Audio.swift`, line 129, verified), a fix this needs and DAISY 3 books may want now. About 500 lines plus tests. Risks: src paths that climb out of the package; Ogg audio inside goes through the converter. Sample: epub3-media-overlays/three-tones-read-along.epub (three pars in three clip-time forms).
- [x] **FMT47 — TextMouth's own readers of Word's and RTF's heading styles.** **Done for rich text, RTFD, and Word 97-2003:** merged November 5 in version 0.3.1, on the Mac too (see the merge entry, "TextMouth's own readers of rich text and Word 97-2003"); OpenDocument and Word 2003 XML still go to textutil. Difficulty L · Impact 3 · The user's wish, if FMT13's font sizes get headings wrong in the user's files. .doc per Microsoft's [MS-DOC]: the compound file, the FIB, the piece table, the style sheet (built-in Heading 1 to 9), and each paragraph's style and outline level from the PAPX pages; a 150-line Python prototype found Heading 1 to 6 in a real Word file (verified); about 450 to 600 lines of Swift, every offset bounds-checked (a classic fuzz target), refusing encrypted files. RTF: groups, destinations, \stylesheet names and \outlinelevel, \u and \' escapes with code pages, tables and lists: about 500 to 650 lines. The compound-file reader would also serve legacy .xls. Samples: doc/ (real Word-format files with heading styles), rtf/word-styles.rtf. **The user (November 4):** "I think small Text Mouth readers for .doc /odt etc might be good for both so formatting extraction is under our control, same for kfx": TextMouth's own reader, in the Mac app and the iOS app alike, replacing textutil on the Mac once checked against its reading (`FORMAT-LIBRARIES-RESEARCH-2026-11-04.md`, "Your answers").
- [x] **FMT48 — AZW3 (KF8).** Difficulty L · Impact 2 · After FMT42: KF8 adds skeleton, fragment, and flow tables (FDST) and kindle:pos and kindle:embed links on top of MOBI's records, about 700 lines more; most AZW3 books are copy-protected and refused. Sample: none (libmobi's test files are listed by URL only). **Revised November 4** (`FORMAT-LIBRARIES-RESEARCH-2026-11-04.md`, Part 2): about 600 lines more than FMT42, near 1,300 for both, with foliate-js's `mobi.js` (MIT) as the reference: join each part's skeleton and fragments (SKEL, FRAG, FDST), rewrite kindle:pos, kindle:flow, and kindle:embed links, and in a hybrid file jump to the KF8 part that EXTH 121 names, checking DRM in both halves. A KF8 file can be named .mobi, so the reader goes by the MOBI header's version, not the extension. Samples: azw3/se-the-time-machine.azw3 (Standard Ebooks, CC0), mobi/pg1952-yellow-wallpaper-kf8.mobi (Project Gutenberg's KF8 link); no hybrid yet (calibre's ebook-convert, on this Mac, can make one from a public-domain EPUB). **Done** in 0.4.2 (November 8, 2026): see the entry "Kindle books in KF8: .azw3, and KF8 .mobi (FMT48)".
- [ ] **FMT49 — Tagged PDF export with an outline.** Difficulty L · Impact 2 · The user's wish (any supported format). After FMT18. AppKit's PDF has no structure, but Core Graphics' `CGPDFContextBeginTag` writes one (verified: H1 and P tags, MarkInfo marked): lay out pages with Core Text, tag headings and paragraphs, set the language and title, and add an outline of the headings with PDFKit. About 400 lines plus tests; for sighted sharing and printing, since a blind reader prefers EPUB, HTML, or DOCX.
- [ ] **FMT50 — A bundled ffmpeg.** Difficulty L · Impact 2 · Only if FMT31's formats should work without Homebrew (question 9). Build an ffmpeg executable with only the needed demuxers and decoders, AudioToolbox for AAC, and no GPL or non-free parts; ship it as a separate signed executable in the bundle with its exact source, configure line, and notices (ffmpeg.org's legal checklist), and rebuild it on every ffmpeg security release. About 1.5 to 3 MB, estimated from reports (no build was measured).
- [ ] **FMT51 — TextMouth's own contracted braille translator.** Difficulty XL · Impact 3 · The user's wish taken literally ("writing the encoder and a decoder"), only if question 5 rules out liblouis. A table-driven translator and back-translator for UEB Grade 2 from the ICEB's rulebook (contractions and their position rules, shortforms, bridging, lower signs, typeforms): liblouis's UEB Grade 2 table alone is about 150 KB of rules (2,037 rule lines in Apple's copy). Months of work for less than liblouis gives.
- [x] **FMT52 — KFX (DRM-free Kindle books from Amazon's newer tools).** Merged November 6, version 0.4.0 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty XL · Impact 2 · The user's wish (November 4): import DRM-free .kfx books, at least into the reading text. A CONT container of Ion binary entities with Amazon's numbered symbols (about 850 names): content fragments hold the text, storylines and sections the structure and reading order, and the book's navigation the TOC and the print page list. No permissive reader exists in any language (kfxlib and boko are GPL 3: study only; ebook-rs's MIT "reader" only scans for text); Amazon's Ion libraries are Apache 2.0 but know nothing of KFX. About 1,700 to 2,300 lines: an Ion reader (about 400), the container (300), the storylines (800 to 1,200), and the navigation (150). First, a few lines: recognize KFX, and refuse a protected one, which begins EA 44 52 4D 49 4F 4E EE ("DRMION") instead of CONT, as other protected books are refused. Variants: .azw8, .kfx-zip (a ZIP of containers), .kpf (Kindle Create's package, a ZIP or a SQLite file). Samples: none from Amazon's tools. ebook-rs's Alice KFX was written by boko, a GPL converter, and its terms are unstated. One can be made from a public-domain EPUB with Kindle Previewer 3 and calibre's KFX Output plugin (neither installed), or the user may supply one (`FORMAT-LIBRARIES-RESEARCH-2026-11-04.md`, Part 2). **The user (November 4):** "same for kfx": TextMouth's own KFX reader, in both apps, beyond the refusal of a protected one. **Studied November 5:** the user asked whether calibre's KFX Input plugin can be retrieved to learn what reading needs. It can (2.34.2, GPL v3, from calibre's index; TextMouth is MIT, so study only), and the facts are in `KFX-FORMAT-NOTES-2026-11-05.md`, in an agent's own words, for an implementer who never opens the plugin. Checked on one real container, boko's Alice from ebook-rs: 185 entities, the walk, 13 headings, and 77 pages all resolved; no Amazon-made file was available. About 2,000 to 2,700 lines for `.kfx` and `.kfx-zip`, with `.kpf` 450 to 600 more; the order is the recognizer and refusal, Ion and the container, the walk, navigation, then the folder forms. One real Amazon-made file is needed before calling it done (the question is in `humanQuestions/2026-11-05.md`).
- [x] **FMT53 — Word 2003 XML with its real headings.** Merged November 6, version 0.4.0 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 1 · The user's wish (November 4: TextMouth's own readers, in both apps). One XML file shaped like DOCX's document.xml (`w:wordDocument`, `w:body`, `w:p`, `w:pPr` with `w:pStyle`, `w:r`, `w:t`, `w:tbl`, `w:listPr`, `w:br w:type="page"`, and a `w:styles` section naming the built-in Heading 1 to 9), read with the XML reader's external entities off, sharing the DOCX reader's paragraph and run building, in place of textutil, which reads it now (FMT13). About 150 to 250 lines plus tests, with no library (`FORMAT-LIBRARIES-RESEARCH-2026-11-04.md`, Part 1). Samples: wordml/headings.wordml, wordml/page-breaks.wordml.

Set aside (researched, not recommended now):

- Keynote and Numbers (undocumented IWA; XL), and Pages 5 and later (the same; XL): refused with advice by FMT02.
- WordPerfect (libwpd is over 1 MB of C++), CHM (a port of LZX, about 700 lines; impact 1 to 2), DjVu (DjVuLibre is GPL; XL), and XPS and OXPS (impact 1).
- Real RAR comics (unrar's licence is unfree, and the SDK has no public libarchive header), and legacy .xls (BIFF8, about 900 lines; impact 1).
- Tracker modules (libopenmpt in a helper; impact 1).
- Native SSML speech (it crashes on emphasis, ignores sub, and cancels the rate controls), and IPA phonemes from lexicons.
- Exact braille cells on the display while TextMouth speaks: no macOS API sets a braille string, and Braille Access shows a BRF's cells (question 6).
- Ogg Opus and Vorbis export, Matroska writing, WMA writing, DAISY export, and RTFD as an export.
- EDL and oTranscribe files, OverDrive .odm files (retired and copy-protected), audio CD images (.bin and .cue; impact 1), and Microsoft Reader .lit.
- Every copy-protected format: Audible .aa and .aax, .m4p, and protected WMA and Kindle books.

## Pages across formats (researched October 21)

The user's wish (October 21, 2026): "many other formats (Epub, pdf, docx / doc) at least are separated into pages and I think it would be reasonable if all of them supported them(including updating the parsers the text schema to have a special page tag or simular) so we can "go to page" or rewind and fastforward by page (I think page should just be a navigation unit like the others if you can get that working generally) and a optional page number/count on the reader screen / wear am I speech... Please make a feature section of TODOs for this if you think it would be good and lets make that the feature set implemented first today for the formats using pages."

- **The design** is in `PAGES-RESEARCH-2026-10-21.md` (merged 3c7d43b1): an inline `<!-- page: LABEL -->` marker in content.md, built like the heading annotation, which adds no reading text, so no saved place moves.
- **Questions.** Its four questions are in `humanQuestions/2026-10-21.md`, and their recommended answers are built unless the user says otherwise.
- **Today's order:** PG01; then PG02 to PG04 and PG05 to PG08 in parallel; then B12 and PG09, PG11 (which fixes B13), and PG10. PG12 follows the braille emphasis merge.

- [x] **B12 — A text-extraction update replaces text the user changed with Edit Text.** Merged 77e798a0, October 21 (see the merge entry above). Found by the pages research (section 1.4). Only an edit session that is still open is protected (`BookTextEditSessions.permitTextRebuild`, `MarkdownCacheService.swift:120–123, 200–205`; the batch's `isEditing`, `LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift:512`). `Book` records no finished text edit (its `handEdits` cover metadata), so every cache-version bump so far has written the source's text over hand-edited content.md.
  - **Fix:** record a finished Edit Text change on the book, as a flag and the content.md fingerprint it left. Every automatic re-read (a cache-version bump, PG09's page re-read, a launch check) then keeps that text, while Redo Text Extraction still re-reads on request. Log what was kept.
  - **Tests:** `BookTextEditTests`, `TextCacheVerificationTests`, `TextCacheFingerprintTests`, and a new test in which an edited book survives a version bump.
  - **Before PG09.** Earlier bumps' losses cannot be undone, since nothing recorded them.
- [x] **B13 — DOC, RTF, and WordML books run two words together at each page break.** Merged 77e798a0, October 21 (see the merge entry above). textutil's HTML drops a page break and joins the words on either side ("runs on andcontinues", verified by the pages research, section 3.3), so such books are heard that way today. PG11 fixes it as it adds their pages.

- [x] **PG01 — The page marker and the page model.** Merged 77e798a0, October 21 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 5 · The user's wish ("updating the parsers the text schema to have a special page tag"). The foundation for every other item.
  - **What:** `<!-- page: LABEL -->`, escaped as in section 2.2 (`MarkdownPageMarker`, new, Parsers), and `MarkdownMarkerWriter` (new, Parsers). The writer holds the "never begins a line" placement, moved out of `HTMLToMarkdownConverter` (`linePosition`, `emit`); the converter's heading annotations call it, with their output byte for byte the same.
  - **The parse:** `PlainTextExtractor` records page markers in `visitHTMLBlock` and `visitInlineHTML` as it records annotations, offsets shifted by the trimmed prefix and clamped. `Chapter.pages` and `PageBreak`, `DocumentContent.pages`, and `PagePosition` go in a new `Models/BookPages.swift`. `parseMultiChapterMarkdown` sets each page's chapter. `ReadingTextCache` stores pages (layout 2).
  - **The `includesPages` option** (default true) runs from `MarkdownCacheService`'s extraction to each parser's entry point: EPUB, PDF, DOCX, DAISY, textutil, and plain text, never braille's yet. For now it changes nothing.
  - **Goldens** move to format 2 with `page:` lines (`DocumentDump.swift`, whose header is "format 1" at `:51`), regenerated by `Scripts/test/update-goldens.sh`.
  - **No cache version change.**
  - **Files:** `Parsers/MarkdownPageMarker.swift` (new), `Parsers/MarkdownMarkerWriter.swift` (new), `Parsers/MarkdownParser.swift`, `Parsers/HTMLToMarkdownConverter.swift` (the move only), `Models/BookPages.swift` (new), `Models/DocumentContent.swift`, `Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift`, `Services/ReadingTextCache.swift`, the entry-point signatures of `EPUBParser.swift`, `PDFTextParser.swift`, `DOCXParser.swift`, `DAISYParser.swift`, `TextutilDocumentReader.swift`, and `PlainTextParser.swift`, `TextMouthProductionTests/DocumentDump.swift`, and `TestData/golden/*`.
  - **Tests:** new `MarkdownPageMarkerTests` (labels, escaping, forged and broken comments ignored); new `PageModelTests`:
    - reading text identical with and without markers;
    - every placement;
    - position to page, before the first page, several pages at one offset, and "of N".
  - **Existing classes:** `TextPipelineGoldenTests`, `DocumentDumpTests`, `ReadingTextCacheTests`, `ReadingTextOpenTests`, `UnicodeCoordinateTests`, `LegacyMarkdownCacheTests`, `LegacyDocumentContentModelTests`, `MarkdownTableReadingTests`, `EPUBHeadingRecoveryTests` (the moved writer), `ExportDocumentWalkerTests`, `PlaceRelocatorTests`, `PlaceRelocationAtRebuildTests`, `BookContentIndexTests`, `ContentIndexValidationTests`.
  - **Risks:** offsets after the trimmed prefix; UTF-16 with emoji; golden churn.
  - **Samples:** none (strings in tests). About 400 lines plus tests.
- [x] **PG02 — Page as a navigation unit, with Next Page and Previous Page.** Merged 77e798a0, October 21 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 5 · The user's wish ("rewind and fastforward by page… a navigation unit like the others"). After PG01.
  - **What:** `SkipUnit.page` between Paragraph and Heading. `performSkip` calls `nextPage()` and `previousPage()` (new, `ReaderViewModel+Pages.swift`, modelled on `nextHeading`), as section 4.2 says: Go Back returns, it lands at word starts, skipped sections are passed over, and it announces "Page 13". The F and B cycle passes over Page in a book without pages, and a Page skip there says "This book has no pages."
  - **Commands:** "Next Page" (Option-Command-Right Arrow), "Previous Page" (Option-Command-Left Arrow), and "Go to Page…" (Option-Command-G; PG03 makes what it opens), in the Navigate menu, disabled for a book without pages (a `FocusedValue`).
  - **Files:** `ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift`, `ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Navigation.swift`, `ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Pages.swift` (new), `ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift` (subscriptions), `Models/KeyboardShortcut.swift`, `App/MenuCommands.swift`, `Models/ReaderPageWording.swift` (new).
  - **Tests:** new `PageNavigationTests`; existing:
    - navigation: `LegacyNavigationAlgorithmTests`, `LegacySentenceParagraphSkipTests`;
    - settings: `SettingsPolicyTests`, `ConfigSyncTests`;
    - shortcuts: `ShortcutManagerTests`, `ShortcutListingTests`, `ShortcutConfigFileTests`, `MenuKeyShortcutTests`, `KeyboardReachabilityTests`, `WindowScopeShortcutTests`, `SpeakInfoShortcutTests` (no default key on two actions), `LegacyKeyboardShortcutScenarioTests`, `LegacyMenuVisibilityScenarioTests`.
  - **Risks:** a new `SkipUnit` raw value read by an older build falls back to the global unit (`SkipUnit(rawValue:)` is nil). The cycle's order changes by one stop.
  - **Samples:** built in tests. About 300 lines plus tests.
- [x] **PG03 — Go to Page… and the page on the reader screen.** Merged 77e798a0, October 21 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 5 · The user's wish ("go to page", "an optional page number/count on the reader screen"). After PG02, by the same worker.
  - **What:**
    - the status row's "Page 12 of 300" button and `JumpToPagePanel`, as section 4.4 says, with the four-way hints;

    - the setting "Show the page on the reader screen" (`showsPageInReader`, on), with its config.json key and a Pages group in the Playback tab.

  - **Tests:** new `GoToPageTests` (validator, repeated labels, case, refusals); `JumpToTimeTests`, `SettingsPolicyTests`, `ConfigSyncTests`, `SettingsTabFitTests`, `ReaderTextAreaAccessibilityTests`; a hosted accessibility test of the button and field, which calls `skipUnlessHostedWindowsExposeAccessibility()` first; `make lint-a11y`.

- [x] **PG16 — Make PG09's launch re-read safe, then switch it on.** Merged 5f38016b, October 21 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 5 · From the October 21 Opus review of team/pages (`build/orchestrator/review-pages-report.md` that day; its findings are restated here). Until this is done, `PageReaderVersions.launchRereadIsOn` stays false, and existing books gain pages only by Redo Text Extraction.
  - **Finding 1 (high).** PG09's check compares only the chapters' reading text (`LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift:1054`), so an Edit Text change made before B12 that only made a paragraph a heading, changed a heading's level, or renamed a chapter title looks unchanged and would be read over, with no copy kept. Compare headings, levels, and chapter titles too, or the whole content.md without page markers.
  - **Finding 2 (high).** A book PG09 keeps as hand-changed gets no `editedText` (`:1061-1069`, `BookTextEditing.swift:671`), so the next cache-format bump replaces it without a copy. Record it as edited.
  - **Finding 3 (medium).** content.md is written (`MarkdownCacheService.swift:235`) before the record commits (`LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift:1003`), and quit never waits for the batch (`AppDelegate.swift:192`). So an interrupted batch leaves TextMouth's own text looking like the user's edit, which B12 then records. Commit atomically, or record what TextMouth wrote before writing it.
  - **Finding 7 (medium-low).** The page-reader stamp is set even when textutil's second run failed (`MarkdownCacheService.swift:257, 383`), so such a book never gains pages. Do not stamp then.
  - **Also:**
    - a book whose source is missing is chosen and fails at every launch; stamp it, or leave it alone as main did;
    - each book is extracted twice and a scanned PDF OCRed twice; compare with the with-pages extraction for formats whose pages add no reading text;
    - the batch has no cancel.
  - **Then** switch it on, with an Opus review of the switch-on, before the user's next install.

## Before the official 0.2.0 install (planned October 23)

The user's wish (October 23, 2026): "I want to install the new version with it as stable as you can make it so also plan any todos requiring breaking changes for my official update to 0.2.0."
- **The plan:** `PLAN-0.2.0-2026-10-23.md` (merged 737e641f). Its §1 says what 0.2.0 writes that 0.1.1 cannot read, its §3.2 ranks the open follow-ups that could lose data, places, or words, and its questions are in `humanQuestions/2026-10-23.md`.
- **Order:**
  1. the stability items ST01 to ST10 below;
  2. V01 to V04 before the install;
  3. the rehearsal of the first launch, which waits for question 3;
  4. V05 to V07 after the install.

- [x] **ST01 — The first launch's page re-read: rebase the path, and mind open readers.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 1. `pageUpgradeCheck` reads through the batch's snapshot of the path (`LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift:1125-1129`), so a Change Location during the batch stamps every remaining book `+failed`; rebase before reading, or hold Change Location until the batch ends. A reader restored at launch keeps saving places in the old numbering (`ReaderViewModel.swift:1460-1466`): have the batch pass over, or wait for, a book whose reader is open.
- [x] **ST02 — An EPUB page-break element loses all its text in the reading with pages.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 2 (`HTMLToMarkdownConverter.swift:219-224`, `isPageBreakElement` :421). Skip the element's text only when it is no more than its page label; otherwise place the marker before it and read the text, as DAISY 2.02 does (`DAISYParser.swift:785-795`). Before the install: a mis-tagged EPUB would lose real words at the first launch with no copy kept.
- [x] **ST03 — Resume after a very quick Pause restarts the chapter.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 3 (`SpeechService.swift:1939`): fall back to the offset the speech started from.
- [x] **ST04 — Replace Existing on a duplicate import throws away the old copy's state.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 4 (`LibraryViewModel+Duplicates.swift:249-261`): carry the old book's place, bookmarks, notes, settings, edit, and transcript over under its ID, or at least say in the dialog what is lost.
- [x] **ST05 — Edit Text: a page marker at the start of a line drops that line's words.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 5 (`MarkdownParser.swift:249-258`): when an edit is taken, move such a marker (or heading annotation) to the end of the line before it, or refuse the edit with a sentence naming the line.
- [x] **ST06 — Variable-bit-rate MP3s seek imprecisely.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 6 (`AudioPlaybackService.swift:850`, :974): open with precise timing, after measuring load time on long files.
- [x] **ST07 — `AsyncBridge.waitForResult` has no time limit.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 7: bound it, so a read that never returns cannot leave a reader on Loading or an import stuck.
- [x] **ST08 — Play during a package move reads the moving files.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 8 (`ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates.swift:149-159`): guard Play with `isPackageMoving`.
- [x] **ST09 — EPUB files are looked up by exact case.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 9 (`EPUBParser.swift:500, 506`; `EPUBParser+Navigation.swift:258, 311`): use the case-insensitive lookup DAISY uses (`DAISYParser.swift:186`).
- [x] **ST10 — A Word 97-2003 file with fields keeps its words joined at page breaks.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). PLAN §3.2 item 10 (`TextutilPageBreaks.swift:239-301`): count letters after field instructions as the plain text does.

    Each assertion's message says what a change forces: an older build's refusal, a re-read of the library, or a re-index. It points at this plan.

- [x] **V03 — Open a library that a later TextMouth wrote.** Merged 8aa0c690, October 23 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty M · Impact 4 · Before the install, after the stability fixes (question 2).
  - **What:**
    - library.json's rows are decoded one at a time, and so are the rows of each feed's index, which are books too (`Services/Feeds/FeedIndex.swift`).
    - A row that does not decode is kept as its JSON object, with its `id` and `tmbDirectoryName` when they read. It is written back unchanged at every save, and never shown or counted.
    - Its package is treated as owned by every walk of the packages: orphan adoption, the startup repair, Rebuild Directory, the removal sweeps, and the data-directory move, which copies it.
    - A `sortField` that does not decode reads as the default, and is written back as it was.
    - One sentence, in the Library's summary or in Storage settings, says how many books need a newer TextMouth, and one debug-log line says the same.
  - **Why:**
    - Only the build the user goes back to can use this, and 0.2.0 is the official build they would go back to.
    - Every format in the queue adds a `BookFormat` case (FMT15, FMT26, FMT33 to FMT36, FMT40, FMT42, FMT45), and today each would make 0.2.0 refuse the whole library (2.7).
  - **Older build:** no format changes; 0.1.1 is unaffected.
  - **Migration:** none.
  - **Files:**
    - `Models/Library.swift` (decoding and encoding) and `Services/Feeds/FeedIndex.swift`;
    - `Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift`;
    - `Services/FileImportService.swift` (`adoptOrphanedTMBPackages`);
    - `ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift` (the startup repair);
    - the Rebuild Directory, removal, and data-directory move code;
    - the summary's wording.
  - **Tests:**
    - a library holding a row whose format is "fb2", a row without `title`, and the sort field "pages": it loads, shows the other books, and writes the unreadable rows back equal as JSON objects;
    - no maintenance pass moves, adopts, or deletes their packages;
    - `ContentAnalysisMergeTests`, the library index classes, and `LogContentPolicyTests` for the new log tag.
  - **Risks:**
    - a walk of the packages that misses such a row's package, and adopts or deletes it: list every walk first (`grep -rn tmbDirectoryName TextMouth/Services TextMouth/ViewModels`);
    - a save that rebuilds `books` from the shown rows alone.

- [x] **V05 — Read text that a page reader wrote again, for newer pages, without the byte compare.** Merged cf7862f6, October 23 (see the merge entry above). Difficulty S · Impact 4 · After the install. It is required in the release that first bumps a line of `PageReaderVersions`.
  - **What:**
    - In the launch re-read, a book whose `pageReaderVersion` is set and notes no failure (`PageReaderVersions.notesFailure` is false) skips `pageUpgradeCheck`'s compare. Its content.md was written by a page reader that succeeded, so by a build with B12.
    - Such a book is read again, unless it holds a taken edit (already checked), or its content.md changed since its record (the check for an edit saved while TextMouth was closed decides, as now).
    - Every other book is compared as now:
      - one with no stamp, which is text from 0.1.1's time;
      - one whose stamp notes a failure ("+failed-once" or "+failed"). Such a stamp is also written when a book's file is missing or no longer extracts (`unreadableText`, `LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift:1221` to 1240), and that book's content.md is still the old text.
  - **Why:** without it, the first bump of a format's page reader copies every book of that format (2.8): about 730 `content.md.edited-` copies for EPUB.
  - **Older build:** nothing changes.
  - **Migration:** none.
  - **Files:** `ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift` (around lines 990 and 1124), and the comments in `Models/PageReaderVersions.swift`.
  - **Tests:**
    - a book stamped 1.0.0, whose format's reader becomes 1.0.1, is read again with no copy;
    - with a taken edit, it is kept;
    - with content.md changed since its record, the edit check decides;
    - an unstamped book is compared as now;
    - a book stamped "1.0.0+failed" by a missing file, whose file comes back after the reader becomes 1.0.1, is compared, and copied when it differs;
    - a "+failed-once" stamp keeps its meaning, and is compared;
    - the `pages` and `places` areas, and `PlaceRelocationAtRebuildTests`.
  - **Risks:** a stamp lost in a round trip through 0.1.1 sends the book through the compare, which is safe; keep that.
- [ ] **V06 — Escape only what Markdown needs in content.md, with cache format 3.1.0.** Difficulty M · Impact 3 · The first update after the install (question 1).
  - **What:**
    - `MarkdownLiteral` gains a minimal form. A character is escaped only where CommonMark, GitHub tables and strikethrough, or smart punctuation would otherwise read it:
      - the backslash, backtick, asterisk, underscore, square brackets, angle brackets, ampersand, pipe, tilde, and both quotes;
      - a hyphen beside another hyphen, or at a line's start;
      - a period or closing parenthesis after a number at a line's start, and a period beside another period;
      - "!" before "[";
      - "#", "+", "=", and ">" at a line's start;
      - U+FEFF, as now.
    - Every writer that uses it switches: `PlainTextParser`, `HTMLToMarkdownConverter`, `DOCXParser+Converter`, `DAISYParser+TextExtraction`, and `MarkdownEmphasis`.
    - A reading with `includesPages: false` keeps reproducing 3.0.7 exactly, full escaping included. It stays the reference for the compare below, and for the parsers' exact-output tests that read with it.
    - The cache format becomes 3.1.0, with its line in the version history.
  - **Why:** the file Edit Text opens, and anything copied from it, then reads as written, with no backslash before every comma and period.
  - **Older build:** 0.2.0 and 0.1.1 both take 3.1.0 as current (a newer stamp is never outdated), and read the same text from it.
  - **Migration:**
    - The bump reads every text book again in the startup batch, and edits are kept (B12).
    - A book that holds neither an edit record nor a page stamp was never compared by PG16: plain text, Markdown, SSML, web archives, and braille. It is compared first with the 3.0.7 reading, which PG16's reading without pages already gives, and copied when it differs. This is PG16's rule, extended to those books.
  - **Files:** `Parsers/MarkdownLiteral.swift` and its callers, `Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift`, and `ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift`.
  - **Tests:**
    - a property test that the minimal and full forms parse to the same reading text, over generated strings (every ASCII punctuation character in every position, at line starts, in runs, and beside combining marks) and over the golden corpus;
    - the exact-output tests that read with `includesPages: false` pass unchanged, which proves 3.0.7 is still reproduced; the goldens written with pages, such as parts of `TextPipelineGoldenTests`, are regenerated (the brief lists them);
    - the heading-annotation classes, the reading-text cache tests, and `BookTextEditTests`.
  - **Risks:**
    - a context the escaper misses would reach every book at once. So each book's content.md is written in the minimal form only after it parses to the same reading text as the full form; otherwise that book keeps the full form, and the debug log names it.
    - A batch stopped between writing a book's content.md and committing its record leaves TextMouth's own text where the next launch looks for an edit saved while it was closed. PG16 handles this for the page re-read (`writtenWithPages`); the version-bump path must compare with the new reading before it takes such a text as an edit.
    - Transcripts' content.md is the transcripts team's: their places count time, so it can change on its own.
- [x] **V07 — A change to how one format is read re-reads only that format's books.** Closed by the October 30 triage: recorded as a rule in `Models/PageReaderVersions.swift` (a case and a version per format) and applied by ce7f9864 and b7333a73. Difficulty S (a rule, and PG14's design) · Impact 3 · Record it now, and apply it in each item as that item is built.
  - **What:**
    - A later reader change adds or bumps that format's line in the table of reader versions (`PageReaderVersions`, generalized; its JSON key can stay `pageReaderVersion`). It never bumps the global cache format or content-index version. This covers PG12, PG13, PG14, FMT16, FMT17, FMT27, and FMT47.
    - PG14 keeps `BookContentIndex` 1.0.0. Its pages become an optional field of the audio chapters, and DAISY audiobooks get a stamp of their own.
    - FMT17 changes the chapters of audiobooks already in the library, which moves places. It applies to new imports only, unless it moves places with the chapters.
  - **Why:** a global bump reads or indexes the whole library again for one format's books (2.3).
  - **Older build:** 0.2.0 ignores an optional field, and a stamp line it does not know.
  - **Migration:** per format, as each item says.
  - **Files:** `Models/PageReaderVersions.swift`, `Models/BookContentIndex.swift` (PG14), and each item's own.
  - **Tests:** each item's own, with V02's pins changed on purpose.
  - **Risks:** DAISY text books and DAISY audiobooks share the `daisy` format, so their stamps must tell them apart.
